## 2. What exists now

### 2.1 Pinned / busted / backup / buy-back

Rules (the spec the brother wrote down):

- An officer that **touches** a free player **pins** them (held down on the ground). Touch = within `GuardAI.catchRadius` (1.6 m) while chasing.
- A pinned player has **12 s** until they are **busted** (arrested).
- If the pinning officer is **hit by another player** (melee or a thrown item), the pinned player is freed.
- When **nobody is free** (everyone pinned or busted) the whole team is busted: run over (`HeistPhase.Failed`). With one player, a pin is an instant bust, same as before.
- After a guard calls backup, **another officer is called every 30 s** until the floor ends.
- Busted players can be **bought back from the vendor** once the team is at the Loading Docks. The vendor says: *"One of your friends got got. You're lucky I have someone on the inside"*.

Where it lives:

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `Scripts/CustodyRules.cs` (new) | Pure C#, no Unity types: `CustodyState`, all numbers (12 s, 30 s, 3 s immunity, buy-back price `300 + 50 x level`), `BackupClock`, the vendor lines. Unit tested. |
| `Scripts/WobblyPlayer.cs` | `Custody`, `PinLeft`, `BustedOnLevel`, `PinnedBy`, `PinImmuneUntil`; `ApplyCustody(...)` (every machine); a player in custody cannot walk, jump, crouch, hide; body is tipped over and held down (`HoldDown`). |
| `Scripts/HeistManager.cs` | Host/offline logic: `Pin`, `FreePinned`, `BustPlayer` (timer in `TickCustody`), `ReleaseBusted`, `CheckTeamDown`, `CallBackup` + `TickBackup` (repeat clock), `CountCustody`. Banner texts via `Announce`. |
| `Scripts/GuardAI.cs` | New state `GuardState.Pinning` (appended last, so network snapshots stay compatible). Chase ends in `HeistManager.Pin`; `BeginHold` / `UpdatePinning` / `ReleaseHold`. Any `Stun` or `WeaponHitHost` on a holding officer frees the player. Guards ignore players who are not free (`CanSee`). |
| `Scripts/Net/NetPlayer.cs` | Three host-written NetworkVariables (`custody`, `pinSecondsLeft`, `bustedLevel`) applied on every machine. |
| `Scripts/Net/RunSync.State.cs` | Replicates `BackupIn` (seconds to the next officer). |
| `Scripts/Net/RunSync.Requests.cs` | `RequestBuyBack(slot)` client -> host; host rejects pinned/busted callers at the elevator panel. |
| `Scripts/Vendor.cs` | Shop window lists busted teammates with a **Buy back** button; `HostBuyBack` / `BuyBackFor` check cash, counter and target. Pinned/busted players cannot shop. |
| `Scripts/VendorChat.cs` | Speech bubble: greeting `"Glad you made it. Make it fast and make it a good deal."`, or the buy-back line while a friend is busted. Lines also set in `FloorGenerator.Dock.cs`. |
| `Scripts/CustodyHud.cs` (new) | Self-installing IMGUI: big PINNED countdown / BUSTED for you, labels over downed teammates, "BACKUP: another officer in N s". |
| `Scripts/HeistHud.cs` | Top line now shows `Free / Pinned / Busted` instead of the old, unused `Caught x/3`. |
| `Scripts/ElevatorExtraction.cs`, `WobblyPlayerRegistry.cs` | "Everyone aboard" now ignores busted players (`WobblyPlayerRegistry.AllAboard`, uses a set instead of a counter). |
| `Backpack`, `PlayerGrab`, `MeleeController`, `HideSpot`, `PlayerVisual` | Small gates: no inventory/grab/swing/hide while in custody; lying pose. |
| `TestMode.cs`, `Editor/NetDev/NetDevTest.cs` | Cheat buttons and `CustodyTest()`. |

Design decisions worth knowing (change them if the team disagrees):

- **Busted bodies stay in the world**, lying down, and ride along in the elevator car (the floor loader puts every player in the car). They are invisible to guards and ignored by the "everyone aboard" check.
- A busted player's **backpack is left alone** (not dropped). Carried items in the hands are dropped.
- Buy-back price is `300 + 50 x (level they were busted on)`: $350 on floor 1, $900 on floor 12. Tune in `CustodyRules.BuyBackPrice`. Only available at the docks (that is the only floor with a vendor).
- Only a **chasing** officer pins (same rule the old instant-bust used). Walking into an idle patrol guard does not.
- A freed or bought-back player is immune to pinning for 3 s.
- The first backup call still sends 2 officers (existing behaviour); every repeat sends 1. `HeistManager.BackupCalled` is now set on the first call, so the end screen reads "BACKUP CALLED - BUSTED".
- The pinning officer just stands facing the player; there is no kneeling animation yet.
- Pins and the repeat clock are cleared when a new floor starts; busted players stay busted.


### 2.2 Music (`Scripts/Audio/`)

- `MusicDirector` (self-installing, survives scene loads) decides the mood on every machine from state that is already shared; nothing is sent over the network.
  - **Intense**: from the moment backup is called (`HeistManager.BackupCalled/BackupActive`) until the floor is over (elevator seals, run ends, next floor loads).
  - **Elevator** (muzak): while the local player stands in a car, and for 5 s of the ride.
  - Otherwise silence. The docks have no intense music (no guards, no backup).
- **A different intense track on every floor**: `MusicPlan.IndexFor(runSeed, floor)` is a per-run shuffle of the 16 tracks, so nothing repeats within 16 floors, two floors in a row never share a track, and every player hears the same one (the seed is `TowerManager.baseSeed`).
- The tracks are generated by code: `MusicPlan.Tracks` is the "score" (tempo, key, chords, drums, bass, lead per track, edit a line to change one); `ProceduralMusic` renders it on a worker thread when the floor loads, so it is ready long before anybody calls backup. `LoungeMusic` makes the elevator track.
- **Real music**: put clips in `Resources/AudioCatalog.asset` (`intenseMusic`, `elevatorMusic`). They replace the generated ones with no other change (the same shuffle picks the clip per floor).
- Music volume slider in the Esc menu (`MusicDirector.Volume`, saved in PlayerPrefs).
- Verified only by numbers (length, peak, loudness, loop seam, determinism, tracks differ in tone). **Nobody has listened to it.** Expect to tune the mix and the tracks' taste.

### 2.3 Door, elevator and officer sounds

- `SoundId` (`SoundBank.cs`): DoorOpen, DoorClose, ElevatorDing, Keys, KeysRun, RadioStatic, RadioCall. A clip in the AudioCatalog wins (`doorOpen`, `doorClose`, `elevatorDing`, `keys`, `keysRun`, `radioStatic`, `radioCall`); otherwise a placeholder is generated by `ProceduralSound` (a few variants each, made one per frame at startup).
- `GameAudio.PlaySound(SoundId, ...)` plays on a fixed pool of 32 AudioSources (no objects created while playing).
- `GuardSounds` (added to every guard by `GuardVisual`): key jingle every ~7 m walked (about every 3 s walking) / ~6 m running (about every 1.3 s; `walkStride` / `runStride`, each jingle +-25% so it is never a metronome; it was every 2.4 m / 2 m, which was too busy), walkie-talkie static every 7-24 s (first one after 4-18 s), a squelch when he radios for backup. All local to each machine (each guard's rhythm is seeded from his NetId); nothing is networked.

### 2.4 Sliding doors (`Scripts/Doors/`, `FloorGenerator.Doors.cs`)

- A double sliding door in every doorway between rooms (the beam over each doorway marks it). Built **after** the NavMesh bake so guard routes never depend on a door. All doors start shut.
- **Players work them with E, aimed with the crosshair** (section 2.9): point the crosshair at a door (it need not be shut: the doorway counts, and so does the beam over it) while within 2.2 m of the doorway (`DoorManager.UseReach`) and the prompt "[E] Open door" shows; E opens it and it **stays open** until a player presses E again ("[E] Close door"). Walking up to a door no longer opens it, and standing near one without aiming at it does nothing. What E does is decided by what the crosshair is on (item / door / dealer, see `AimMath.Choose`); nothing happens while a menu/shop/panel is open, while hiding or in custody. `DoorManager.Nearest` / `DoorGeometry.UseScore` are the old proximity pick; `UseScore` is still used for the reach test (the host's check too), `Nearest` is no longer called.
- Closing never shuts on something: if a person or a dropped crate is actually **in the doorway** (inside the wall's thickness, `SlidingDoor` clear box = wall depth + 0.03 m) the door stays open (retries every 0.25 s), the prompt says "Something is in the doorway", and it closes by itself once it is clear. Somebody standing beside the door, even right up against it, does not hold it open: E closes it at once. (An earlier version checked 0.35 m each side and made players step away before the door would close.) A player who asks for "close" while standing in the doorway is not squashed; the door closes when he steps out.
- **Officers open doors for any walk through one, and only then** (`GuardAI.GetRoute`, `SlidingDoor.AnyoneWalkingIn`, `DoorGeometry.WalkEnters` / `RouteEnters`). History: be40 made officers open doors only while chasing (the user did not want doors opening for a guard just walking past); be41 added backup officers (they run at the player in the `Investigate` state); be42 (this rule) replaced "which state" by "where is he walking", because the user then reported that officers who go to look in a room walk through the door without opening it. Now an officer in any state that walks about by himself (`GuardAI.UsesDoors`: Patrol, Investigate, Chase; not Calling, Pinning, Stunned, dead) opens a door when **his route enters the doorway's zone within the next 0.9 s of walking** (`SlidingDoor.LookAheadSeconds`; the zone = the gap + 0.25 m each way along the wall, and 0.5 m in front of and behind the wall's middle: `WalkZone`). A guard cannot walk closer than 0.6 m to a wall (agent radius 0.5 + half the 0.2 m wall), so one who walks along a wall or a corridor past a doorway never gets into the zone and opens nothing; one who walks into the doorway does, in time. `DoorManager` collects one `Walker` per officer ten times a second (`GatherWalkers`: his position, then the corners of his NavMesh path, plus his speed) and every door asks whether any of them enters its zone; `GuardNear` is that answer, the door opens while it is true and closes 1.1 s after (never on a body: the physics check of `DoorLogic`). **Host**: the route is the agent's real path (`agent.path.GetCornersNonAlloc`, trimmed to the leg he is on, so a guard who is about to turn into a door is seen early) and the speed is `agent.speed`, so a door opens the moment he sets off; `agent.path` allocates a small object, the doors ask only about guards that are moving, ten times a second. **Remote client** (puppet, no agent): a straight line ahead of the way the puppet faces at its streamed speed (at least 1.5 m/s), so a puppet that turns into a door at the last moment can clip it for a moment. No network traffic: every machine decides for itself. People standing at a door when a floor starts have it snapped open (no sound; players by the old, tighter sensor `SlidingDoor.AnyoneAtStart`, officers only if they stand in the doorway itself `AnyoneInside`); it shuts again when they leave. A throw-away simulation (not in the repo) of an officer setting off from rest or already walking at 1.5-4.6 m/s towards a doorway (straight, or bending at the mouth of the NavMesh opening; narrow and wide door; every scan phase) found the gap at least 1.0 m wide when his body touched the leaves in every case but 20 of 7404 for a guard already running at 4.6 m/s that appears 1.5 m from a shut door, and (puppets) 36 of 8340 sharp bends into the wide door. The generator's speeds are walk 1.5, investigate 2.2, chase 3.6 m/s.
- **Guard-and-door QA scenario** (`NetDevDoors.GuardDoorQa`, part of `NetDevHarness.Doors`; its lines start with `guards:`): finds a doorway with a straight walkable route through it and a route that runs along a wall 1.6-2.4 m from another doorway, blinds one real officer (`sightRange` 0.01, `catchRadius` 0.1) and walks him (a) **through** the doorway four ways: on patrol, going to look at a noise (`NoiseBus.Emit`), sent as backup (`Dispatch`) and chasing (the private `state` / `target` / `unseenFor` fields are set by reflection) - he must get through, the door must be at least 1.2 m open when his body reaches it, and it must be shut again 2.7 s later; (b) **past** the other doorway the same four ways - the door must never open at all; then (c) the **chase length** with the player teleported 8 m in front of him each frame (sight blockers off, range 40): he is still chasing after 7 s, still chasing 3.5 s after the player leaves his cone (range 0.01), a 3-frame glimpse resets the clock, and then he gives up between 4.7 and 5.6 s later (state `Investigate`). If the reflected fields are renamed the scenario logs `SKIP guard QA`.
- Classes: `DoorManager` (one per floor, `DoorManager.Active`) handles the key, the prompt and the officers' scan and moves only the doors in motion; `SlidingDoor` is a plain class, not a component (`Latched` = a player opened it, `GuardNear` = an officer is at it); `DoorLogic` is the pure state machine (`Step`, `CloseNow`; tested outside Unity). The doorway only has a collider while the door is completely shut. At most 3 door sounds per frame.
- **Networked**: a door is addressed by its index in `DoorManager.Doors` (floors are built identically on every machine). The player's machine sends `RunSync.RequestDoor(index, open)` (an explicit wish, not a "flip", so two presses at once cannot cancel); the host checks the player is near the door and not in custody and announces `DoorSetRpc(index, open, epoch)` to everybody including itself; every machine then sets the door. A client that finishes building the floor late asks the host (`RequestDoorStates`) for the list of doors players have opened and snaps them open. Requests/events carry `RunSync.Epoch` so ones for the previous floor are ignored. Offline the door is set directly. The officers' automatic opening stays local on each machine.
- Not done: the player's own machine waits for the host's answer before the door moves (a round trip; no optimistic opening). `DoorManager.UseKey` is fixed to E; it does not follow `Backpack.pickupKey`. `FloorGenerator.slidingDoors` switches all doors off.

### 2.5 Guard speech bubbles

- `GuardBubble` (every guard) keeps the timing, `GuardBubbleHud` draws it, `SpeechBubble` is the shared bubble drawing (the vendor uses it too), `SymbolSpeech` makes the text: 3-6 symbols from `#@$%&*!?~^+=/|`, never letters or digits, so never a real word.
- A bubble pops up every 6-20 s at random, and when a guard is knocked down. Only drawn for guards within 22 m, on screen and in clear view (never through a wall or a shut door). Local to each screen.

### 2.6 Room numbers

- Every numbered room (kind 0 with a doorway: offices, meeting rooms, break rooms, storage, server and copy rooms) has a plate over its doorway on the corridor side: floor 1 = 101, 102, ... 199; floor 2 = 201 ...; floor 12 = 1201 ... A room with two doors has the same number over both. **The ORDER of the numbers follows the corridors** (changed 2026-10-16 evening; the plates themselves are as before, the owner likes them): 101 is the first room you meet leaving the elevator and the numbers keep rising as you walk away from it; at a fork the branch on the left (as you walk away from the elevator) is numbered first, then straight on, then right (at the elevator itself: the way its door faces, then the other); inside a cell the rooms go along the corridor and the two sides pair up (the left side first); a cell's rooms are never split between two rounds, and the letter round (101A ...) only starts where a branch starts, so a corridor is never cut in two by it (the letter rooms are a whole wing, the "annex"). Before, the numbers went column by column from the west with every column starting again at the south, so neighbouring cells could differ by 20 and the count jumped across the whole building, and a number went DOWN while walking away from the elevator in 14% of the cell pairs on the audited floors; now never (0 of 928). A suggestion for the How to Play text (the owner writes the words): "Room numbers start at the elevator and go up as you walk away from it. At a fork the left-hand way comes first. A letter after the number (101A) is the annex: the last wing."
- A floor has about 80-130 numbered rooms, so after 99 the numbers go round again with a letter (101A, 102A, ...), like an annex. Corridors, lobbies, open-plan zones, the docks and the boss floor have no plates.
- `Interior/RoomNumbers.cs` (pure: `Of` = the order and the labels, `Plates`, 3x5 dot font; it reads the maze from `Layout.input`, the `PlanInput` the planner was given) and `FloorGenerator.RoomNumbers.cs` (builds **one mesh for the whole floor** with two materials, so 2 draw calls; freed by `OwnedMesh`). `FloorGenerator.roomNumbers` switches them off. Tune size with `DotSize` / `PlateMargin`.
- Never seen on screen: check the plates are readable from the camera's usual distance, face the corridor (not the wall), and are not clipped by the door header.

The corridor **direction signs** that point at blocks of these numbers are section 2.12 (they reuse the same numbering: `RoomNumbers.Label`, `IsNumbered`, the `layout.rooms` order).

### 2.7 After hours: ceilings and lights (night look)

The heist is at night in an empty office, so every floor (docks included) has a ceiling, ceiling light panels and a dim night look, and only some areas have their lights on. **Never seen in Unity: expect to tune the numbers below.**

- **Ceiling**: one box tile per 18 m cell (`Ceiling_c_r`, 0.3 m thick, flush with the top of the 3.5 m walls, outer tiles reach over the outer walls), with colliders (the over-the-shoulder camera is sphere-cast against non-rigidbody colliders, so it cannot rise through it). Built **after** the NavMesh bake and after `BuildDoors()` in `FloorGenerator.Generate` (otherwise the ceiling top would be baked as a walkable island); on the docks (no bake) right after `BuildLoadingDock`.
- **Panels** (`Interior/AfterHours.cs`, pure and tested outside Unity): fluorescent 1.2 x 0.6 m panels in a grid per room, corridor, lobby and open zone, 0.3 m clear of the walls, never over an elevator shaft, one inside each elevator car (always lit). Each room is lit or dark by a seeded roll (`AfterHours.LitChance` per theme; the lobby by the elevator and the cars are always lit; every 18 m cell and every ~18 m block is guaranteed some light; about 40 % of all panels are on). The docks and the boss floor are fully lit (`allLit`). Same seed = same lit rooms on every machine, and nothing is networked (purely cosmetic, no `ProtocolVersion` change).
- **Look**: lit panels have an emissive material (warm or cool white, `panelWarmMat/panelCoolMat`, `RealtimeEmissive`); dark panels are grey. One mesh per cell with four submeshes (frame / dark lens / warm lit lens / cool lit lens), freed by `OwnedMesh`; materials are created at runtime and destroyed in `FloorGenerator.OnDestroy` (`DestroyNightMaterials`).
- **Real lights** (`FloorLights`, on the `AfterHours` root): one spot light (125 degrees, pointing down, no shadows, `LightRenderMode.ForcePixel`) per ~4.6 m of lit area, about 100-300 per floor, **all disabled except the ~8 nearest the camera** (within 30 m, rescanned 4 times a second, 0.4 s fade, hysteresis so they do not flicker). Max count = `min(maxActiveLights, 4 + QualitySettings.pixelLightCount * 2)`. Without shadows, light leaks through partition walls onto the floor of the next room; accepted (partition walls have shadow casting off and real-time shadows were too costly). `GuardVision`'s flashlight beam is now `ForcePixel` too, so it works next to the ceiling lights.
- **Night**: `ApplyNightLook` sets flat ambient (`nightAmbient` 0.25/0.27/0.34 on office floors, `dockAmbient` a bit brighter in the basement so the shop is readable), the scene's directional light turned into faint moonlight (`moonIntensity` 0.2, `moonColour`), reflection intensity 0.3 and a darker clone of the skybox (visible only through windows / the sky outside, if any). The scene file itself is not changed.
- **Knobs** (inspector of `FloorGenerator`, header "After hours"): `afterHours` (off = the old open-topped, daylight floors), `nightAmbient`, `dockAmbient`, `moonIntensity`, `moonColour`, `litScale` (0.3-2: share of lit rooms; 1 = ~40 % of panels), `lampBrightness`, `maxActiveLights`. If the dark areas are too dark: raise `nightAmbient` first (the asked-for look is "not too dark overall"). If a lit room looks dim: raise `lampBrightness` (Unity's attenuation is steep, the numbers in `AfterHours.Build` are range 9 / intensity 2.6 for rooms, 10.5 / 2.4 open zones, 7.5 / 2.2 cars). Per-theme lit chances are in `AfterHours.LitChance`.
- **Risks / to check in Unity**: (1) the emission keyword `_EMISSION` on materials made at runtime can be stripped in a player build when no asset uses it (ElevatorPanel and GuardVision rely on the same thing); if lit panels look flat grey in a build, put a material with emission on in a scene or add the Standard shader to Always Included Shaders / use a custom unlit shader; (2) brightness of the ambient and the moon (gamma colour space, `Standard` shader); (3) frame time with 8 forced-pixel lights over 18 m ceiling and floor boxes (lower `maxActiveLights` first, then split the ceiling tiles); (4) Quality levels other than Ultra have `pixelLightCount` of 1-2, so fewer lights are on; (5) the camera clipping into the ceiling when the player stands under a wall (the sphere cast should stop it); (6) NavMesh: nothing from the ceiling should end up in it, since it is built after the bake - open the NavMesh visualisation to confirm.
- Tests: `NetDevTest.LightingTest` (part of `PolicyTest`: deterministic, panels inside rooms and clear of shafts, no overlaps, lobby and cars lit, light in every cell, 30-80 % on, mesh winding) and the "night:" lines of `NetDevHarness.Doors` (ceiling tile per cell with colliders, real lights present, no more than the budget on, dim flat ambient, nothing above the walls but the ceiling).

### 2.8 The elevator interior and its mirror

The service elevator (the 8 x 8 m room in the corner cell; interior 7.6 x 7.6 m) used to be a plain box. It is now dressed like a passenger elevator. **Never seen in Unity** (there is a rough preview made by a test renderer, not by Unity).

- **Look** (planner `Interior/ElevatorLook.cs`, pure, tested outside Unity; mesh by `CarMesh`): wood-veneer wall panels (about 1.25 m wide, dark seams between), cream lower wall, stainless kick plate, chair rail band + handrail all round (the rail stops before the floor-button panel and the GO button), stainless jambs at each doorway (plus a frame on the outside of the front door), a stainless sill, burgundy carpet, a wood ceiling in three strips with nine round downlights (steel ring + glowing lens). The elevator doors themselves are now stainless steel (`CarSteel`). Everything is axis-aligned boxes in car coordinates, trim stands at most 11 cm out of the walls, and **nothing in the interior has a collider** (the camera and players are not affected).
- **Mirror**: the wall opposite the floor-button panel (south wall if the front door is east, west wall if it is north; never a wall with a door) is a mirror, 7.2 x 3.2 m, with a steel frame and the handrail mounted on it. `PlanarMirror` (on the `CarMirror` quad) is the classic planar reflection: when a game camera is about to draw the mirror it renders a second, smaller picture (`quality` x the screen size, default 0.6) with a hidden camera that is reflected in the mirror's plane and clipped at the glass (oblique near plane, `GL.invertCulling`), and the shader `BuildingEscape/ElevatorMirror` (**in `Assets/Resources/Shaders`, so a build always contains it**) shows that picture in screen space. Cost: a second draw of the scene, only while the camera is in front of the glass and either inside the car (`insideBounds`) or has a clear line to it through an open door (rays ignore rigidbodies; wall and shut-door colliders block), and within 16 m. If the shader is missing the mirror falls back to a shiny metal material and a warning is logged.
- **Light**: the planner's ceiling panel in the car is hidden inside the wood ceiling; instead four **point lights** in the car's ceiling (`FloorGenerator.carLampBrightness`, range 8, intensity 2) are added to the `FloorLights` pool (spot lights could not reach the upper walls). The wood ceiling has a slight emission (it gets almost no light from below). Without `afterHours` the car only has the scene's own light.
- **Wiring**: `FloorGenerator.BuildElevatorRoom` calls `BuildElevatorInterior` (only in Play Mode, `elevatorLook`; the editor tools that build floors keep the old box) and builds the doors from the steel material; `ElevatorLook.PanelAlong` is the one place that says where the floor panel hangs (it was a literal 2.9 in two places); `FloorGenerator.OnDestroy` frees the materials and the generated wood texture (`DestroyCarMaterials`); the meshes are freed by `OwnedMesh`; `PlanarMirror.OnDestroy` frees its render texture and camera.
- **Knobs** (inspector of `FloorGenerator`, header "Elevator interior"): `elevatorLook` (off = the old box), `elevatorMirror`, `mirrorQuality` (lower = faster, blurrier), `carLampBrightness`. Colours are in `CarMaterial`; the wood texture is generated by `ProceduralWood` (seed 11, tiles in both directions).
- **To check in Unity**: (1) the mirror shows a correct, not flipped or offset, reflection of the player and the room (the screen-space lookup in the shader is the classic one, but it has never run); (2) no pink: if the mirror or the wood is pink, look at the console for the shader message; (3) the car is bright enough and the wood looks orange, not dark brown (raise `carLampBrightness`); (4) frame rate with the mirror in view (lower `mirrorQuality`); (5) the mirror does not draw when you stand in the corridor outside the shut doors; (6) players, guards and loot in the car show in the mirror; (7) nothing z-fights where the rail meets the floor panel; (8) floor 12: the back door in the car's far wall is framed in steel, the mirror is on the side wall; (9) in a build, the `ElevatorMirror` shader is there (Resources).
- Not done: the guest elevator (where backup arrives) and the floor-12 hidden elevator keep the old plain look; a second mirror or a mirror on the back wall.
- Tests: `NetDevTest.ElevatorLookTest` (part of `PolicyTest`) and the "car:" lines of `NetDevHarness.Doors`.

### 2.9 The crosshair: E works what you point at

The player now has a crosshair, and the crosshair decides what E (and Q, and a melee swing) act on. Before, E took "whatever is nearest and in front"; now you point at it. **Never seen or played in Unity** (compiled against stubs; the arithmetic is tested outside Unity).

- **The reticle** (`Interaction/Crosshair.cs`, IMGUI on `Repaint`): a dot with four ticks and a dark shadow at the middle of the screen, scaled with the screen height. White normally; **yellow** when E would work what it is on (an item, a door or the dealer within reach, the ticks close in); **red** when a melee weapon is equipped and a guard or training dummy within 3.2 m is under it; **grey** when it is on something usable that is out of reach. Hidden while the cursor is free or any menu / shop / elevator panel / inventory is open. Knobs: `Crosshair.size`, `Crosshair.show`, and the `PlayerPrefs` key `OvertimeEscape.Crosshair` (0 = hidden; there is no settings-page toggle yet). It installs itself with `[RuntimeInitializeOnLoadMethod(AfterSceneLoad)]` (a `DontDestroyOnLoad` object), so no scene needs changing.
- **The target** (`Crosshair.Aim`, static, worked out once a frame the first time anyone asks, from the camera as last drawn): `AimTarget { Kind (None/Pickup/Door/Dealer), Pickup, Door (index in DoorManager.Doors), Foe, TooFar, Distance }`. The ray goes from the camera through the middle of the screen but **starts at the player's own depth minus 0.25 m** (`AimMath.StartT`), so a wall, desk or the camera collision between the camera and the player never counts, and runs to the player's reach + 1.2 m past his depth. Then (`Crosshair.Resolve`, the testable entry): a thin physics ray finds the first thing in the way (walls, desks, guards; **not** the player himself, teammates or items); items near the player are tested as their collider box **grown 6 cm** (`AimMath.RayItem`, `Pickup.TryGetBounds`) so a mug on a desk is easy to hit, they must be within the backpack's `reach` (2.2 m, measured from the player to the item's nearest collider point) and in front of the first wall (15 cm slack because items rest on surfaces); doors are tested as their doorway box (`SlidingDoor.RayHit`, grown 10 cm sideways, plus 0.7 m above for the beam), within `DoorManager.UseReach`, and a wall in front hides them (25 cm slack for the shut door's own collider); the dealer (`Vendor.Nearby`, only while `CanTalk`) is an upright cylinder of 0.7 m radius around `Vendor.npc` and is **not** hidden by anything (he stands behind a counter). `AimMath.Choose` then picks: the nearest wins, an item beats the dealer beats a door on a tie, and **an item in or in front of the doorway beats the door** (so a crate that keeps a door open can be picked up).
- **Users**: `Backpack` (E picks up the aimed item; if nothing is aimed at it says "Nothing there - aim the crosshair at an item" or "Too far away - get closer"; nothing is said when the crosshair is on a door or the dealer), `DoorManager.HandleUse` (E on the aimed door; the prompt is drawn every frame now, no more 10 Hz scan), `Vendor` (E talks to the dealer only when aimed at him, or to close the open shop; near him without aiming the hint "Aim the crosshair at the dealer to talk" shows), `PlayerGrab.TryGrab` (Q carries the aimed item first, then the old nearest / sphere cast), `MeleeController.Hit` (the hit zone, 1.2 m in front of the player, is centred towards the crosshair: `Crosshair.SwingForward` = the horizontal direction to the point of the aim ray 2.4 m past the player's depth; the facing direction while free-looking or for anyone but the followed player). The old `FindNearest` is only left as Q's fallback; the inventory's "On the ground nearby" list is still proximity (`ScanNear`).
- **Unchanged**: F (sell / elevator panel), H (hide), G, Tab, LMB throw, the networking (the old requests; the host's checks are still the generous ones: 4.5 m for a store, door reach + 1.3 m). Because the target is local, nothing is sent over the network for it and `NetConst.ProtocolVersion` stays "3".
- **Known limits**: with the camera at its steepest (60 degrees down) the crosshair meets the floor about 0.8 m in front of the player, so an item right at his feet or behind him cannot be aimed at: step back or look around (free-look works). The item box is the axis-aligned box of its colliders, so a long item lying at an angle (the crowbar) has a slightly generous corner. The dealer can be targeted through walls inside his talk range. `Pickup`s are transparent to each other only when out of reach.
- **To check in Unity**: (1) the reticle is in the middle, readable on dark and light floors, turns yellow on a nearby mug / crate / door, grey on a door farther than ~2 m; (2) E on an item picks it up only when the crosshair is on it, and not the one next to it; (3) E on a door opens/closes it from the right distance, also while looking at the beam above it, and a door behind a wall cannot be worked; (4) with a crate in an open doorway E picks the crate up, then works the door; (5) the dealer: the crosshair on him turns yellow and E opens the shop, aiming elsewhere in the trailer shows the hint and E does nothing; (6) swing a bat at a guard / the dock's punching bag with the crosshair on it (red) and off to one side; (7) Q on a crate you point at; (8) menus hide the reticle and E in the shop closes it; (9) host + client: same checks on the client (store and door requests are the old ones).
- Tests: `NetDevTest.AimMathTest` (in `PolicyTest`), the "aim:" lines at the end of `NetDevHarness.Doors`, the "dock:" crosshair lines in `NetDevSneak`; the arithmetic was also run with a .NET console test (47 checks).

### 2.10 How a guard sees and how long a chase lasts

Added in be42 at the user's request ("after a guard gets triggered they should continue to chase the person for as long as you are in the cone of vision; if the player leaves the cone for more than 5 seconds the guards stop"). Never played in Unity.

- **The cone** is the fan `GuardVision` draws on the floor: flat, `GuardAI.sightAngle` (65 degrees) each side of the way he faces, `sightRange` (14 m; `hiddenSightRange` 1.5 m for a hiding player) long, cut by anything in the line of sight (`CanSee`'s ray from his eye to the player's chest; the shut door's collider blocks it). `GuardRules.InCone` is the flat test (it used to be the 3D angle from his eye to the chest, which made a player standing right next to him vanish at the edge of the fan); somebody within 0.4 m always counts.
- **The chase** (`GuardAI.UpdateChase`): as long as the target is in the cone, or another free player is (he then switches to that one), the out-of-view clock is zero and he keeps after him, however long that is. Out of the cone **5 s in a row** (`GuardRules.LoseSightSeconds`, `GuardAI.loseSightSeconds`; was 4 s; a glimpse restarts it, `GuardRules.PursuitOver`) and he gives up (`LoseChase`): he walks to where he last saw the player (`Investigate`, at the generator's `guardInvestigateSpeed`, 2.2 m/s), waits 3 s and goes back on patrol; if the player is in his cone again he spots him again (the 2 s "calling backup" pause and the "!" ring come first, as before). During the 5 s he still runs at the player's real position (as before).
- **Not changed**: the first spot is still a 2 s look (`callSeconds`) in which leaving the cone for more than 0.3 s (`loseSightGrace`) drops the call without backup - the "you have 2 seconds to get out of view" rule; a pinned/arrested target ends the chase at once unless another free player is in view.
- Tests: `GuardRules` is pure (`NetDevTest.DoorOpenersTest` runs the checks; the same 47 ran in a .NET console); the chase length is part of `NetDevDoors.GuardDoorQa`.

### 2.11 Guard outposts, the lock hack, notes and floor safes

**UPDATE 2026-10-16 (task 4, section 2.15): the backup no longer arrives in the outpost.** The elevator alcove in the back-west corner that is described below is **gone**: the outpost now has lockers along its whole back wall, sits in the farthest cell that is not the guards' own elevator's (that elevator is in the corner opposite the players', and all backup arrives in it), and holds one guard on duty who sees and hears nothing outside the room. Read the sentences below about the alcove, "the red room" and "the guest elevator in the farthest cell" as history.

Added in be43 at the user's request (the red room that is on every floor becomes a guard outpost; a keypad hack to get in; loot that scales with the floor; notes that lead to safes full of cash). **Never played in Unity** - compiled against stubs only (0 errors in the runtime and editor assemblies); the rules and the planner are tested outside Unity (below). Read this section before changing anything in `Scripts/Outpost/`.

**What the player sees**

- On every office floor (not the boss floor) the farthest room of the floor is a **guard outpost** (9.0 x 6.6 m inside, dark slate walls, dark red carpet). Its front wall faces the corridor the cell opens onto: a **long one-way window** (a mirror from the corridor side, an ordinary opening seen from inside) with the **steel door** beside it (a double sliding door like the others, dark steel; each leaf has a tall wired-glass pane over a shorter one, after the reference picture), a red-lit keypad on the wall to the right of the door and a red alarm lamp over it. Inside: three monitoring desks with monitors under the window, a briefing table with a chair and a whiteboard along the east wall, four lockers on the back wall and one on the west wall, wall TVs, a clock, a bin - and in the back-west corner the **small red elevator the backup officers arrive in** (the old red room, kept as an alcove: its door opens into the room, the officers walk out through the room and the front door).
- **Getting in**: aim the crosshair at the keypad (or the door) and press **E**. A panel comes up ("SHORT-CIRCUIT THE LOCK"): after a 0.6 s "hacking" wait a key (**W, A, S or D**) shows in the middle of the screen and you have **1 second** to press it (`HackSession.Window`, plus a 50 ms grace so one slow frame cannot lose it); then the next, **four in all, all different** (each of W, A, S, D once, in random order). Four in time: "ACCESS GRANTED", the lock turns green and the door slides open and stays open (afterwards it is an ordinary E door). A wrong key or a missed second: the lock shorts out - **alarm for 14 s** (`OutpostController.AlarmSeconds`): siren, flashing lamp and keypad, the lock is jammed meanwhile, and **backup is called exactly as when a guard spots a player** (`HeistManager.CallBackupAt`: two officers now, one more every 30 s until the floor ends, the intense music starts). After the alarm stops the hack can be tried again. Esc, walking more than 4.2 m from the keypad, the menu, being grabbed or somebody else's alarm back out **with no alarm**. While the panel is up the player stands still (the keys must not walk him away from the keypad) and the cursor stays locked: the hack is all keyboard (`OutpostUi`).
- **Inside**: the contents are random per run and scale with the floor (`OutpostLoot.Roll(intelSeed, floor)`): a **note** about a floor safe (if this outpost holds one), 0-2 **weapons** (crowbar / bat from floor 1, hatchets from floor 4; chance 40% on floor 1 up to 75% on floor 12) and 0-3 **valuables** (a cash envelope, a gold watch or casino chips, each worth about 3.5% of the floor's quota per unit) lying on the briefing table. Weapons are ordinary `MeleeWeapon` pickups; valuables are `Pickup`s with `bankValue` (carry them to the elevator and bank them like loot).
- **Notes and safes**: about **one floor in five has a safe** (1-12; never more than one per floor; `SafeRules.HasSafe`). The safe is a small steel safe against the wall of one of the numbered rooms. An outpost's note names the room of one safe - on this floor or one or two floors above (the note for a safe sits in the outpost of its own floor 50% of the time, one floor below 30%, two below 20%; an outpost holds at most one note) - and either **gives the 4-digit code** (62% of safes) or says nobody knows it and **a crowbar will open it**. Read a note with E (it stays on the desk); everything you learn goes into your **journal (J)**. The safe's keypad (E) takes the code, with a 1.5 s lock-out after a wrong one; the safe's panel also has a **crowbar** button: 5 s of noisy prying (guards hear it), it **always opens** the safe and the crowbar **breaks 35% of the time** (`SafeRules.CrowbarBreakChance`; the crowbar in hand is used first, else any in the pack). Inside: **cash bundles worth half of the floor's quota** (`SafeRules.Cash`: $2,150 on floor 1 = half of the $4,300 quota; 4 to 8 bundles), real `Pickup`s you carry to the elevator.

**Interpretation decisions (change them if the team disagrees)**

- "The red room" = the red **guest elevator** in the farthest cell (red `guardMat` panels, the backup's arrival car). The outpost takes that cell; the backup elevator lives on as the alcove. The **boss floor** and any floor whose furnishing failed keep the old red car (no outpost there).
- The "security room" of the user's text = the outpost. "Codes (one per floor if there even is one)" = the safes' codes: at most one per floor.
- Notes are **not consumed** and the journal is **per player** (each player reads the notes himself); in a networked run the notes read the same on every machine (they follow from the run seed). If the team wants a shared journal it is `SafeIntel` plus a small RPC.
- Cash is carried and banked like other loot (it counts towards the quota and is paid out as surplus), not added straight to the wallet.
- The hack keys are W A S D (the user's example); the hack is played locally and only its result goes to the host, who checks that the player is near the keypad and not in custody.
- While the alarm sounds the lock is jammed; a lost hack while the alarm is already ringing does not call backup a second time.
- Guards walk through the locked door: it opens for them by the same route look-ahead as every other door (`GuardNear`) without unlocking it, so backup can leave its elevator and a patrolling guard can enter.

**How it fits together**

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `Interior/OfficePlanner.Outpost.cs` | Pure planner: the outpost room in the guest cell (`OutpostInfo`: room, bounds, window, door, keypad, alcove, item slots), the furniture (`ThOutpost`) and `PlaceSafe` (a spot against a wall of a numbered room; `SafeSpot`). Walls of kind `lockheader` mark the locked doorway. `PlanInput.outpost / hasSafe / safeRoll`. |
| `Interior/FloorShape.cs` | Pure: the maze (`FloorShape.Maze`, the exact logic and random-number order the generator always used), distances, the farthest cell, the guest door's direction, and `PlanFor(...)` that makes the planner's input. The generator and the safe-note dry run (below) both use it, so they cannot disagree. |
| `FloorGenerator.Outpost.cs` | Builds it: the alcove car (before the NavMesh bake), the door (`MakeOutpostDoor`: the panes are flat quads hung on the leaves), the one-way window, keypad, alarm lamp and the `OutpostController` (after the bake and the doors), the lockers and safe (called from `SpawnPiece`), and `FillOutpost` (notes, weapons, valuables, the safe's cash - last of all, after loot and guards). `SafeRoomLabel(floor)` names the room of the safe of **any** floor, also one not built yet (the planner is re-run on that floor's seed; about 20-50 ms, cached). Every step is wrapped (`OutpostStep`) so a mistake there is logged and the floor still loads. |
| `Outpost/OutpostController.cs` | One per floor: state (lock open, alarm going, safe open), the host's decision on a hack (`HostHackResult`), the siren, the keypad/lamp lighting. |
| `Outpost/HackRules.cs` + `HackPanel.cs` | The hack rules (pure, tested) and the screen/keys (`HackPanel.TestKeyDown` is an Editor-only stand-in for the keyboard that the tests use: 8.7). |
| `Outpost/SafeRules.cs`, `SafeNotes.cs`, `OutpostLoot.cs` | Pure rules (tested): which floors have a safe, codes, where the note sits, the note texts, cash, bundle split, crowbar break chance, what an outpost holds. Everything is a function of `(intelSeed, floor)` through an integer hash - **never** the floor's `System.Random`, so adding outposts did not change any floor's maze, loot or guards. |
| `Outpost/FloorSafe.cs`, `SafePanel.cs` | The safe prop (boxes, a door that swings open), its dormant cash bundles (real `Pickup`s created with the floor so ids match on every machine, switched off until it opens), the keypad / crowbar screen. |
| `Outpost/Interactable.cs`, `KeypadProp.cs`, `NoteProp.cs` | A small base for "aim at it and press E" things (the crosshair asks `Interactable.Aimed`), the keypad and the notes. |
| `Outpost/IntelScreens.cs`, `SafeIntel.cs`, `OutpostUi.cs` | The note and journal screens, the player's knowledge, and the one-screen-at-a-time gate that stops movement / E / Tab / swings while an outpost screen is up (`OutpostUi.Open`; Esc closes the screen instead of opening the menu). |
| `Net/RunSync.Outpost.cs` | Networking: the state is one `NetworkVariable<ushort>` (floor epoch in the high byte, bits 1 unlocked / 2 alarm / 4 safe open in the low byte); `RequestHack` and `RequestSafe` go to the host, which validates (near the keypad / safe, not in custody, right epoch) and decides; `SafeOpenedRpc` / `SafeReplyRpc` announce the result. |
| `Audio/OutpostSound.cs`, `SoundBank.cs` | Generated sounds: keypad beeps, hack win / fail, siren, safe open / wrong, crowbar prying and snap, paper. |
| `TowerManager.cs` | `IntelSeed` (the seed of safes and notes: random per Play offline - the floors themselves still repeat with `baseSeed` - and `baseSeed ^ 0x2D5AFE1` online), `intelSeedOverride`, `SetIntelSeed`, `RerollIntel` (test mode). |
| `PlanarMirror.cs`, `Resources/Shaders/ElevatorMirror.shader` | The window reuses the elevator's mirror: `blankWhenIdle` (shows a dark base colour, `_Base`, instead of a stale picture when the mirror camera is not drawing) and a one-way rule (visible only from the corridor side). |
| `Doors/SlidingDoor.cs`, `DoorManager.cs`, `FloorGenerator.Doors.cs` | `Locked` (E and the host refuse to open it; guards still pass), `LowLeaf/HighLeaf`, decor panes that follow the leaves, `IsLocked`, E on a locked door starts the hack. |
| `TestMode.cs`, `HowToPlayContent.cs` | Test panel section "Guard outpost & safes" (floors with a safe, their rooms and codes, go to a floor, learn every safe, re-roll the safe seed, unlock / alarm the lock, teleport to the door or the safe, open the safe); controls and a how-to-play section. |

**Randomness and networking in one paragraph**: the floor (maze, rooms, loot, guards) comes from `baseSeed + floor * 7919` as before. The outposts' and safes' content comes from `IntelSeed` through `SafeRules.Hash`. Online both are derived from the host's seed, so every machine builds the same floors, the same outposts and the same safes; the lock / alarm / safe state is mirrored by `vOutpost`. Dormant safe cash and the loot on the table are created in the same order everywhere (`NetIds`), after loot and guards, so earlier ids did not move. Offline the floors repeat each Play (as they always did) but the intel seed does not, so a given floor's safe and notes differ between Plays.

**Tests**

- Outside Unity (plain `csc`/.NET 8 consoles, not in the repo): `SafeRules`, `HackRules`, `SafeNotes`, `OutpostLoot` and `TowerRules` (2.59 million checks: 1-in-5 safes, codes, note placement, cash = half the quota, splits, the keys, the session timing, loot scaling, the note words); the planner (600 floors of random sizes: an outpost on every floor, the window/door/keypad geometry, the alcove, items on the table, safes placed 127 of 127, `FloorShape` identical to the old generator over 400 mazes); the after-hours and elevator tests were re-run with the outpost.
- In Unity: `NetDevHarness.Outpost` (above) plays the whole thing offline, **the way a player does**: the crosshair on the locked door or on the keypad (the game's own `Crosshair.Resolve` ray), E (`OutpostController.UseLockedDoor` / `KeypadProp.Use`, which is what the E handlers call), then W, A, S, D and Esc through the panel's own `Update`: `HackPanel.TestKeyDown` (Editor only, like `WobblyPlayer.testMove`) stands in for `Input.GetKeyDown`, because a batch run has no keyboard. **Never feed the `HackSession` from outside** (`session.Press`): the panel only reports a result it saw come about in its own `Update`, so a session finished from outside leaves the Hack screen up (`OutpostUi.Open`: the player cannot move or open anything) and the alarm off (8.7). `NetDevHarness.OutpostNet` (two processes) plays the same over the network with the client hacking floor 4's lock and taking the pew.
- Not tested at all: the look, the sounds, the mirror, the feel of the 1 second window, and the operating system's reading of the keys (8.7 says what is and is not proven). The lock hack over the network is `OutpostNet`.

**Check first in Unity**

1. Floor 1: does the farthest room look like an outpost from the corridor (long dark mirror, steel door with two panes, keypad, lamp)? Is the keypad on the right side of the door when you face it, and at a good height? Stand in front of the window: you should see the corridor mirrored; from inside the room you should see out through an opening, with nothing in the glass.
2. E on the keypad starts the hack; the keys are big and readable; W/A/S/D in time wins; the player does not walk while hacking; Esc backs out; a miss rings the siren, flashes lamp and keypad, calls two officers who arrive from the little red elevator inside the room and walk out through the (locked) door.
3. After a win the door slides open and stays; E on it closes and opens it like any door.
4. Inside: desks with monitors, lockers (procedural boxes with louvres), the note on the last desk (a sheet of paper you can point at), weapons and valuables on the table. Items should rest on the table, not float or fall through (their y comes from the table's top).
5. Reading a note: the screen comes up, J shows the journal, Esc closes. The room number named by the note must match the plate over a real door on that floor (the dry run for a later floor uses the same plan; compare once on a floor reached by the lift).
6. A floor with a safe (use test mode: "Go" next to a floor in the safe list): the safe stands against a wall of the numbered room the note names; the code works; a wrong code locks the keypad for 1.5 s; the crowbar path takes 5 s and sometimes snaps; the cash bundles can be picked up and banked ($2,150 on floor 1).
7. Frame rate while standing in front of the window (it renders the scene a second time while you can see it; `FloorGenerator.mirrorQuality` and `outpostMirror` are the knobs).
8. Host + client: the lock opens / the alarm rings / the safe opens on both machines; the client's hack result and safe attempts are judged by the host; the client's notes read the same.

**Known limits / ideas**

- No dedicated art: lockers, safe, keypad, monitors' glow are boxes; the "wired glass" is a generated texture. The alcove's door is the usual `ElevatorDoor`.
- Notes are not synced between players (journal per player), and a note read on a floor stays readable (not consumed).
- The safe's cash is not scaled by the number of players.
- A player who is not near the keypad when the host judges a hack (laggy position) can have the result ignored; `OutpostController.HostReach` (6.5 m) is generous.
- `Outpost/Interactable.cs` is the place to hang further "point and press E" things (the crosshair already handles them).
- The Esc handling relies on `EscapeMenu`'s previous-frame state (`outpostWasOpen`); if another screen is added that closes on Esc, give it the same treatment.

### 2.12 Alarm panel, direction signs and the bigger loot plan (be44)

Added in be44 at the user's request (Fri 2026-10-16): "a few signs in a few areas on the walls that will point the player in the direction of the room numbers (rooms 101-110 this way)", "a little bit more loot", and "a button in the outpost rooms that shuts off the alarm and stops the guards from auto spawning - this will also turn off auto spawning if a guard has spotted a player". **Never played in Unity**; compiled against stubs (0 errors, runtime and editor) and the pure logic is tested outside Unity (below). The user promised a picture of what the signs should look like; **it never arrived**, so the signs are a standard hotel-style dot-matrix sign (dark plate, light lettering, amber arrow). Restyle when the picture comes (the colours are the `Sign*Colour` constants in `FloorGenerator.Signs.cs`; the lettering is `Interior/DotText.cs`).

**A. The alarm panel (stand down)**

- **Where**: inside every guard outpost, on the room's face of the front wall's pier, beside the door (the same pier as the keypad, other side of the wall; 0.3 m past the door's edge, 1.3 m up; `OfficePlanner.OutpostPanelOffset/OutpostPanelY`, `OutpostInfo.panelX/Y/Z`). A steel box with a big red button in a yellow ring, two status lamps (green = all quiet, flashing red = alarm or backup going, bright green for 1.5 s after it was pressed) and the words ALARM OFF. `FloorGenerator.Outpost.cs: BuildAlarmPanel` (+ `DotLabel`), `Outpost/AlarmPanelProp.cs` (an `Interactable`; the prompt is `OutpostController.PanelPrompt`).
- **What E on it does** (`OutpostController.UsePanel` -> offline `HostStandDown()`, online `RunSync.RequestStandDown()` -> host validates epoch / player / not in custody / within `PanelHostReach` 5.5 m -> `HostStandDown()` -> `StandDownRpc` plays the sound and lamps everywhere): switches the **siren** off and clears the lock **jam** (the keypad can be used again), **cancels the 30 s repeating backup clock** whoever started it (the failed hack or a guard who spotted a player), and **jams the officers' radios for 10 s** (`CustodyRules.StandDownJamSeconds`, `HeistManager.RadiosJammed`: `CallBackup(GuardAI)` announces "RADIOS JAMMED" and does nothing in that window, so a guard who is still looking at the player cannot immediately call it back in). Nothing to switch off -> the notice "Nothing to switch off - the alarm is quiet".
- **Interpretation decisions** (change them if the team disagrees): the button is **inside** the outpost ("in the outpost rooms"), so the door has to be open first, i.e. a clean hack. A *failed* hack jams the lock for the siren's 14 s, so the panel cannot silence that siren itself; once the siren has run out, a retry can open the door, and then the panel ends the repeating backup (the officers every 30 s) that the failed hack started - and any backup a guard's sighting started. If the team wants the button reachable from the corridor too (it would make a failed hack nearly free), it is one more `AlarmPanelProp` outside the wall. Officers who already **arrived stay** (no despawning). A **later sighting calls backup again** after the 10 s jam. The `BackupCalled` flag is cleared, so the music drops out of its Intense mood when no officer is chasing.
- **Where the code is**: `HeistManager.StandDown()` (resets `backupClock`, `BackupCalled`, starts the jam, announces "ALL QUIET - the alarm is off and no more officers are coming"), `BackupClock.Reset()`, `OutpostController` region "the alarm panel inside the room", `RunSync.Outpost.cs` region "the alarm panel", sound `SoundId.AlarmOff` (`OutpostSound.AlarmOff`: a falling siren, a relay clunk and two blips; appended last in `SoundId`, `GeneratedVariants` has one more entry). State needs no new network variable: the siren bit and `vBackupIn / vBackupCalled` already mirror everything.
- **Test mode**: "Alarm panel: ... Press it / To the panel" in the Guard outpost section.

**B. Direction signs**

- **What they say**: the numbered rooms of a floor are grouped into **blocks of ten in the order of their numbers** (101-110, 111-120 ... and again 101A-110A ... on a floor with more than 99 rooms; a tail shorter than 4 joins the block before it, a short round of its own is one block, "ROOM 101A" for a block of one). A sign names a block that has **no room in the sign's own cell**, and its arrow points the way **the shortest walk through the maze's doorways to the nearest room of that block begins**: along the corridor the sign hangs in, towards a doorway that is open. So a sign is never wrong: it is true by construction, and the probe checks it on 400 floors.
- **Where**: `Interior/DirectionSigns.Plan` (pure): `AreasFor(cells)` areas (4 on the usual 8 x 5 floor; the first is the cell nearest the elevator that can take signs, then always the cell farthest from the chosen ones), at most 2 signs per cell (about 8 per floor), on wall faces that look into a corridor (kind 2 room, outside the room's `blocks`, outside the outpost), clear of doorways, furniture and other signs (0.5 m deep slab, 2.45 m up, 0.85 m tall, 2.2 m wide at 5 cm dots; it shrinks the dots down to 3.2 cm on a short wall and gives up below that). Everything follows from the layout + the floor seed, so every machine builds the same signs.
- **How they are drawn**: `FloorGenerator.Signs.cs: BuildDirectionSigns` (called right after `BuildRoomNumbers`): ONE mesh per floor with four materials (frame, plate, lettering, arrow), flat quads, no collider, shadows off, `OwnedMesh` so it is freed with the floor. `directionSigns` on the FloorGenerator turns the feature off. `PlanSigns()` returns the plan (cached per layout) for tools and tests.
- **Test mode**: "Direction signs" section: what each sign says and a "To the next sign" button.

**C. More loot**

- **Diagnosis**: only about a fifth of the rooms held anything: the themes flag many pieces as stealable, but the old picker took them in random order with at most two per 18 m cell, so floor 1 had ~44 small things + 9 treasures and only 23% of the numbered rooms held anything.
- **Now** (`Interior/LootPlan.cs`, pure): `SelectSmall` picks **coverage first** (one stealable piece per room, then more, at most `CellCap` = 4 per cell) up to `SmallTarget(floor)` = 52 + 4 x floor (was 40 + 4 x floor); `TreasureCount(floor)` = 10 + floor treasure spots (was 8 + floor); and `LooseSpots` + `FloorGenerator.PlaceLooseLoot` leave `LooseCount(floor)` = 12 + floor / 2 **small valuables** (cash envelope, gold watch, casino chips: `MakeValuable`, `OutpostLoot.Loose` for kind and worth, about 3.5% of the quota each) on desks and counters (the mug slots that got no mug) of numbered rooms that hold nothing else, at most one per room. They are bankable `Pickup`s with `bankValue`, like the outposts' valuables.
- **Simulated** (12 floors of 8 x 5 per level): floor 1 53 -> 79 items, 23% -> 48% of numbered rooms with something; floor 12 97 -> 136 items, 40% -> 76%. Floor totals go up (floor 1: $34.5k -> $49.7k against a $4,300 quota) because the quota is **not** raised (`min(QuotaFor, 60% of the floor's loot)`), so there is more surplus cash. If that is too generous, lower `SmallTarget` / `LooseCount`, or raise `QuotaFor` in `TowerRules`.
- **Determinism**: nothing here touches the floor's `System.Random` consumption (the shuffles use their own seeded generators: `seed ^ 0x2F6E2B1` for the small things, `layoutSeed ^ 0x5A17E3` for the loose valuables, the same shuffle of the treasure slots as before), so mazes, rooms and guards did not change; pickups are created in the same order on every machine (`PlaceLooseLoot` runs last, after `FillOutpost`). The `NetIds` count rises (about 30-40 more objects on a floor): the desync checksum compares it, so a host and client must run the same build (hence protocol "5").

**Files**

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `Interior/DotText.cs` | Pure 3 x 5 dot font A-Z 0-9 '-' and 7 x 7 arrows, as runs of lit dots (the room-number plates keep their own copy of the digits). |
| `Interior/DirectionSigns.cs` | Pure planner: blocks, maze distances, wall sites, candidates (block x direction), placement with clearance checks, `Compose` (the lettering). |
| `Interior/LootPlan.cs` | Pure loot arithmetic (see C). |
| `FloorGenerator.Signs.cs` | Sign mesh builder, `PlanSigns()`, `layoutSeed`. |
| `FloorGenerator.Outpost.cs` | `BuildAlarmPanel`, `DotLabel`, three more `OMat` colours. |
| `FloorGenerator.Interior.cs` | `SelectLoot` (now calls `LootPlan`), `PlaceLooseLoot`, `spareMugSlots`, `treasureSpots`. |
| `Outpost/AlarmPanelProp.cs`, `OutpostController.cs`, `Net/RunSync.Outpost.cs`, `HeistManager.cs`, `CustodyRules.cs` | The panel (A). |
| `Audio/OutpostSound.cs`, `SoundBank.cs` | `AlarmOff`. |
| `Editor/NetDev/NetDevOutpost.cs` | Play-mode scenario for the panel (offline): "panel:" lines (one panel, inside the wall, offers to shut off, pressing it ends the backup and jams the radios, a jammed sighting does nothing, after the jam backup can be called again and off again). |

**Tests** (outside Unity, not in the repo): the direction-sign probe (400 floors: every sign names a real block, no room of it in the sign's cell, the arrow's first step is on a shortest route and is an open doorway, arrow side agrees with the wall, the sign hangs on a full-height wall face inside a corridor, no wall / doorway / furniture in front of it, lettering fits and its runs do not overlap, two per cell at most, deterministic); the loot probe (cell cap, targets, one valuable per room, never in a room that already has something); the planner test now checks the panel's position on every one of 600 floors (beside the door on the keypad's side, on a full-height wall, nothing in front of it); `custody_test` (`BackupClock.Reset`, the radio jam window); a sound probe (`AlarmOff` is 1.1 s, peaks at 0.85, clicks nowhere, deterministic). All earlier tests still pass. **Not tested**: anything seen or heard in Unity, anything networked (the stand-down RPCs), the sign colours and size on a real wall.

**Check first in Unity**

1. Floor 1: walk the corridors. Do you see signs ("ROOMS 111-120" + arrow) within a minute or so of the elevator? Is the lettering readable at normal distance, the right way round (reads left to right), not mirrored, and not floating off or buried in the wall? Follow an arrow: does it lead towards rooms of that block (test mode "Direction signs" shows the target cell)? If the lettering is mirrored, flip `right` in `BuildDirectionSigns` (the room-number plates use the same convention and were fine).
2. Signs must not sit in front of doors, lights, plants or pictures; if one does, check `DirectionSigns.Blocked` (it only knows layout pieces, not the doors' decor).
3. In an outpost (open the door first - test mode "Unlock it"): the panel on the wall beside the door, red button visible and readable "ALARM OFF" label; the crosshair turns yellow on it; "[E] Shut off the alarm and call off the backup" while an alarm / backup is going, "Alarm panel - all quiet" otherwise.
4. Trip the alarm (test mode "Trip the alarm"), open the door, press the button: the siren dies away with the relay sound, the lamps flash green, the "ALL QUIET" banner, the backup countdown disappears, no more officers; officers already in the room stay. A guard spotting you within 10 s says "RADIOS JAMMED" and nothing happens; after that a sighting calls backup again.
5. Host + client: the client presses the button; both see the siren stop and hear the sound once; the backup banner vanishes on both.
6. Loot: several floors, count what a room offers (desk items, loose envelopes / watches / chips on desks of otherwise empty rooms). Do the loose valuables sit ON the desk (not floating or inside it)? They use the mug slots' heights.

**Known limits / ideas**: the signs do not glow (they are lit only by the ceiling lights); a sign is not placed on walls that are shorter than about 1.5 m between doorways; the planner never puts a sign in the elevator lobby's own corridor if it has no free wall; block ranges say how far a room is only through the arrow, not the distance; the panel can only be pressed from inside, which is on purpose.

### 2.12 Multiplayer QA, five players and the "players fall out of the map" fix (2026-10-16)

**The bug ("loading into a public match the player spawns outside the map and falls").** Commit 8cfe7ef ("audit fixes") made `WobblyPlayer.OnEnable` put the owner's Rigidbody on interpolation. With interpolation on, writing `transform.position` - which is all `NetworkTransform.Teleport` and the old offline placement did - is undone by the next physics step, so the placement of the team in the elevator car (`TowerManager.BuildFloor`) was lost: every networked player stayed where the host spawned them (the origin, outside the map; all capsules in one spot, which also flung them apart) and fell for ever. Found by a two-process test that logged the body every 0.1 s: `transform` = elevator, `rigidbody` = origin, one frame later back at the origin. It was in every networked run (public or not); solo play hid it because the car is in the same place on every floor.

**What changed (all host/offline rules are unchanged):**

- `WobblyPlayer.TeleportTo(pos, rot)` moves the Rigidbody (interpolation switched off and on) - **the only way to teleport a player**. `NetPlayer.PlaceAt`, `TowerManager` (offline placement), `TestMode`'s teleport buttons and the new rescue use it. Anything else that sets `transform.position` of a player is undone.
- Players spawn in the first elevator car (`TowerManager.PreFloorSpawn`, not the origin) and are **held still** (kinematic: `WobblyPlayer.HoldStill`, `NetPlayer.Hold/Release`) from the spawn, and again at the start of every floor build, until the floor is built and they have been placed (`TowerManager.HoldTeam / PlaceTeam / ReleaseTeam`). A body held still is not pushed by `WobblyPlayer.FixedUpdate`.
- A player (of this machine) below y = -30 is put back in the elevator (`TowerManager.Update`, twice a second, only while a floor exists): a net for anything that still goes through the floor.
- `TowerManager.StartNetworked` waits up to 150 s (was 30) for the host's run and the other players; without the host's seed it leaves with a message instead of building a different floor; `RunSync.expected` follows the number of connected clients, so a player who drops while the others load cannot make them wait.
- **Five players:** `NetConst.MaxPlayers` = 5 (the Steam lobby limit, the join rule and its message, the lobby menu text), a fifth `PartyCharacterFactory.PlayerLooks` entry ("Ember"), the lobby stage frames five (`LobbyStage.OverviewCamFive`, `TightSpacing`). `NetConst.ProtocolVersion` = "6".
- **Security / abuse limits:** `Net/RateLimiter.cs` (pure token bucket): a client's requests to the host (`RunSync.PlayerOf`, so every `*RequestRpc`) are limited to a burst of 60 and 40 a second, relayed voice packets to 100 and 70 a second (`VoiceChat.voiceBudget`); the excess is dropped and logged once every 5 s. `SteamNetworkTransport`: at most `NetConst.MaxPlayers` connections (accepted + being accepted) and one connection per Steam account (not in the dev loopback mode, where every process is the same account) - anybody who knows the host's Steam id could otherwise connect to the listen socket without being in the lobby; connection timeouts 25 s initial / 15 s connected; failed sends are counted and logged; traffic counters (`BytesSent`...). `NoiseRequestRpc` refuses NaN / infinite positions and loudness (a guard would be sent to nowhere). A join gets at least 30 s from the moment the Steam connection itself starts.
- A player who disconnects mid-run puts their pack on the floor where they stood (`NetPlayer.DropPackWhereTheyStood`), so the team keeps the loot and weapons.

**How the multiplayer QA is run** (one Unity process per player, one project copy each - Unity locks a project; never the owner's open project): `NetDevHarness.Spawn` (where everybody stands, nobody falls, positions agree on every machine, the rescue, and with `BE_SOAK=1` the traffic of five players walking on floor 1) and `NetDevHarness.Qa` (the whole suite: lobby, the sixth player refused, leaving and rejoining, spawn, voice between five, shop, panel, floors, loot, backup, elevator rules, a drop-out, the boss, the host leaving). Roles (`BE_QA_ROLE`): `host`, `c1`..`c4`, `late6` (refused when the lobby is full, and again after the run started); `BE_PLAYERS`, `BE_RUN` (keeps the signal files of two runs apart), `BE_TRANSPORT=sock` (the real Steam transport code over loopback; needs Steam running), `BE_SIM=delay,jitter,loss%` (Unity Transport's network simulator). The scripts that start the processes are not in the repo. **Unity's batch runs empty `Library/PackageCache` of a scratch copy: refill it from a working project before every compile or launch.**

**Measured (this machine, batch mode, no graphics):** `Qa` with five players: host 46 / c1 45 / c3 44 / c4 43 / late6 5 checks, c2 33 (it drops out), 0 failures, no desync, no exceptions; the same suite on a simulated 80 ms +-30 ms / 2 % loss network: identical result; `Spawn` with five players on a 120 ms +-40 ms / 3 % loss network: all pass on all five machines. Five players over the Steam transport code (loopback), walking on floor 1 for 20 s: the host sends 33 KB/s in all (about 8 KB/s per client), a client receives 8 KB/s and sends 1.4 KB/s, about 130 messages/s per client, 0 failed sends; the per-client receive budget at the host is 128 KB/s.

**Not tested (needs hardware or a second account):** a real Steam relay connection between two accounts (Steam refuses a P2P connection to your own account; the P2P calls are unchanged, only the limits and timeouts around them are new), a public lobby with strangers, real microphones, a frame-rate on real hardware with five players. **Known limit:** the owner of a body decides where it is (`OwnerNetworkTransform`), so a modified client can teleport or fly; the host validates what it *does* (reach, ownership, cost, rates) but not where it *is*. Fine for friends; a public release should make movement server-authoritative or at least validate speed on the host.

### 2.13 The pew pistol and armed guards (be46)

Added at the user's request (Fri 2026-10-16): "implement the pew prefab to work as a weapon that is only available in test mode right now ... the most powerful weapon currently ... reload after every 10 shots ... use the gun sound effects assets ... Guards will eventually have this weapon once a certain floor is reached so please make armed guards for testing as well ... it should be able to be spawned from the dev menu". Played in Unity batch mode offline (`NetDevHarness.Pew`: 69 checks, 0 failures; `PewLook` takes the pictures) and over the local transport with two processes (`NetDevHarness.Armed` = `ArmedHost` + `ArmedClient`: 0 failures); the pure rules are `NetDevTest.GunTest`, inside `PolicyTest` (160 PASS). **Applied to the repo on 2026-10-16** (checked by file hash against the verified scratch tree). **Never seen on a real screen except through the tests' screenshots, never heard.**

**What the player gets**

- **Only from the test menu** (F1, offline only): Weapons > **Pew (gun)** puts a pew in the pack and in the hand. It is an ordinary `Pickup` (1.2 kg, can be dropped, picked up and equipped from the inventory like the melee weapons) with a `RangedWeapon` component. The test menu also has a "Pew & armed guards" block: rounds left + refill, "Every guard carries a pew", and the floor rule stepper. Since be50 two other things give it out (2.17): the outpost of floor 4 always holds one, and the vendor sells it for nothing once floor 4 is finished.
- **Controls**: the pew is shown in the right hand with the arm raised at the crosshair; **LMB fires** (hold to keep firing, one shot every 0.3 s), **R reloads**. The magazine holds **10 rounds**; after the tenth the gun reloads by itself (0.25 s later, **2.2 s**, the gun tilts up while it does); the trigger of an empty gun clicks. The reload is five sounds in order (slide lock, magazine out, drawn, in, charge). An ammo panel (bottom right) shows `PEW 7 / 10`, one pip per round and a reload bar.
- **The strongest weapon**: one bullet counts as **five of the ten hits** that put a guard down (`RangedWeapon.Specs[Pew].guardHits`), so two bullets (0.6 s) do what ten swings did; it stuns 8 s (crowbar 4), knocks things about harder (16 vs 5-9), takes 60 off a training dummy (melee 11-22) and is loud: **45 m** (melee 14-18), every guard that hears it comes to look. `NetDevTest.GunTest` checks that every number beats every melee weapon.
- **A bullet** goes from the muzzle to wherever the crosshair points (into a 1.2 degree cone), stops at the first wall, door or piece of furniture (loose items and teammates do not stop it), shows a muzzle flash (light and quad), a tracer, a spark where it lands and throws a brass case (the pack's `bulletPEW` model), and plays one of the three gunshots with its tail. The crosshair turns red when a bullet would hit a guard or the dummy, at any distance.

**Armed guards**

- `ArmedGuards` (static): **`FirstArmedFloor`** is the one number for "once a certain floor is reached": the guards made on that floor and above carry a pew. It is **4** since be50 (`ArmedGuards.DefaultFirstArmedFloor`, 2.17); the test menu can step it or switch it off. `Everywhere` (test menu toggle "Every guard carries a pew") arms every guard of every floor, now and when they are made, backup included; `ForceForTests` (editor) does the same for the QA runs.
- An armed guard (`GuardGun`, added to him by `GuardVisual` when he is made, or by the test menu) holds the pew in his right hand (his flashlight moves to the front of the gun). After the backup call, while he **sees** a free player who is 3 to 22 m away he **stops, raises the gun (a rustle of leather lets you hear it), takes 0.5 s to aim and fires one shot every 0.85 s** at the player's chest; the bullet goes into a cone of 2.5 degrees + 0.28 per metre, stops at walls and doors and passes his colleagues; a player it hits is **knocked down** (a 1.8 s stumble, shoved 7 m/s; a pinned or arrested player is passed over; **god mode in the test menu makes the shots harmless**); after ten shots he stands still for 2.2 s reloading - the moment to rush him. Closer than about 3 m he does what an unarmed officer does and grabs. His shots are 45 m noises. Everything tunable is a public field of `GuardGun`.
- **Networked**: the host decides everything (aim, hit, stumble). A guard's `armed` state is bit 4 of the guard snapshot's flags (`RunSync.Snapshots.cs`), so a client dresses its puppet with the gun (and takes it away) by itself; each shot is one reliable message (`RunSync.BroadcastGuardShot` / `GuardShotRpc`: guard id, where the bullet ended, whether it hit something) that the client turns into the flash, the tracer, the spark and the sound. `NetConst.ProtocolVersion` = "7". The player's own pew **is networked since be50** (2.17, `Net/RunSync.Guns.cs`; before that `GunController` did nothing while `NetSession.Online`); `NetConst.ProtocolVersion` is "10" now.

**Files**

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `Scripts/Guns/GunRules.cs` | Pure C#: `GunMagazine` (rounds, the 0.3 s between shots, the reload; the caller passes the time), `GunRules` (the spread cone, guard accuracy, bullets-to-kill, "stand and shoot?"), `ArmedRules` (which floors). Tested outside Unity. |
| `Scripts/Guns/RangedWeapon.cs` | The item: `Specs` (the numbers), the magazine that travels with the item, the model measured from the prefab (`LayoutOf`: scale, muzzle, grip), `Create` (a pickup lying on its side), `BuildHeld` (the model on a hand bone). |
| `Scripts/Guns/HeldGun.cs` | A gun in a hand (player or guard): turns the right arm along `AimDirection` after the animation each frame (`LateUpdate`), kick on a shot. |
| `Scripts/Guns/GunController.cs` | The player's side: input, aiming from the camera, firing, reloading, the ammo panel. Added to every player by `WobblyPlayer`. |
| `Scripts/Guns/Ballistics.cs`, `GunFx.cs`, `GunSounds.cs` | The bullet's path, the pooled effects (nothing is created while firing), the sounds. |
| `Scripts/Guns/ArmedGuards.cs`, `GuardGun.cs` | Who is armed, and the guard's behaviour. `GuardAI.UpdateChase` asks the gun whether to stand and shoot; `GuardAI.WeaponHit(stun, by, weight)` takes a bullet's weight. |
| `Scripts/WeaponCatalog.cs`, `AudioCatalog.cs`, `Editor/WeaponCatalogBuilder.cs`, `AudioCatalogBuilder.cs` | `pew`, `pewBullet` (Guns pack) and `gunShot/gunTail/gunReload/gunEquip/gunAim` (Free Weapon SFX, Handgun) are filled in by the builders when the editor loads (only what is missing); the two `.asset` files already contain them. **Commit `Assets/FreeWeaponSFX_v1.1` (it was untracked) or the sounds are missing on other machines.** |
| `Editor/NetDev/NetDevTest.cs` (`GunTest`), `NetDevPew.cs`, `NetDevPewNet.cs`, `NetDevPewLook.cs` | Tests: pure rules; the offline play-through; the two-process replication; pictures of the pew in every kind of hand. |

**Decided by the owner (2026-10-16):** a gunshot on a player stays the knock-down stumble (no damage, no death - there is no health in the game). **Open design questions** (defaults chosen, change them if the team disagrees): whether a guard who is armed should also shoot a player he has only heard (now: only one he sees); the real "armed from floor N" number; (answered in be50, 2.17: it is loot in floor 4's outpost and a free shop item after floor 4); sounds for a bullet hitting a wall; a bullet hole decal; the hit reaction of a guard (he is stunned or lies down like with melee).

**Check first in Unity**: (1) F1 > Pew: the gun is in the right hand, the arm points at the crosshair, the flash is at the muzzle, the tracer ends on the crosshair, the shot sounds right and not doubled; (2) ten shots then the reload with its five sounds, the panel and the bar; (3) a guard goes down in two hits, the crosshair goes red over him; (4) F1 > "Every guard carries a pew": the gun in his hand and the flashlight on it; get seen: he stops at range, raises it, shoots at you, you are knocked down, he reloads after ten; god mode makes it harmless; (5) host + client with the editor-only `ArmedGuards.ForceForTests` (or `FirstArmedFloor`) set in the host: the client sees the guns and the shots.

### 2.14 The toon HUD (be47)

Added at the owner's request (Fri 2026-10-16): "currently in-run the UI is just text on the screen. I would like the UI to be a bit more straight forward and easy to read with colors that make sense, perhaps have the cash be green and things like that" and "Not just colors for the UI also, it should look nicer, better styling". The owner picked the **cartoon / toon** look to match the toon characters: chunky dark ink outlines, bright saturated fills in two flat tones (a lit top, a shaded bottom), a hard drop shadow, a pale "sticker" rim so a card still stands out on the dark night floors, outlined text, key caps, and bouncy pop-in animation.

**What the player sees** (all drawn by `HudWidgets`, from plain numbers):

- **Status card** (top left): a round floor badge ("FLOOR 4 of 13", "DOCKS", "BOSS"), the **cash big and green** next to a gold coin (it counts up and punches when it changes; a "+$250" floats off to the right), a **quota bar** (gold, turns green with "QUOTA MET!" and sparkles), a LOST chip (salmon) and a RUN TOTAL chip (blue); on the docks the bar says NEXT QUOTA, on the boss floor KNOCK OUT THE BOSS. Under it the team row: FREE n (green), PINNED n (red, pulsing), BUSTED n (grey), and BACKUP IN n (orange, with a flashing siren) while backup is coming.
- **Key hints** along the bottom as two rows of key caps (they shrink to fit a narrow window; the words are the same as before, plus "CTRL / C crouch").
- **Banner** at the top centre for the moments that matter (QUOTA MET!, LOADING DOCKS, ESCAPED THE TOWER, BUSTED) with the old sentences as one or two lines under it, and a toon **Restart / Back to menu** button on the end screens.
- **Prompts** ("[E] Pick up Mug (0.4 kg, $120)", doors, the dealer, the elevator panel) as a pill with a key cap; they pop in when they appear or change. **Notices** (elevator toast, backup message) as coloured pills.
- **Pack card** (bottom left): weight bar green / orange / red, loot count and value in gold, what is in hand. **Ammo panel** (bottom right, only with the pew out): rounds as bullet icons, the count (orange at 3 or fewer, red at none), a reload bar, LMB / R key caps.
- **Backup**: the call ring is a thick ring that fills with a flashing siren in it (big one at the top, small ones over the callers' heads); **pinned / busted**: a big plate with handcuffs, PINNED + a round seconds badge (yellow to red as it runs out) or BUSTED + the buy-back price, and a tag over every teammate who is down. **Voice** pills at the top right (mic icon, "Player 2 is talking"), the TEST MODE tag, and an ink-outlined **crosshair** that punches when what is under it changes.

**The colours mean the same thing everywhere**: cash green, quota / loot gold, lost salmon, danger red (police, alarm, pinned), warning orange (heavy pack, few rounds, backup coming), information blue (floor, run total), grey-violet = off / busted, cream = key caps. Change them in `HudTheme`.

**Files**

| File | Role |
