**What the owner asked (after the door terminal):** "On the Nav map when an officer or player is moving their icon should have a slight ping going off. Also please work to make the Nav map easier to read, for example the locked door being read like the officer might be confusing at first. I attached a bunch of reference images, these are not to be copied but referenced ... Use these to make a map that fits this game and don't fully implement it yet, for now just show it as a test so that we can decide if we want to use it or keep working on it." The four pictures: a live "employee tracking" floor plan (person icons in team colours, a trail behind each, zoom +/- and start/stop); a plan with an info pop-up card on a person, a FIND box and a highlighted room; a desk-booking plan (status dots, a legend, a highlighted room); an "areas of interest" plan (coloured zones with names, custom symbols, a search with suggestions).

**Built.** A test, opt-in: the old Safes / Nav / Hacked pages are untouched, except that the monitor is a little taller (0.74 of its width, it was 0.70; the owner said it could be bigger). Files: `Outpost\NavMapRules.cs` (NEW, pure: `NavView` = zoom / pan arithmetic, `NavMotion` = the ping and trail tracker, `NavMapRules` = room kinds, typed-number matching, ping geometry), `Outpost\ComputerScreen.NavTest.cs` (NEW: `ComputerScreen` is now `partial`; `TabMapTest = 4`, the button "MAP TEST", drawn by `DrawMapTestPage`), `ComputerScreen.cs` (the fourth tab button, the dispatch, the shared terminal on it). What the test tab does, and which picture each idea came from:
- **Pings (the owner's ask):** a thin ring spreads from every officer and player icon WHILE IT MOVES (smoothed speed of at least 0.6 m/s, measured frame to frame): the first ping 0.25 s after it sets off, then one every 0.9 s; each lives 1.2 s, spreads from 1.1 to 3.3 times the icon and fades (never above 60 % strong, the colour lightened). Standing still = no ping; a jump (the elevator, a new floor) is not movement; no last ping while it slows to a stop. PINGS on/off button.
- **Easier to read:** a locked room can no longer be mistaken for an officer. A LOCKED room has an orange edge, an orange tint and a white padlock on a dark badge pinned to its corner (and its locked doors are orange bars); an OFFICER is a red diamond with a dark edge (no letter); a PLAYER is a round badge in the player's own colour with the number, a little arrow for where he faces, and "YOU" under this machine's player; speakers are small dots (the full icon when zoomed in; click = on / off as before); the lifts and stairs are tiles with arrows / steps; the security office is green with a computer glyph; suites are purple, the server room blue; the rooms are lighter blocks with a dark wall between them that grows with the zoom, and every door is a light tick across its wall (the plans' look).
- **From the pictures:** zoom + / - / FIT, the mouse wheel (zoom about the pointer) and dragging (the tracking plan); a short fading trail behind moving icons, TRAILS on/off (the tracking plan); a click on a room opens a small card - number, kind, doors, LOCKED / OPEN, who is inside, a LOCK / UNLOCK button (the pop-up card); a number typed in the terminal lights up the room it names and the rooms that start with it, in cyan (the FIND box); a key whose rows are drawn exactly like the things on the plan, and colour-coded kinds (the desk-booking and areas-of-interest plans). The same terminal sits under it (a number switches the doors, SECURITY the office).
- **Left out on purpose:** the search-suggestions list, floor / building selectors (the computer only knows its own floor), an evacuation route, custom symbols for rooms, names on the icons.
- **Pictures** (`ui.ps1 -Methods ComputerUi`, written to `%TEMP%`): `be_ui_computer_nav_locked.png` (the OLD Nav, for comparison), `be_ui_computer_maptest.png`, `_maptest_zoom.png` (zoom 3.2 on a locked suite with its card), `_maptest_find.png` (a typed "10"). The scenario now keeps the officers from arresting the player (sight 0.1: it was a 6-second scene with a guard in the room) and shows a party that is not there (`ComputerScreen.DebugPlayers`, test-only list).
- **Tests:** pure +3 (the view maths; the ping tracker: standing still, 2.8 s of walking = 3 pings and a trail, stopping, a 30 m jump, forgetting; the typed-number matches, the room kinds, the ping geometry): 497; R7 +3 (the tab exists and has the same terminal): 97. **2. Safes with weapons, several from floor 3** (pure rules in `Outpost\SafeRules.cs`, the words in `SafeNotes.cs`):
- Floors 1 and 2: ONE safe with cash, exactly as before. Floor 3: two safes; floors 4-6: three; floor 7 up: four (`SafeCount`, `MaxSafes` = 4). - The computer's record has the FIRST safe's code always on a weapon floor and each other safe's code 62 % of the time (`RecordGivesCode`; floors 1-2 as the note did); a safe whose code is not on record takes a crowbar, and `AnySafeNeedsCrowbar` is what makes a crowbar likely on the outpost's desk now. The record also says what is inside ("Pew pistol", "2 Pew pistols", "Cash $4,300": `SafeNotes.ContentsOf`).
- Runtime: `FloorSafe.OnShow` (the safes of the floor on show; NOT `All`: that hid `Interactable.All`), `Active` (the first), `ByIndex`, `Index`, `GunCount`, `HoldsGun(go)`, `FillWithGuns` (the pistols lie side by side 0.30 m apart on the floor of the hollow, barrels to the door, switched off - with their network ids already taken, like the gun safe's pistol - until the safe opens; `ApplyOpen` switches them on). `FloorGenerator.Outpost`: `floorSafes`, `SafeRoomLabel(floor, index)`, `BuildFloorSafePiece` finds the safe's index from the planned spot, `FillOutpost` makes pistols on weapon floors and cash on floors 1-2, in index order (every machine numbers the things the same). `OutpostController`: `Safes`, `Safe` = the first; state bits 4 = first safe, 16 / 32 / 64 = second to fourth (the same state byte, no new variable). `RunSync.Outpost`: the safe's number in `RequestSafe`, `SafeRequestRpc`, `HostSafe`, `SafeOpenedRpc`, `SafeReplyRpc`. **Protocol "18": everybody needs this build.** The keypad's title says "FLOOR SAFE 2" when the floor has several; its note ("Your notes say the code is ...") reads the (floor, safe) record.

**3. Only the current floor (the owner's third message).** The record on a computer is about the outpost's OWN floor: `SafeRules.NoteFor` / `SourceFloor` are now the identity (nothing is announced a floor or two ahead any more; `MaxLookAhead`, the look-ahead roll and its salt are gone). `SafeIntel.OnFloor(floor, list)`; the Safes tab lists `prop.outpostFloor`'s records and the journal (J, "NOTES - FLOOR SAFES") lists `HeistManager.Floor`'s (at the docks: its "Nothing yet" text). The records of other floors stay in memory (same run, same seed, so they are right again if the team comes back) but are not shown. `SafeIntel` entries are keyed (floor, safe number) with `count` and `contents`. (A scrolling list for many floors that I built first is gone again: a floor has four safes at most.)

**Words I changed (the owner writes the game text: reword freely):** the journal's empty text ("Nothing yet. Every floor has a safe: cash on the first two floors, weapons above. The guard outposts ... keep a record of the floor's safes on their computer: ..."); the Safes tab's new column INSIDE and the "(2/3)" next to the floor number; the journal line ("Safe 2 of 3 on floor 5, room 507 - code 4821 - 2 Pew pistols"); the keypad title "FLOOR SAFE 2"; the `HowToPlayContent.cs` rows for J and for outposts and safes (which still said "about one floor in five hides a safe full of cash"); README items 7 and 12.

**Calls I made (change any):** (a) Only PISTOLS go in the safes. The pew is the only gun there is, and a crowbar (0.85 m), a bat (1.0 m) or a hatchet (0.70 m) hardly fits a safe whose hollow is 0.78 m wide; "mainly guns" is therefore all guns, and the melee weapons stay on the outpost's desk (the owner's rule of 2026-10-17 night). The owner confirmed it (2026-10-18, when asked): keep the safes pistols only. (b) Floor 3 now has 3 to 5 pistols (the outpost's gun safe, still hacked open, plus 1-2 in each of the two floor safes); the vendor still sells the pew once floor 3 is done. (c) The first safe of a weapon floor always has its code on the computer, so the team always has one safe it can open at once. (d) A safe the planner cannot place is simply not listed (it never happened in 240 tries).

**What the owner asked (after update 7):** (1) "Each safe should be one weapon max, up to 4 safes per floor and the note/safes tab should not say what is in each safe" (and, asked, "Keep the safes pistols only."). (2) "Safes with a weapon can also have a chance to spawn a bit of cash and a chance to spawn a back up clip of ammo that all have to be picked up separately (one click hovering the weapon, one click hovering the ammo, and one click hovering the cash)". (3) "The security outpost should not have a room number but rather should be called Security so for locking the door should be typing Security". (4) "I added more Synty assets for the weapons, please utilize and implement them and make sure the silencer is on the exit barrel of the pew"; then "shotguns and snipers should not be silenced either"; then photographs of suppressed rifles ("the last point the bullet should exit from is the silencer (basically making it an extension of the existing barrel visually)") and "Silencers should be on pistols, sub machines, and rifles". (5) "Please also add the chance of a watch being in the safe". (6) "Please also make the UI main color blue instead of purple and make it a bit cleaner/less cartooney but don't stray from the current look too much ... Please create this UI as a separate test UI, don't fully implement it yet until we confirm we like it" - and, after the test, "The UI looks good please implement it".

**1. Safes (pure rules in `Outpost\SafeRules.cs`).** One safe on floors 1 and 2 (cash, as before), two on floor 3, three on floors 4-6, four from floor 7 (`SafeCount`, `MaxSafes` = 4). A weapon safe (floor 3 and up) holds exactly ONE pew pistol (`WeaponsPerSafe` = 1, `WeaponContents`) and sometimes extras, each an item of its own (aim at it, E for each; dormant - switched off, network id already taken - until the safe opens): a bit of cash (`SideCashPercent` 40 %, 3 % of the quota in $25 steps, `SideCash`), a spare pistol clip (`SideClipPercent` 50 %; `AmmoClip`, picked up with E: its 8 rounds go into the pistol's reserve; refused with no pistol in the pack) and, new, a gold watch (`SideWatchPercent` 30 %, any safe, floors 1 and 2 too; `HasSideWatch`, `SideWatchValue` = 1.4 typical small valuables of the floor, the same gold watch as the desks' and the outposts', made by `FloorGenerator.BuildValuable`; `FloorSafe.WatchSpot` / `AddWatch` / `WatchCount` / `Watches`, front right of the hollow). Everything is decided by the seed alone (salts 34, 35, 36), so every machine builds the same. Nothing the computer or the journal says tells what is inside: the Safes tab and the journal (J) list floor, room and code only, and only for the floor you are on (`SafeIntel` entries are (floor, safe number, count, room, code)). **Protocol "20": everybody needs this build** (a floor holds other things than in build i).

**2. Security.** The guard outpost has no room number; the computer's terminal says "ROOM NUMBER OR SECURITY >", the map labels the office rectangle SECURITY (always on), the card and the log say Security (`FloorDirectory.OutpostName`).

**3. The Synty weapons** (the pack is `Assets\Synty\PolygonBattleRoyale`; the catalog `Resources\WeaponCatalog.asset` now points at its prefabs: crowbar = `SM_Wep_Crowbar_01`, bat = `SM_Wep_Bat_01`, hatchet = `SM_Wep_Fireaxe_01`, pew = `SM_Wep_Pistol_Heavy_01`, silencer = `SM_Wep_Attachment_Silencer_01`, the thrown-out case = `SM_Wep_Pistol_Ammo_01`, the spare clip = the pistol's own `..._Mag_01` part). `WeaponCatalog` has the new fields (turns, grips, `pewMuzzleFace`, `silencerAt`, `modelsVersion`) and `WeaponCatalog.Measure` (a model's box once turned; a build cannot read mesh vertices, and neither can a Play-mode test: "isReadable is false"). **`WeaponCatalogBuilder.FillSynty` measures what the runtime cannot** - the middle of the pistol's muzzle face (front-most ring of its body mesh), the bottom of the silencer's rear recess, where the hand closes on each melee handle (the crowbar's origin is a hand's width beside its shaft) - and stores it in the catalog; it runs once per catalog (`modelsVersion` 1; the menu "Employees Take Revenge > Setup Weapons" does it again; `-executeMethod BuildingEscape.EditorTools.WeaponCatalogBuilder.UpgradeBatch` for the QA runs). `MeleeWeapon`: `LayoutOf` (scale to the spec length, box, grip), items are centred on their box, the weapon is held by its handle (`GripFromEnd` 0.16 / 0.14 / 0.12 m up from the end opposite the head). `RangedWeapon`: `LayoutOf` works in the game's frame (barrel +X, top +Y: the pistol model is turned a quarter about Y, `pewTurn`), `CreateClipModel` builds the spare clip from the pistol's Mag part. Mesh colliders are switched off before they are destroyed (a non-convex mesh collider must not sit on a rigid body even for a frame). The old pack's models stay in the project and are used if the Synty ones are missing.

**4. The silencer** (the owner's photographs: it is an extension of the barrel). The Synty silencer sits on the END of the barrel, on the barrel's own line: its rear recess (8 mm deep, measured) is over the barrel's last millimetres, so the exposed barrel and the front sight stay as the model has them and the tube continues in line. `RangedWeapon.AttachSilencer` (a child called "Silencer": direct child of the lying item and of the hand's grip transform), `Layout.silencedMuzzle` = the middle of its front face = the `Muzzle` transform when silenced: the flash, the tracer, the host's trace and the noise all start there (`GunController` uses `held.Muzzle`). The empty case is thrown out of the slide's ejection port (`HeldGun.Port`, `Layout.port`; `GunFx.Shot(..., casingFrom)`), not from the muzzle. **The rule by weapon class** (`RangedWeapon.WeaponClass`, `TakesSilencer`, `IsSilenced`, `Spec.weaponClass`/`CanSilence`): pistols, sub machine guns and rifles can take a silencer; shotguns and sniper rifles never do and are never silenced, whatever is asked - `silenced` is now a property that applies the rule. The pew is the only gun in the game (a pistol); the other four classes are rules waiting for guns: **no SMG, rifle, shotgun or sniper exists yet** (the Synty pack has models for all four). The officers' pews are not silenced (loud, as before).

**5. The blue UI (the owner approved the test: "The UI looks good please implement it").** The blue look is THE look now: `HudTheme`, `MenuKit` and `HudWidgets` draw only it, and the test scaffolding is gone (`UiStyle`, `HudTheme.Style` / `Preview` / `Blue`, the Settings page's "UI LOOK" switch, the F1 test menu's button, the harness's `UiBlue` ... pictures). It is the earlier "cleaner UI" pass of `DevTools\CleanerUI_2026-10-17` (thinner ink lines, a smaller and fainter shadow, a quieter gloss, a slimmer sticker rim, a thin even text outline, quiet section tags, lettering outlined in a dark shade of the button's own colour) in a blue palette instead of purple: card `0x2D5BB4`, ink `0x0E1B3C`, muted text `0xD2E1FC`, dim `0x93A8D2`, bar track `0x15306C`, tags `0x5584D6`, the secondary ("violet") buttons `0x5A80F0` (`HudTheme.Violet`; `SettingsPanel.Violet` and the lobby menu's `Violet` read it). The finish numbers are the constants under "the finish" in `Hud\HudTheme.cs` (outline 0.78, shadow 0.55 deep and 0.30 dark, rim 0.65 and 0.55, shade 0.18, gloss 0.20), the palette is the first block of that file: one edit changes every screen built from it. The old purple look is in the repository's git history and in the backup `DevTools\QaBackups\before_round8m_apply_2026-10-18`. The computer's own screens, the player colours (a violet is player 6's) and the 3D world are untouched.

**Words I changed (the owner writes the game text: reword freely):** the Safes tab / journal lines lost their contents text (update 7's "INSIDE" column and "- 2 Pew pistols" are gone); the computer prompt "ROOM NUMBER OR SECURITY > "; README_PLAYTEST items 9, 12 and the new item 16.

**Calls I made (change any):** (a) The gold watch: 30 % in any safe, worth about $200 on floor 1 (1.4 x a typical small valuable of the floor), lying at the front right of the hollow; `SafeRules.SideWatchPercent` / `SideWatchValue` are the two numbers. (b) The classes: the rule is by weapon class, so the owner's two sentences ("pistols, sub machines and rifles" yes, "shotguns and snipers" no) hold for any gun added later; I did NOT add an SMG, rifle, shotgun or sniper (the owner did not ask for the guns themselves and the Synty pack has models for all four: say so and I add them with their own specs, models, hold poses, vendor and loot entries). ### 2.28 The owner's queue of 2026-10-19 (guards, bullets, ammunition, armour, the last floors, zoom, darts) - recorded, built only where a line says "built"

The owner sent these after the weapons round ("Upon restarting please add these to the list of tasks"). Their words are in quotation marks; "=>" is my reading; "Q:" is what I will ask before or while I build that item. Nothing here was invented by me: the numbers (which floor unlocks which gun) are theirs.

**The unlock ladder, as the owner gave it** (a gun or an armour is "unlocked" on the floor where it is first found): floor 3 pistol (the pew) and Light Armour; floor 5 sub machine gun; floor 6 shotgun; floor 7 Medium Armour; floor 8 assault rifle; floor 9 Deagle; floor 10 sniper and Heavy Armour; floor 11 rocket launcher; floor 12 "complete chaos"; floor 13 the boss, who only talks.

**A. Guards and damage**
- Q1 "Bullets should not knock down, bullets should eliminate and depending on the bullet determines how much health a guard loses" => a guard has health, every gun's bullet takes its own amount, at zero he is eliminated, and a bullet never knocks a guard over. (Today: `RangedWeapon.Spec.stun` knocks a guard down for 8 s and `Spec.guardHits` counts a bullet as 4 of the guard's 10 hits, `GuardAI.WeaponHit`.)
- Q2 "Shooting a guard should also stop the pinning animation" => a guard that is pinning a player lets go when he is shot.
- Q3 "Melee weapons knock over a guard one shot (depending on the tier of the weapon determines on how long guard are knocked down)" => one swing knocks a guard over, the weapon's tier sets for how long (today a swing counts as one of ten hits and stuns 3 to 6 s).
- Q4 "There should be unarmed guards and armed guards in the game"; "The toon characters should remain the main unarmed officers".
- Q5 Armed guards by floor: "The first level (3rd floor) of armed guards should have pistols with light shield"; "The floor (5th) that unlocks the sub machine gun should have guards with sub machine guns and light shield as well as pistol guards with light shield"; "The floor (6th) that unlocks the shotgun should have guards with shotguns and heavy shield (there should be very few of these shotgun guards on all floors above this one)"; "The floor (8th) where the assault rifle is unlocked should have guards with assault rifles and medium shield"; "The floor (9th) where the Deagle is unlocked should have a guard with the Deagle and heavy shield"; "The floor (10th) where the sniper is unlocked should have guards with snipers with no shield". Mixes: SMG floors "some submachine gun guards and a few shotgun guards"; the assault rifle floors "should be only assault rifle guards and a few shotgun guards"; once snipers are unlocked "there should be assault rifle guards, a few sniper guards, and two sniper guards" (garbled: my reading is assault rifle guards, a few shotgun guards, a few sniper guards, and two more sniper guards - Q: confirm the numbers).
- Q6 "Only shotgun/sniper officers eliminate the player as in not just knockdown but end for the current floor (when a player is hit by a guard with the 50 cal sniper or the shotgun they should explode in a manner with reference to R.E.P.O)" => a hit from a shotgun or sniper guard ends that player's floor with an explosion effect; every other guard still only knocks down.

**B. Ammunition**
- Q7 "The Vendor should sell ammo for the specific guns after finding them, ammo from the Vendor should be expensive." => the dealer sells a gun's ammunition once that gun has been found; dear.
- Q8 "A player should be allowed to have three gun clips max."
- Q9 "Guns found should have one clip in them, guards should drop a fair amount of bullets per guard." => a found gun holds its one magazine and no spares; an eliminated guard drops bullets for his gun.
- Q10 "Different guns take different ammo." => every gun has its own ammunition.
- Q11 "Deagle gets special ammo that is also armour piercing (no silencing a Deagle)."

**C. Armour**
- Q12 "On the third floor players should have the chance of finding Light Armour instead of a weapon in the safe." "After finding the Light Armour if the player who found the armour makes it back to the Loading Docks, everyone in the party should be able to buy the Light Armour from the Vendor. The same should go for the Medium Armour after found on the 7th floor and the Heavy Armour found on the 10th floor." => armour is found in a safe; the finder bringing it back to the docks unlocks it in the dealer's shop for the whole party.

**D. The last floors**
- Q13 "The twelfth floor should just be complete chaos where the players can experience everything they have unlocked, fight their way through the floor, get to safes with very good weapons in them if they want to or just fight the guards with the weapons they already have."
- Q14 "The boss himself on the thirteenth floor will not really be a fight per se. The boss will not really fight back and will just talk smack to the players."

**E. Other asks**
- Q15 Zoom: "mouse wheel scroll up to zoom in to first person and ... scroll down to zoom back out to third person or the player should be able to use the CTRL button plus the up arrow key for zoom in and CTRL plus the down arrow key for zoom out for those without a mouse."
- Q16 "Please make sure any item that is meant to be carried by two people spawn in an open area where two players can access the object at the same time without bugs."
- Q17 Darts: "a darts game with the darts board in the basement, let players choose between the dart game modes 301 and Cricket"; amended: "in the Loading Docks (basement) should be in the break room and should use the dart board asset from Synty".
- Q18 Two more guns: the Deagle (9th floor) and the rocket launcher (11th). Q: the Synty pack's heavy pistol - the model the pew already is - looks like a Deagle; which model is the Deagle (the pack also has two revolvers), and does the pew keep its model?

**What this changed in the weapons round that was already under way** (the owner's "Yes, add the SMG, rifle, shotgun and sniper guns"): the guns are found, not sold - the unlock floors are SMG 5, shotgun 6, assault rifle 8, sniper 10 (Q5) - the dealer sells their AMMUNITION after they have been found (Q7, Q10), a found gun holds one magazine (Q9), a player holds at most three clips (Q8), and the safes (until now "pistols only" because the pew was the only gun) hold the gun of their floor. The dealer's sale of the pew after floor 3 stays as the owner set it. Status of each Q: see the State paragraph of this section when it is written (nothing is built when this paragraph is written).

### 2.29 Round 9 (2026-10-19): the SMG, the rifle, the shotgun and the sniper rifle - `Playtest_2026-10-19b` is the build to send

**What the owner asked:** "Yes, add the SMG, rifle, shotgun and sniper guns from the Synty pack, each with its own spec, hold pose and shop/loot entry." (the answer to my question after update 8) and, in the same hours, the queue of 2.28 whose parts about the guns I built in at once: the ladder of unlocks (SMG 5, shotgun 6, assault rifle 8, sniper rifle 10), "The Vendor should sell ammo for the specific guns after finding them, ammo from the Vendor should be expensive", "A player should be allowed to have three gun clips max", "Guns found should have one clip in them", "Different guns take different ammo".

**1. The four guns** (`RangedWeapon.Kind { Pew, Smg, Rifle, Shotgun, Sniper }`; every number is in `RangedWeapon.Specs`, placeholders for the owner to tune). The models were chosen from a side-view sheet of all 13 candidate guns of the pack (`ui.ps1 -Methods GunSheet`): the SMG is `SM_Wep_SubMGun_Lite_01` (the other SMG, a P90, has no barrel outside its body for a silencer), the rifle `SM_Wep_Rifle_Assault_01`, the shotgun `SM_Wep_Shotgun_01`, the sniper rifle `SM_Wep_Sniper_Mil_01` (a bolt-action with a scope).

| | Pew | SMG | Rifle | Shotgun | Sniper |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| length (m) / weight (kg) | 0.30 / 1.2 | 0.50 / 1.9 | 0.90 / 3.4 | 1.00 / 3.6 | 1.15 / 4.8 |
| damage on the dummy / hits of a guard's ten | 60 / 4 | 24 / 2 | 42 / 4 | 14 each / 1 each | 150 / 10 |
| stun, knock | 8 s (as before), 16 | none, 4 | none, 8 | none, 2 each | none, 20 |
| magazine (+ spare clips of a bought gun) | 8 (+2) | 24 (+2) | 30 (+2) | 6 (+2) | 5 (+2) |
| fire interval (s), reload (s) | 0.30, 2.2 | 0.085, 2.0 | 0.12, 2.6 | 0.85, 3.0 | 1.35, 3.4 |
| range (m), spread (degrees) | 45, 1.2 | 38, 3.4 | 70, 1.6 | 24, 5.5 (x 8 pellets) | 120, 0.25 |
| heard from (m) loud / silenced | 45 / 12 | 40 / 11 | 55 / 14 | 60 / never silenced | 85 / never silenced |
| unlocked on floor | 3 | 5 | 8 | 6 | 10 |
| dealer's clip (rounds) | $120 (8) | $480 (24) | $750 (30) | $420 (6) | $600 (5) |

The silencer rule is the owner's, by weapon class: the SMG and the rifle wear one (the same Synty silencer, on the barrel's end, an extension of it, the bullet leaving from its front face), the shotgun and the sniper rifle never. The way a gun sits is stored in `WeaponCatalog.longGuns[]` (`GunModel`: prefab, turn, muzzle face, the right hand's grip behind the trigger, the left hand's foregrip, silencer seat, the case it throws, its magazine part) and measured by the editor (`WeaponCatalogBuilder.LongGunsBatch` / `FillLongGuns`; the same on every editor start with the entries missing). `RangedWeapon.LayoutOf` / `Create` / `BuildHeld` / `CreateClipModel` work per kind now; the pew's fields in the catalog are untouched.

**2. The hold.** A pistol is held as before (the right arm straight along the aim). A long gun is held in both hands (`HeldGun`): the right hand on the grip near the shoulder with the elbow bent, the left on the foregrip, both arms solved by a two-bone solver (`SolveArm`, the elbows down and out), the gun scaled to the holder's arm (`BodyScale`: arm length over 0.585 m, the Synty office people's, times 0.88) and `FitTwoHand` places it - as far forward and to the right of the chest's middle as the arms allow, smaller until the left hand reaches. The Synty office characters and the Party characters hold every long gun in both hands (pictures `be_shot_gunlook_*`); the Tiny Toon people cannot: their upper arms start 0.35 m from the middle of the chest and are 0.35 m long, so a hand cannot reach across the chest and the fit fails - they hold a long gun in one hand, straight out, scaled to their arm (a toy-sized rifle on a chibi person).

**3. Firing.** `GunController.Shoot` fires `Spec.Pellets` pellets, each with its own spread from the shot's seed (`GunRules.PelletSeed`); the hit sound plays once per shot; flash, case, sound, pitch and kick are the gun's own. The shot over the network now carries its seed: `RequestGunShot(muzzle, aim, guardId, seed)`, the host traces every pellet itself (`HostPellet`) and announces the first pellet's end, the best result and the seed (`PlayerShotRpc`), so a teammate's machine draws the other pellets' tracers from the same seed; fast guns are allowed their burst of bunched messages (`GunBurstSlack` 0.15 s, the average rate is still the gun's). The sounds are the Free Weapon SFX pack's Handgun folder (the pew and the SMG), Assault Rifle folder (the rifle and the sniper rifle, deeper) and Shotgun folder (`AudioCatalog.rifle*` / `shotgun*`; reload sounds 5 / 4 / 5 steps, `GunSounds.ReloadTimes`). `GunFx` keeps a pool of cases for each kind of case.

**4. Ammunition (the owner's rules).** A clip is for one kind of gun (`AmmoClip.kind`, `Vendor.GunOf(pack, kind)`). A player carries three clips of a gun at most, the one in the gun counted (`GunRules.MaxClips`, `GunMagazine.MaxRounds` / `Room`): the dealer refuses a fourth and takes no money, a spare clip in a safe stays where it lies. A gun FOUND in a safe holds its one clip and no spare (the new guns; the pew keeps its two for now, see the queue); a gun bought or handed over by the F1 menu comes with two spares. The dealer sells the ammunition of a gun once it has been found and carried back to the Loading Docks (`RunUnlocks`: a player in the docks with the gun in his pack unlocks it for the party, host decides, `HeistManager.TickUnlocks`, mirrored to the clients in `RunSync.State`, cleared when a run starts; the F1 menu's gun buttons unlock too). The guns themselves are not sold (only the pew, after floor 3, as before). The dealer's list is thirteen things now (`Vendor.OnOffer` says which are on offer).

**5. The safes hold the gun of their floor.** `SafeRules.WeaponContents` (pure): floors 3 and 4 the pew only (as before), then each gun from its unlock floor on by weight (`GunWeights`; three times the weight on its own floor), and the FIRST safe of a floor that unlocks a gun always holds that gun (its code is always on the computer). The spare clip in a safe is for the safe's gun. A long gun is longer than the hollow is deep: it lies slanted across it with its barrel out through the open door and never moves (kinematic); the crosshair aims at its middle (`Pickup.aimBox`) because the world box round a slanted gun covers the whole hollow. Pictures: `be_shot_gunsafes_*`.

**Words I changed (the owner writes the game text: reword freely):** the dealer's four new cards ("SMG Magazine", "Rifle Magazine", "Shotgun Shells", "Sniper Rounds" with "24 more rounds for your SMG" and so on); the messages "You can't carry more than three clips", "You carry no <gun> to load", "You already have a gun" (was "...a pew"), "You have no <gun> for this clip", "+N rounds for your <gun>" (the pew's clip texts are as they were); "Out of ammo - the dealer in the Loading Docks sells <the gun's ammunition>" (the pew's is the same sentence as before); the gun names "SMG", "Rifle", "Shotgun", "Sniper" on the HUD and in the prompts; the safes' clips are called after their ammunition.

**Calls I made (change any):** (a) the numbers in the table (placeholders); (b) the guns are FOUND, not sold, and the dealer sells their ammunition - the owner's words about the ladder say "unlocks" and "ammo", say if the dealer should sell the guns too; (c) a gun is unlocked when a player carries it into the docks (the owner's words about the armour: "if the player who found it makes it back to the Loading Docks"); (d) the pew keeps its two spare clips and its $120 clip until the rest of the ammunition rules (guards that drop bullets) are built, but the three-clip limit applies to it already; (e) a bullet of the new guns does not knock a guard over (stun 0), the pew still does (8 s) until the combat model of the queue (bullets eliminate) is built; (f) the toon people hold long guns in one hand.

**Tests.** Pure 539 (+29: `GunsTest` - the specs, the silencer rule for all five, the ammunition and its three-clip limit, the pellets' seeds, the unlocks, what the dealer has on offer, which gun a safe holds on which floor). Play Mode: new scenario `Guns` (86 checks: each gun in the hand from the F1 menu, its silencer / foregrip / magazine, shots on the dummy - damage, rate, pellets, flash, case, noise -, the reload with its own sounds, the unlock in the docks, the dealer's purchases and refusals, a clip fitting its gun only); `Outpost` 110 (+1: a clip is refused at three clips), floor 7's safes hold slanted long guns; `Demo` 40. Pictures: `GunSheet` (the candidates), `GunItems` (the five lying, the silencers end-on), `GunLook` (every gun in the hands of a toon person, a Synty person and a Party character), `GunSafes` (every gun in an open safe), `GunUi` (the dealer's window, the HUD). The built-player autopilot also fires the client's pistol over the loopback and checks that the host accepts and shows the shot.
**Not tested:** two real machines; a shotgun's pellets over the network (no built player can reach a floor that holds one: the demo ends after floor 4 - the pellets' seeds and the host's trace are the same code as the pistol's single bullet, which the autopilot does fire over the loopback); the new guns in a built player (the F1 menu is not in it); the hold on the other 20 characters of the catalog (three kinds of rig were looked at).

**State (update 9).** The real project = the QA tree `C:\bqm` (identical by `ship.ps1`: the first ship applied it, the second applied the one line of the autopilot's test that the three-clip limit had outdated). Build `Builds\Playtest_2026-10-19b` (+ .zip, 47 MB; raw `Final21_2026-10-19`, 154 MB; protocol "21"). `Playtest_2026-10-19a` is superseded (the same game, but its built-player autopilot still expected the old unlimited clips: 2 of its checks failed; nothing else differs). Built-player autopilot on the unzipped copy of b: solo 85, host 24, client 29 PASS / 0 FAIL, no errors logged - and the client fires its pistol over the loopback: the host accepts the shot (it carries its seed) and shows it from the pistol in his hand. Numbers on the tree of build b: pure 539; Offline done; Demo 40, PewLoot 28, Pew 83, Guns 86, Shaft 93, Floors 56 pictures (0 magenta), Doors 76, Outpost 110, Sneak 46, R7 96, LootQa 301, FloatQa 4, Looks 30, Etr 30, R5 36, R6 22 - all 0 FAIL, 0 exceptions (Doors' summary line was recomputed from its log: my own polling had hidden it). Backups of replaced files: `DevTools\QaBackups\before_round9a_apply_2026-10-19` and `before_round9b_apply_2026-10-19` (code), `before_2_28_queue_notes_2026-10-19` and `before_2_29_notes_2026-10-19` (these notes), `before_2_29_scripts_2026-10-19` (the old README_PLAYTEST copy). The patch scripts of the round are in `DevTools\QaScripts\round9_patches` (r9_g01 .. r9_g17; read r9_g08b with g08: g08's own insertion of the GunLook scenario did not take). README_PLAYTEST.txt (item 9: the three-clip limit) and its copy in `DevTools\QaScripts` are current. **Next (the owner's queue, 2.28, in this order):** the combat model - bullets eliminate by health (a bullet's damage per gun, melee knocks over for a tier time, a shot guard lets go of his pin), shields light / medium / heavy, armed and unarmed guards by floor (the toon people the unarmed ones), guards that drop bullets, shotgun and sniper guards that end a player's floor with an explosion; then armour, the Deagle and the rocket launcher (which model is the Deagle: ask), floors 12 and 13, the zoom, two-person items, darts.

**Starting points for the combat model (read before coding; found 2026-10-19 while checking the dealer card, nothing changed yet):** a guard's life is the count `GuardAI.Hits` against `hitsToKill` (ten for a normal guard). `GuardAI.WeaponHitHost` (GuardAI.cs line 232) adds the weapon's weight - a melee blow is 1, a bullet is the gun's `Spec.guardHits` (pew 4, SMG 2, rifle 4, shotgun 1 per pellet, sniper 10), halved for a bullet on an `Armoured` guard (`GuardRules.ArmouredHits`: the vest rule the shields of the queue replace) - and then either leaves the guard down for good (`Dead`) or stuns him for the weapon's `stun` seconds (`Stun`). The pew's stun is 8 s, the other guns' 0: the pew still knocks a guard over, the new guns do not. Every weapon hit already calls `ReleaseHold(true)`, which frees a player the guard is pinning (a bullet too) - check that it also stops the pinning animation on both machines. A punch (`PunchHost`) stuns without counting. The dealer card "Pew Pistol: ... 3 hits drop a guard" is right under today's numbers (4 + 4 + 4 of ten). A bullet reaches a guard in `GunController.cs` line 300 (offline) and `RunSync.Guns.cs` line 83 (host, for a client's shot). In the dealer window the descriptions of the Pew Pistol and Pistol Clip cards run one line past their cards (the last line is clipped: seen in `be_ui_gunui_shop.png`).

### 2.30 Round 10 (2026-10-19): the combat model - `Playtest_2026-10-19d` is the build to send

**What the owner asked** (Q1-Q6 and Q9 of 2.28, answered "Yes, start the combat model now"): bullets damage a guard by the bullet and never knock him over; a shot guard lets go of the player he pins; melee knocks a guard over for the weapon's tier and does not eliminate; unarmed and armed guards by floor (3 pistols + light shield, 5 SMG + light, 6 shotgun + heavy, 8 assault rifle + medium, 10 sniper no shield); shotgun and sniper guards end a player's floor with an explosion; guards drop bullets; a found gun holds one clip.

**What I decided (my readings - the "Calls I made" of the report):**
1. "Shield" = a tier of body armour for the guard (none / light / medium / heavy): the share of a bullet's damage that gets through is 1 / 0.75 / 0.5 (the 2026-10-18 vest, which halved bullets) / 0.3 (`GuardLoadouts.BulletShare`); it does nothing against melee. Not a riot shield in his hand (the pack has `SM_Wep_RiotShield_01` if that was meant). The picture is the vest in three looks (`GuardArmour.AddVest(model, root, tier)`).
2. A guard's health is `hitsToKill` (10, boss 30) as damage in `GuardAI.Damage`; a bullet does the gun's `Spec.guardHits` (pew 4, SMG 2, rifle 4, shotgun 1 a pellet, sniper 10) times the shield's share, never stuns (`GuardAI.BulletHitHost`; a guard not busy with a player walks to where it came from); a melee blow is `GuardAI.MeleeHit(Host)`: stun only (bat 3 s, crowbar 4 s, hatchet 6 s), counts for nothing (the boss: 1 of 30 until floor 13 is redone). Both cut his radio call and `ReleaseHold(true)` frees a player he pins. `WeaponHitHost(stun, by, weight)` stays as the legacy "stun + count N hits" the old tests use.
3. Who carries what (`GuardLoadouts`, pure): armed share of a floor's patrolling guards 25 % on floor 3, 33 % on 4, 40 % on 5-6, 45 % on 7, 50 % from 8 (my numbers); floors 3-4 pistols; floor 5 half SMG half pistol; from floor 6 one shotgun guard (two from 8); from floor 8 the rest all assault rifles (no pistols or SMGs); from floor 10 exactly two snipers (my reading of the garbled line: rifles, a few shotguns, two snipers). Shield by gun: pistol and SMG light, rifle medium, shotgun heavy, sniper none; unarmed guards wear none. Backup and the guard on duty roll by the same shares (`GuardLoadouts.Roll`, never a sniper). The plan uses its own `System.Random` (seed of the floor), so the maze and the loot do not change. The Deagle (floor 9) is not in it yet.
4. Guard guns: `GuardLoadouts.Tuning(kind)` (engage range, aim, rate, burst, reload, magazine, spread, stumble): the SMG and the rifle fire bursts, the shotgun (8 pellets) and the sniper take 0.9 / 1.6 s to aim. `GuardGun.Configure(kind)`.
5. A hit from a shotgun or sniper guard (`GuardLoadouts.Eliminates`) is `HeistManager.Blast`: the player is Busted at once (the existing "knocked out for the floor": they spectate, the vendor buys them back), the body is thrown clear, `BlastFx` (fireball, smoke, pieces in the player's colour, light, boom from the shotgun and rifle recordings) plays on every machine (`RunSync.BroadcastBlast`). I could not check what R.E.P.O. does exactly: this is a cartoon blast.
6. An armed guard who goes down drops one clip of his gun's ammunition (`GuardDrops`, `RunSync.BroadcastSpawnClip`); the clip is the same `AmmoClip` as a safe's. The pew found in a safe now holds one clip and no spare, like the other guns.
7. Network: the snapshot's `kind` byte (never read before) carries the guard's loadout (`GuardLoadout.Pack`); protocol "22".

**Tests.** Pure 562 (+23: `GuardsTest` - the shields, the bullets it takes through each shield, who is armed on each floor (the counts, the plan, the roll), the loadout byte, how each gun is handled, which guns end a floor, the drop). Play Mode: the new scenario `Combat` (47 checks, `qa_regress.ps1 -Methods Combat:gfx`; pictures `be_shot_combat_guard_*`, `be_shot_combat_blast_*`: the loadouts of floors 3 / 4 / 5 / 6 / 8 / 10, the vest of each tier, the bullets through each shield, no knock-over by a bullet, a shot guard letting go of a pinned player, melee for 3 / 4 / 6 s and never a knock-out, the dropped clip and taking it, a rifle and an SMG guard only knocking the player down, a shotgun and a sniper guard ending his floor with a blast); the whole suite on the final tree: Offline, Demo 40, PewLoot 34, Pew 83, Guns 86, Shaft 93, Floors 56 pictures, Doors 76, Outpost 111, Sneak 46, R7 97, LootQa 301, FloatQa 4, Looks 30, Etr 30, R5 36, R6 22 - all 0 FAIL, 0 exceptions. Built player (the zip of `d`, unzipped): autopilot solo 85, host 27, client 31 PASS, 0 FAIL; new over the loopback: the client sees the host's armed guards with the pistol, the light shield and the vest; the host puts an armed guard down and the clip he drops appears on the client with the host's network id, the client takes it and the host finds it by that id; a shotgun's hit played by the host on the client's body busts him on both machines and the blast plays on both.

**Words I changed (the owner writes the game text: reword freely):** the banner when somebody is blown up ("Player 2 was blown up - buy them back from the vendor at the Loading Docks", "You were blown up - ..."); `README_PLAYTEST.txt`: item 9 ("three hits drop an officer with no shield, four one with a light shield"), item 12 (the sentence about the floor-4 body armour is gone), the new item 17 (officers and guns); the F1 test menu (the row "Every guard on this floor carries ...: nothing / Pew / SMG / Rifle / Shotgun / Sniper", the button that shows the blast, "Guards on this floor: N, armed: M"). Not changed: the dealer's Pew card still says "3 hits drop a guard" (true for a guard with no shield; it is four through a light one).

**Calls I made (change any):** (a) "shield" is a tier of body armour (light lets 75 % of a bullet through, medium 50 % - the old vest -, heavy 30 %), not a riot shield in the hand (the pack has `SM_Wep_RiotShield_01`); (b) a bullet damages and never knocks over (the pew's 8 s stun is gone, `Spec.stun` is 0 for every gun) and melee knocks over and never knocks out (the boss: one hit of thirty, until his floor is redone); (c) the armed share of a floor's guards and the counts by floor are placeholders (25 % on floor 3 up to 67 % on floor 12, twelve guards at most; one shotgun on floors 6-7, two from 8; from floor 10 exactly two snipers - my reading of the garbled line); (d) how a guard handles each gun (`GuardLoadouts.Tuning`: bursts, ranges, aim times) is mine; (e) "end for the current floor" is the existing busted state (they spectate, the vendor buys them back) with a cartoon blast - I could not check what R.E.P.O. does; (f) an armed guard drops one clip of his gun's ammunition; (g) a gun found in a safe holds one clip and no spare, the pew too (the pew from the menu or the dealer keeps two spares); (h) armed and unarmed guards look the same (the Synty security people, role Guard in the catalog); (i) floor 3 is the first armed floor, the floor where the pew is found.

**Not tested:** two real machines (the loopback autopilot is the network check); floors 5 and above in a build (the demo ends after floor 4: the SMG, shotgun, rifle and sniper guards were only seen in the Editor); a real shotgun or sniper guard's hit over the network (the host's `Blast` call was played on the client's body instead); the vests on other characters; the balance of any number.

**Open questions for the owner:** whether armed guards should look different from unarmed ones; whether "shield" meant a riot shield. **Answered while round 11 was being built (2026-10-19):** the sniper floor - "For floor 10 two snipers with no shield as well as some assault rifle guards and some shotgun guards" - is what is built (two snipers, three rifles, two shotguns); the Deagle - the owner sent a picture of a Desert Eagle-type pistol: the only model like it in the project is `SM_Wep_Pistol_Heavy_01`, which the pew already uses, so the Deagle (floor 9: heavy shield, armour-piercing ammunition, never silenced) will be that model, bigger and without the silencer the pew wears (my reading: say if it is another model); and, said again in the next message, "No silencer for the Deagle please" (so the Deagle's weapon class must be one that cannot take a silencer, like the shotgun and the sniper rifle).

**State (update 10, 2026-10-19):** shipped as `Builds\Playtest_2026-10-19d` (zip 47 MB; raw `Final23_2026-10-19`; protocol "22": everybody must run this build). `Playtest_2026-10-19c` (the same game without the network test of the clip drop and the blast) is superseded. The real project and the QA tree `C:\bqm` are identical (ship.ps1 cmp). Backups of replaced files: `DevTools\QaBackups\before_round10_apply_2026-10-19` and `before_round10d_apply_2026-10-19` (code), `before_2_30_notes_2026-10-19` (these notes), `before_2_30_scripts_2026-10-19` (the README copies). The patch scripts of the round are in `DevTools\QaScripts\round10_patches` (r10_g01 .. g10, r10_readme; the drafts in `drafts`; read g05 after g01-g04: it re-applies two drafts). README_PLAYTEST.txt and its copy in `DevTools\QaScripts` are current. **Next (the owner's queue, 2.28):** armour found in safes on floors 3 / 7 / 10 and bought from the dealer by the whole party once its finder is back in the docks; the Deagle (floor 9) and the rocket launcher (floor 11); floor 12 (chaos) and floor 13 (a boss who only talks); the zoom; two-person items in the open; darts in the break room.

### 2.31 Round 11 (2026-10-19): armour, vertical safes, lucky safes, the sniper in the outposts, a send build that is safe to send, the Loading Docks in the lobby's look, the vendor's black market and wall of guns, the journal's checklist and the scan's glow - `Playtest_2026-10-19g` is the build to send (`e` and `f` are superseded: do not send them)

**What the owner asked** (in the order it came): "Yes, start on the armour: found in the safes on floors 3, 7 and 10, and the whole party can buy it from the Vendor once the finder is back in the Loading Docks" (Q12 of 2.28); "The safes for snipers and shotguns should be vertical with the gun vertical inside the safe"; "The sniper and rocket launcher should be inside the outpost not in safes"; "Safes should not always have a gun, it should be lucky to get a gun and even luckier to get the specific one you wanted"; (also "For floor 10 two snipers with no shield ..." = round 10's plan; "The Deagle is this model" with a picture; "No silencer for the Deagle please"); then, while the build was being made: "The build folder should be completely safe to send to play-testers. They should not be able to access anything integral to the game or its undeveloped features yet"; "Loading Docks should look like the lobby Loading Docks" - made clear as "the assets from the lobby scene look way cleaner ... the layout is great ... use the lobby as a reference, for example the clock is great"; "the Vendor is a black market shop he shouldn't have boxes he should have all cool gadgets and weapons and stuff in his truck", "The briefcases in the Vendor's shop were a nice touch as well", "On the wall behind the Vendor should be guns", "The guns should only appear as you unlock them though since he will then sell those specific guns", "The briefcases should be on the shelves and flat not standing"; "On the journal should be a tab that has check boxes and a list: All valuables collected / All unique items collected / All safes opened / All cash collected ... checked (green check) ... crossed out (green)"; "The scan should make safes glow red, unique items purple (for example a weapon), cash green, valuables gold".

**Built (all in the QA tree `C:\bqm`, applied to the real project by `ship.ps1`; patch scripts `r11_g01 .. g17`, `r11_g16b`, `r11_readme*.ps1`, `patchlib2.ps1`, `r11_draft` in `DevTools\QaScripts\round11_patches`):**
1. *Armour* (g01-g05, build e): three tiers = the guards' shield tiers (`ArmourRules`, `ArmourItem`; weights 2.5 / 5 / 8 kg, prices $600 / 1,400 / 2,800 - placeholders); a safe may hold a piece instead of a gun (item numbers 21-23, no spare clip with armour); worn while it is in the pack, one piece at a time, a vest on the character (`PlayerArmourVisual`); a guard's bullet knocks the wearer down for 75 / 50 / 30 % of the time (my placeholder: the owner has not said what armour does; a shotgun's or a sniper's blast is not stopped); `RunUnlocks` bits 9-11: a tier carried back to the docks is unlocked for the party; the dealer's list has three more entries (16 in all); F1 menu buttons.
2. *Vertical safes* (g06): `FloorSafe.Build(..., tall)` 1.7 m high on the same footprint for a safe that holds a shotgun (`SafeRules.HoldsTallGun`), the gun standing upright.
3. *Lucky safes* (g07/g08): a weapon safe holds armour first (`HoldsArmour`, as before: 50 % on floors 3 / 7 / 10, 25 % on the others from floor 3 - and now the FIRST safe of a floor too, since it is no longer sure to hold a gun), else a gun `GunPercent` = 40 % of the time (`HoldsGun`), else no weapon (`WeaponContents` returns an empty array; its cash, spare pistol clip and gold watch still roll on their own). `PickGun` rolls by `GunWeights` whatever the safe; the gun that unlocks on a floor is no longer sure to be in the first safe nor likelier there (`UnlockFloorBoost` 1, it was 3); the sniper rifle's weight is 0.
4. *The sniper rifle lies in the outposts* (g07): `OutpostLoot.SniperKind` (7) / `SniperFloor` (10): from floor 10 on every outpost has one, first in its weapon list, lying on the briefing table in the open (`RangedWeapon.Create(Sniper, ...)`, one clip, no spare) - not in a gun safe (the owner said "not in safes"; the pew is still hacked out of the floor-3 gun safe). Carried back to the docks it unlocks the sniper's ammunition. The rocket launcher does not exist yet: it will lie in the outposts of floor 11 the same way.
5. *A send build that is safe to send* (g09): the QA autopilot only starts in the Editor or in a copy with the marker file `be_qa.flag` next to the exe (`hd.ps1` writes it beside any exe it starts with `-be-autopilot`; a playtester starting the exe with the switch gets the normal game); `final_build.ps1` now scans the send copy before it zips it (only `EmployeesTakeRevenge.exe`, `_Data`, `MonoBleedingEdge`, `D3D12`, `UnityCrashHandler64.exe`, `UnityPlayer.dll`, `README.txt`, `steam_appid.txt`; no `*DoNotShip*`, `.pdb`, `.cs`, `.md`, `.ps1`, `.meta`, `.log`, source or raw assets, `be_qa.flag`; none of `phick`, `OneDrive`, `C:\Users`, `BuildingEscape\Assets`, `DEVELOPER_NOTES`, `Supabase` inside any file; a README with nothing about floors above 4, the SMG, shotgun, rifle, sniper, medium or heavy armour) and stops without a zip when anything is found. The README that goes in the build is now `README_SEND.txt` (the demo's content only); `README_PLAYTEST.txt` stays our own list of changes. The F1 test menu was never in a player (`TestMode` needs `Application.isEditor`). What stays true of any Unity build: the game's C# is in `Managed\Assembly-CSharp.dll` (Mono backend) and can be decompiled, the models and sounds can be extracted - IL2CPP would make the code harder to read (the editor has the module and Visual Studio is installed; it needs a full retest and a slower build): ask the owner.
6. *The Loading Docks in the lobby's clean look* (g10-g13, g15, g16): `Scripts/DockDressing.cs` builds the lobby dock's props (`LobbyDock`'s helpers are `internal` now) in the basement's dock; the layout is unchanged (the same clear zones, the same walls and doors): steel shelving, cardboard stacks and pallet blocks square to the room and sitting on the floor, up to two forklifts, drums, yellow bollards, steel door frames and roller boxes, a dark band along the foot of the bay wall, a wall clock that tells the real time, pipes under the ceiling, a notice board and an extinguisher on the east wall, two hanging lamps and a turning beacon (`LobbyDockLife`), the glowing strip in the trailer; each prop has ONE box collider on its root (`Solidify`). The old prop-pack cabinets, boxes, pallets and chairs are gone from the hall; `BoxBlock` (also the PVP room's boxes) builds the clean blocks.
7. *The vendor's truck - a black market* (g12, g13, g16): no cardboard: four steel shelf units (`StockShelf`) with night-vision goggles (glowing green), spy cameras, tool cases, a radio, a torch, a drone, a stack of cash and gold bars on the two lower boards and the demo's melee weapons (crowbar, bat, hatchet: their real models) or more gadgets on the top board; the counter has a dark steel body, a wooden top and a yellow stripe (its own box stays, hidden, for the collision), the sign a dark frame, a lamp hangs over it, a rubber mat lies in front; the briefcases (the prop pack's) lie FLAT on the boards (`PlaceBriefcaseFlat` turns the standing model onto its broad side by its measured box); on the wall behind him a peg board with a pair of brackets for each of the five guns (`DockDressing.GunWall`, `VendorGunWall`): a gun hangs there only once it is unlocked in this run (`RunUnlocks.Has`) or, the pew, once the dealer sells it (`Vendor.PewForSale`); looked at twice a second, the same on every machine (the unlocks are mirrored). In the demo only the pew can ever hang there.
8. *The journal's checklist* (g14): the journal (J) has two tabs, SAFE NOTES and CHECKLIST; the checklist lists "All valuables collected", "All unique items collected", "All safes opened" and "All cash collected" with a check box after each text; when none of a kind is left on the floor the box gets a green tick, the text goes green and a green line crosses it out (`FloorChecklist.cs`: `ChecklistRules`, `ChecklistState` - counts after a 1.5 s settle time, a line stays done, a floor that never had a kind has it done at once -, `FloorChecklist.Remaining` - what lies about, what is inside a shut safe, the safe itself -, `FloorChecklistTracker` - counts twice a second, local, the same on every machine). Nothing is tracked at the docks (the lines stay empty).
9. *The scan's glow* (g14): `LootScanner` now also marks the shut safes of the room and the weapons and armour lying about (unique items), and draws a soft glow behind each mark in its kind's colour: safes red, unique items purple, cash green, valuables gold (`ChecklistRules.Rgb`); a safe that is open no longer glows. Status line: "SCANNING ROOM 114   N valuables here, about $X + M items" / "M items here" / the old "nothing to steal here"; after the pulse "N things marked".

### 2.32 IL2CPP trial (2026-10-19): `Playtest_2026-10-19i` is `g` built with IL2CPP - it works; send it when the code should be hard to read

**What the owner asked:** "Yes, try an IL2CPP build for the send copy: check it builds on this machine, retest it fully, and tell me the build time and anything that breaks."

**It builds here, with nothing to install:** Unity 6000.0.45f1 has the Windows IL2CPP module; Visual Studio 2026 Community ("18", MSVC 14.51.36231) and the Windows SDK 10.0.26100.0 are installed and Unity accepted them. The switch is `ship.ps1 ... -Backend il2cpp` -> `final_build.ps1 -Backend` -> `DemoBuild -buildBackend il2cpp` (patch `r11_g19.ps1`; Mono stays the default, and the pristine build copy's ProjectSettings are synced again from the real project every time, so the setting never reaches the real project). Build time: the first, cold IL2CPP build took 122 s inside Unity (about 2.5 min for the whole build step); the same build again with nothing changed 21 s; the Mono build 33 s. Sizes: the raw output is 1,105 MB (942 MB of it is `EmployeesTakeRevenge_BackUpThisFolder_ButDontShipItWithYourGame`, the generated C++ for debugging: `final_build.ps1` leaves it, and the Burst folder, out of the send copy), the send copy 163 MB (Mono 155), the zip 48 MB (47).

**What the IL2CPP send copy holds:** `EmployeesTakeRevenge.exe`, `GameAssembly.dll` (38 MB: the game's code as native code), `UnityPlayer.dll`, `baselib.dll`, `UnityCrashHandler64.exe`, `D3D12`, `_Data` with `il2cpp_data\Metadata\global-metadata.dat` (8 MB), `README.txt`, `steam_appid.txt`. No `Managed` folder, no `Assembly-CSharp.dll` (the Mono build has it as a plain 1.2 MB .NET assembly any decompiler reads), no `MonoBleedingEdge`. What stays readable: the class and method NAMES (`global-metadata.dat` holds `TestMode`, `QaAutopilot`, `GuardLoadouts` ...; tools like Il2CppDumper recover names and signatures, not the code), and the models, textures and sounds (extractable from any Unity build). The safety scan's allow-list got `GameAssembly.dll` and `baselib.dll` (the first attempt stopped at `baselib.dll`: the scan did its job; it left `Builds\Final27_2026-10-19` and `Builds\Playtest_2026-10-19h` behind - 1.3 GB, the owner may delete them); the second attempt scanned clean (0 problems), no `be_qa.flag`, no developer path anywhere.

**Retest of the built player (the unzipped send copy, `final_autopilot.ps1`; the Editor suites are Mono and unchanged):** run 1: solo 85 PASS; host 12 PASS / 1 FAIL and client 15 / 1 FAIL - both "timeout waiting for the client's code unlock" in the outpost-lock step on floor 1 (the client's UNLOCK by code never took effect on the host); runs 2 and 3: solo 85, host 31, client 36 PASS, 0 FAIL, no errors logged. The Mono build `g` in the same hours: three runs of three clean (85 / 31 / 36). No exception, no stripped-code error, no magenta picture in any IL2CPP log. The one failure is probably the autopilot, not IL2CPP: the same "Guard_Outpost spotted something: watching for 2 s" line is in the Mono run's log too, and in the failing run the duty guard of floor 1's outpost went on to chase the client who stands inside the room ("lost the player and gave up the chase"): the host ignores a lock request from a player who is held or not inside the room. A three-line fix is NOT applied: blind the guards (sightRange 0.1, hearingMultiplier 0) at the start of `QaAutopilot.OutpostLockNet`, as `SafesNet` does.

**Not tested:** the real Steam transport under IL2CPP (the loopback test does not use it), a person playing the IL2CPP build, a comparison of frame rates (the autopilot's reports `C:\bqs\fl7_ap_*` have the perf lines).

**State:** `Builds\Playtest_2026-10-19i.zip` (48 MB, raw `Final28_2026-10-19`) is the IL2CPP send build - the same game, README and scan as `g`; `g` stays as the Mono fallback. Backups: `DevTools\QaBackups\before_round11h_apply_2026-10-19` and `before_round11i_apply_2026-10-19` (DemoBuild.cs), `before_2_32_notes_2026-10-19`. `ship.ps1`, `final_build.ps1` and `r11_g19.ps1` in `DevTools\QaScripts` are current.

**Traps found this round (worth keeping):** (a) `patchlib.ps1` keeps the source text in `$script:s`: PowerShell variables are case-insensitive, so a patch script's own `$S` is overwritten by the first `Open-Src` (use another name, `$SC`); (b) `FloorGenerator.FindSun()` (the night look) takes the FIRST DIRECTIONAL LIGHT in the scene when `RenderSettings.sun` is not assigned (the Greybox scene's is not): a second directional light (a "moon" for the buildings) can be taken for the sun, so none was added; (c) a `while (!condition) seed++` loop in a scenario runs for ever when a rule change makes the condition impossible (the Outpost scenario on floor 3); (d) `NetDevCombat.CombatDuel` picks its spot before a 0.7 s wait during which a patrolling guard walks on: re-check the spot after waiting; (e) `Renderer.localBounds` and `Mesh.bounds` work on imported (non-readable) meshes in the Editor; a runtime mesh vertex read does not: the city places prefabs one by one (about 70 draw calls for the near ring), no combining; (f) `PlaceKey` puts the FOOTPRINT centre at (x, z) and the model's bottom at y: a wall prop's x is the wall's face minus half its depth.

**What was built (patches `C:\bqs\r12_g26.ps1` .. `r12_g34.ps1`, archived in `DevTools\QaScripts\round12_patches`; the new file `Editor\NetDev\NetDevDockSeeds.cs` is made by G30).**
- G26 `FloorGenerator.Dock.cs`: `BuildDartBoard` hangs the board on the WEST face of the wall stub between the two halves of the docks (x 17.8, z = `BreakRoomDoor.z` 2.4, middle 1.7 m up, facing west); the tape (x 15.38) and the scoreboard go with it. `ScatterStorage` has a `floorClear` rectangle (the break room's doorway x 9.5 .. 17.8, z 0 .. 7) that every NON-wall item must keep out of; shelves against a wall skip it ("leave the shelves against the wall"). The old east-wall dart-lane rectangle is gone. G28 added a keep-clear rectangle for the wall stub itself (x 17.8 .. 18.2, z 0 .. 7: a box stack had stood half inside it).
- The stance (G26): `DartBoard.Begin` calls `LockStance()` (unless already locked, so R keeps the place): the local player is placed (`NetPlayer.PlaceAt`, or `WobblyPlayer.TeleportTo` offline) at the middle of the tape, `DartsRules.StandBehind` 0.3 m behind it, facing the board; `WobblyPlayer.inputLocked` zeroes the move keys, sprint, crouch and jump; `DartBoard.FixedUpdate` slides the body along the tape with A / D (`DartsRules.Lateral`: +-0.45 m at 1.6 m/s, `Rigidbody.MovePosition`); `CameraFollow.LimitAim(centreYaw, 25 degrees, pitch -8 .. 24, lookAt = the board)` / `TurnAim` / `ClearAimLimit` keep the mouse turn within 25 degrees either side of the board (free look off meanwhile; G34 turns the view a few degrees to the left so that the crosshair starts ON the board: the camera hangs a shoulder to the right of the head); E leaves from anywhere (`UpdatePlaying`; `Use` ignores a second E in the same frame), and the game lets go by itself when the body is knocked over, pinned or more than 1.5 m off its stance (a run starting teleports him). `Leave` / `OnDisable` release everything.
- The keys: `DartBoard.DrawHints` draws a plate above the weapon bar from `DartsRules.Hints` (`A  D` move side to side, `MOUSE` turn the camera slightly, `LMB` hold to wind up, let go to throw, `E` leave). Result: DockSeeds 24 of 24 seeds fine (minimum 1 forklift in one seed, 2 drums, 6 blocks, 12 stacks, 8 shelves), DockLook 12, Sneak 46.
- Tests: pure checks for `Lateral`, `ClampAim`, `Hints` (pure 597 PASS); the Round12 scenario checks the new board place, the cleaned hallway, the tape in the hallway, the lock (position, `inputLocked`, `AimLimited`), D / A slide to +-0.45 m, W does not walk, `TurnAim` stops at 25 degrees, the crosshair meets the board's middle, leaving releases (40 PASS); the old third-person camera check now runs with the player back at the tape (it had run 1.3 m from the break room's wall and measured 1.08 m). `README_PLAYTEST.txt` item 20 describes the new place, the keys and the warehouse.

- **Three more asks while it was being tested (verbatim): "Guards that are hit should call for backup upon getting up" / "Backup guards should have a much shorter scan time when a player enters their vision cone" / "The dart camera should also be able to go up and down as well" (patch G36, G35 fixed the crosshair start).** (1) `GuardAI.Stun(seconds, by)` with somebody who did it (melee, punch, a thrown thing; not the boss, not the test panel's `Stun(30, null)`) sets `callOnGetUp`; when the stun is over `BeginGetUpCall()` starts the same visible call as a sighting (`callSeconds` 2 s, the ring, another blow cuts it; `callAnyway` skips the "player must stay in view" rule) and `AfterGetUpCall()` sends him after the player who hit him (chase) or to where the blow came from; `HeistManager.CallBackup(this)` sends the officers to him. A bullet does not knock over, so it starts no call. (2) `GuardAI.IsBackup` (set in `FloorGenerator.SpawnReinforcements*`) and `SpotSecondsAt(distance)`: `GuardRules.BackupSpotFactor` 0.25 (14 m: 0.46 s instead of 1.83 s). (3) The dart camera's pitch range is -30 .. 50 degrees (it was -8 .. 24: it could not reach the lower third of the board), `CameraFollow.TiltAim` / `AimPitch`; G35: `LimitAim` turns the view left by asin(shoulder / distance) and tilts it from the shoulder point, so the crosshair starts on the board's middle (tested within 12 cm). New scenario `GuardCall` (`NetDevGuardCall.cs`; 11 checks: knocked over -> stunned, on his feet he calls, a second blow cuts the call, the call ends in backup + officers, they are backup officers with a quarter of the spotting time, a punch calls too, a bullet starts no call). **First, the new asset packs** (`Assets\Blink`, `Assets\Synty\PolygonConstruction`) were `robocopy`'d with their metas from the real project into `C:\bqm` (`cmp_trees.ps1` only compares Scripts / Editor / Resources / ProjectSettings / Packages, so a missing pack does not stop a ship; `final_build.ps1` syncs the WHOLE real `Assets` into `C:\bqfinal`, so the build has them).

**Patches (`C:\bqs\r12_g43.ps1` .. `r12_g49.ps1` plus b / c / d / e fixes, all archived in `DevTools\QaScripts\round12_patches`; new files in `...\newfiles`).**
- **G43 stunned officers** (`CharacterCatalog.ToonClipSet.stunned`, filled by `CharacterCatalogBuilder` from `Assets/Blink/.../Combat/StunnedLoop.fbx` for all five clip sets; `CharacterAnimator`: slot `Stun`, `Stun()` / `HasStun`, `Recover()` snaps out; `GuardVisual`: `anim.Stun()` instead of `Fall()`; players still fall: `PlayerVisual` untouched). The Blink pose looks like the owner's picture (standing, head down). Tests: `NetDevToon` (weights + picture `toon_lineup_stunned`), `NetDevGuardCall` (a real knocked-over officer: dazed slot > 0.5, down slot < 0.2).
- **G46 Barrett + sounds + red dots.** The long gun table of `WeaponCatalogBuilder` has `SM_Wep_Sniper_Heavy_01` (the pack's Barrett M82: body z -0.436 .. 1.403, so it is drawn at scale 0.625 to the spec length 1.15 m; the foregrip is found at 50-62 % of the body); `LongGunsBatch` rebuilds `WeaponCatalog.asset`. `GunSounds`: a gun with a silencer plays a SYNTHESISED suppressed shot (`MakeSilencedShot`: band-passed noise "pap" + falling sine thump + two metal ticks for the slide / bolt, three variants each for the handgun and the rifle set, soft tail `SilencedTail`; volume `SilencedShotVolume` 0.5) instead of the loud recording turned down; `SoundsSilenced(class)` = the silencer rule (pistol, SMG, rifle yes; shotgun, sniper, and a rocket launcher when there is one, no). OFFICERS' guns are unchanged (no silencer, loud, heard 45 m): my reading of the ask was "the weapons that carry a silencer"; flip `GuardGun.ShowShot`'s `silenced` argument to `spec.CanSilence` if the owner wants theirs silenced too. Red dots: `WeaponCatalog.redDot` (`SM_Wep_Attachment_Redot_01`) and `GunModel.sightAt` (measured by the builder: on the barrel's line, on top of the highest point of the body at 46 % / 46 % / 38 % along; `BodyRange`, `BodyTop`, `SightSeat`), `RangedWeapon.TakesRedDot` (SMG, rifle, shotgun), `Layout.hasSight`, `AttachSight` (+ `AddDot`: a small emissive red sphere in the window) on the held gun, the item on the floor and the vendor's wall display. Tests: R7 pure checks (clips 0.15-0.45 s, rules), `NetDevGuns` (Barrett prefab, sights in the catalog, per gun: sight on held + item, silenced-shot counter 1 for SMG / rifle, 0 for shotgun / sniper).
- **G44 / G45 the five characters.** The owner's pictures, matched on candidate sheets (`NetDevCharSheet`, `NetDevDressSheet` scenarios: `qa_regress.ps1 -Methods CharSheet:gfx / DressSheet:gfx / ThemeSheet:gfx`): (1) the man in the orange hi-vis vest with the teal shirt and the tan cap (it looks brown in the dark of the owner's picture) = `SM_Chr_Builder_HighVis_01` + `SM_Chr_Attach_Hat_01` in colour theme 4 (tan); (2) the woman with the yellow hard hat, overalls, tool belt = `Builder_Female_01` + `Helmet_01` theme 1 (yellow); (3) the bearded man, tank top, knee pads, orange hard hat with goggles = `Builder_Singlet_01` + `Helmet_Goggles_01` theme 2 (orange) + `Beard_02` (moustache into a chin beard; `Beard_01` is the full beard); (4) the older man, white hard hat, hi-vis harness = `Builder_Harness_01` + `Helmet_01` theme 4 (white) + `Beard_Grey_01`; (5) the woman in the red plaid shirt = Office `Developer_Female_02` (the laptop is a prop, not part of her). The pack's character prefabs are BARE: the attachments (parented to the Head bone with no offset: their pivots are made for that; `ToonDress` in `CharacterCatalog`, `ToonCharacterFactory.AddDress`, the table `CharacterCatalogBuilder.Dress`) and the colour theme of a hat (Textures 01 yellow, 02 orange, 03 blue, 04 white for a hard hat; caps red / blue / green / tan; A / B / C are skin tones, `PolygonConstruction_01_A` is the body's atlas) are what the pictures show. `DockWorkerIds` = `PlayerAppearance.DefaultToons` = these five in this order: they are the ONLY playable characters (Dock worker 1-5; the other office people and the rest of the builders stay in the catalog as `Other`, so old saved looks of friends still show, but a saved look of a no-longer-playable character becomes Dock worker 1 on this machine: `PlayerAppearance.Migrated`) and, because `IntroCinematic.ToonCycle` = `DefaultToons`, the five workers of the intro. To offer more characters again: add ids to `DockWorkerIds`/`DefaultToons` (or `SyntyRole`) and run Employees Take Revenge > Setup Characters. Tests: R4 pure (five, order, dress counts, others not offered), `NetDevToon` (dress on the Head bone in its own theme, picture `toon_five`).
- **G47 / G48 / G49 first person.** Why the character blocked the screen: `CameraFollow.ShowBody` spared renderers that have a `HeldGun` / `MeleeWeapon` in their parents, but `HeldGun` sits on the character's MODEL root, so with a gun in the hands the WHOLE body counted as "the gun" and stayed in the picture (a held melee weapon was the opposite: its held model has no `MeleeWeapon`, so it was hidden). Now the held models are found by name (`X_Held`, `CameraFollow.IsHeldModel`) and stay whole; the body is out of the picture except two forearms and hands, drawn by `Resources/Shaders/FirstPersonArms.shader` (the body is one skinned mesh a build cannot cut, so it is cut per pixel: everything farther than 10.5 cm from the forearm lines is thrown away; `PushArms` hands the arm bones in at `Camera.onPreCull`; what the character wears on the head is hidden whole; the whole body still casts its shadow; a rig without arms is hidden as before). The gun used to be carried at the chest (0.5 m below the eyes: only its muzzle tip showed at the bottom edge): `HeldGun` now SHOULDERS it in first person (`FirstPersonShare`, blended with the zoom: the grip 0.42 m in front of the eyes, 7.5 cm right, 21.5 cm below the line of sight, the barrel along it, the left hand on the side of the handguard; a pistol held out in one hand) so the red dot stands near the middle of the screen. `FirstPersonMelee` raises the right arm for a bat / crowbar / axe (lower right, up and forward; a swing or punch takes it back). Also found: `GunController` kept a gun's model in the hand when the equipped gun item was DESTROYED (`==` on a destroyed object equals "no gun"): fixed with `ReferenceEquals`. Tests: `NetDevRound12` (first person with / without the SMG, back in third person) and the new `NetDevFpLook` scenario (`qa_regress.ps1 -Methods FpLook:gfx`: the real camera in a plain arena with each gun, the crowbar and empty hands: muzzle and sight positions in the viewport, nothing but arms drawn; pictures `be_shot_fplook_*`, side views `be_shot_fplook_side_* / leftside_*` show the arms the camera does not).
- **G52 the PVP room** (`r12_g52.ps1`; `FloorGenerator.DockRooms.cs`, `NetDevRound12`). The doorway is a FORKLIFT door: `PvpDoorWidth 2.4 x PvpDoorHeight 2.8 m` (`DoorwayWall` takes a width and a height; the break room's stays 1.6 x 2.6; the lobby's forklift `DockDressing.Forklift` is 1.21 x 2.23 m and 3.2 m long, so it goes through side-on with 0.6 m to spare each side); the centre of the doorway is where it was (x 9.5, z 15.6). The three shipping containers (`container` x 2, `container_small`) are gone; the cover is the lobby's steel shelving with its cardboard (`DockDressing.Shelf`, 1.8 x 0.66 x 2.4 m, one box collider each): ONE line of ten down the west wall from the pocket's south wall to the north row (the pocket and the hall are one line), five across the north wall, two on the elevator room's north wall (flush with the east wall), and three rows of cover - north of the red mat (z 21.9, three units), south of it (z 15.5, two units, west of the doorway's lane) and down the middle of the pocket (x 2.4, three units): 25 units. Kept: the pocket's two stacked-crate pairs, two loaded pallets and two crates on the hall's east side; two blocks of boxes on pallets (x 1.9) by the west line replace the four old ones. The way in from the doorway (x 6..9.3, z 14.4..16.8), the joint between pocket and hall, and the middle of the room (radius 1 m round the mat) stay open (R12 checks). Tests: `NetDevRound12` now says no container, 20+ shelves with 4+ boxes each and one collider each, 6+ pallets / crates, no two pieces overlapping and all of them inside the walls (floor plans), a forklift-sized box passes the PVP doorway and a 1.7 m one does not pass the break room's; pictures `round12_pvp_forklift_outside / inside` (the real forklift prop standing in the doorway), `round12_pvp_plan` (a camera straight down just under the ceiling; north to the right, west at the top), and the old four.
- **G53 the Intro's opening** (`r12_g53.ps1`; `IntroRules`, `IntroCinematic`, `NetDevIntroTest`, `NetDevEtr`). The five workers stand in a RING (radius 0.9 m, `RingSide -2.2` / `RingOut 2.6` in the TV's frame = the west side of the break room, in front of the counters; the first version had it on the east side with the camera in the north-west corner and the real-window pictures showed the line-up end-on, as a column), facing its middle, and TALK: `IntroRules.TalkerAt(t, 5)` gives a different one every 1.4 s (a step that goes round all five), the talker's head moves with the words and glances round, the others turn their heads to the talker and now and then nod (`Pose`, toon people only; a Party character just stands), and soft Intro blips play for the talker's turn (`CircleVoices`, volume 0.11, a pitch per worker, a breath between turns, ends a quarter second before the TV clicks on; set `CircleVoices = false` for silence - never heard by me). When the TV comes on (`TvOnAt` is now 5.4 s, it was 3.2 s: EVERY later time moved by +2.2 s, see the beats table) the one nearest the TV looks round first, then each walks (1.7 m/s, the walk clip at 1.13x) from its spot to its place in the line in front of the TV: each place is paired with the circle spot that makes the walks shortest altogether (`PairWithRing`, brute force over 5!, so no two walks cross); the walks END one after another from `FirstArrival` on (`IntroRules.DepartAt/WalkProgress`: the longest set off first, nobody before `TvOnAt + 0.3 s`), all five are in the line by about 7.5 s, as the boss's first line starts (7.4 s); positions are a pure function of the clock (so a skip forward in the tests, and every machine, lands right). The first camera set-up (`DockShot.Establish`, new numbers: the break room from its north-east corner looking west-south-west, circle on the right, TV on the left, the row side-on) now lasts the first TWO beats (the dark TV and the TV coming on) with one slow move. Tests: `NetDevIntroTest` (pure: new times, `TalkerAt` goes round all five and changes every turn, `DepartAt` / `WalkProgress` steady, nobody sets off before looking round, the last arrival is within half a second of the boss's first word), `NetDevEtr` (the ring: radius, facing in, inside the break room; some on the walk 0.8 s after the TV comes on; the old "in the line, facing the TV" check at the first line), pictures `intro_tv_opening_workers`, `intro_tv_opening_talk`, `intro_tv_gather_walking`, `intro_tv_gather_arriving` (+ the per-beat ones).

- **G52b / G55 nothing may block the way into the PVP room** (the owner, 2026-10-20, three messages: "The Forklift should not be blocking the PvP room doorway" / "That is important, players need to be able to get into the PvP room" / "Implement that before the build please"; the forklift in my first test pictures stood IN the doorway on purpose to show it fits - a test prop, never part of the game - and those pictures are gone). Generator (`FloorGenerator.Dock.cs`, `ScatterStorage.clear`, which `Free()` checks every random placement against, shelves against walls included): the plaza in front of the doorway is now 6.4 x 4.8 m (`PvpRoomDoor.x .. +6.4`, z +-2.4; it was 4.6) and a lane 2.7 m wide (`elev.x + 4.2 .. +6.9`, z 12 to 18) runs from it down the elevator room's east wall to the way through to the bay, so a forklift fits all the way from the elevator's east door (z 7.5..10.5) to the doorway. Tests: `NetDevHarness.PvpWayIn` (DockSeeds, 24 seeds, and Round12): a flood fill on the floor plan (0.2 m cells, `Physics.CheckCapsule` at each) from just outside the elevator's door - a player (radius 0.4, 1.8 m high) must reach EVERY free spot of the PVP room (hall, north part, pocket) and a forklift-sized body (radius 0.65, 2.3 m high) the plaza, the doorway and the first metres inside; R12 also says no forklift parks within 6.4 m of the PVP doorway. G52b: the stacked crates of the pocket moved 10 cm west (the turned one poked 2 cm into the wall), two blocks of boxes on pallets stand on the pocket's east side (the docks need six pallet blocks in all: DockLook / DockSeeds), the pocket's middle row moved to x 2.3 (25 shelf units in all; see the picture `round12_pvp_plan`, north to the right, west at the top).

**Known limits / to tell the owner.** The forearms are mostly hidden behind the gun from the eye's point of view in the shouldered pose (they show when looking down and in the side views); the silenced sound is synthesised and was never heard by me (levels guessed: tune `MakeSilencedShot` / `SilencedShotVolume`); the red dot is a small red sphere (no reticle projection); officers' guns unchanged; the five characters' hats are the pack's attachments matched to the pictures by colour theme (a different hat = a different row of `CharacterCatalogBuilder.Dress`); the Barrett is drawn smaller than the pack's own scale (0.625) because every gun is scaled to its spec length; the hat attachments add about 3 x 4096 px atlases to the build; the circle's "talking" is the head and the soft blips only (the rig has no gesture clips; the arms hang), and the blips were never heard by me; the Intro's cameras were placed from pictures, not by eye in motion; the PVP room's shelves are solid blocks (bullets stop at them, nothing is seen through the gaps in the uprights) and 25 of them add about 330 renderers (one batch of materials, no cost to speak of).
