### 2.38 The owner's HUD and first-person asks of 2026-10-20 night (a Claude session working on the pulled code; compiled against stubs only, NEVER run in Unity)

**The asks (verbatim in spirit).** (1) "the top right has two layers there. It says player 1 on top of another bubble. For now put them below each other so we can decide which one we want." (2) "When I go into first person mode my character is glitching out. I am able to see my whole character while I am moving. My head is about where the red dot sight is on the gun." (3) "when zoomed in all the way to first person I should be looking out the red dot sight/scope/iron sights of whatever weapon it is". (4) "add two more shortcuts for weapons and equipment ... a total of 6 different short cuts 1-6". (5) A DESIGN PREVIEW ONLY (not in the game, "very important do not put this code into the game"): see-through equipment bubbles and no gold backpack card at the bottom left, so that Tab shows how much more you can carry. The preview is a Design artifact made in the chat (two artboards: normal view and holding Tab); nothing of it is in the code. If the owner likes it, the work is: make `HudWidgets.WeaponBar` plates translucent, stop calling `HudWidgets.PackCard` from `Backpack.DrawHud`, and show the weight while Tab (the inventory) is open.

**What was built.**
- *Top right stack.* The player badge is at y 14 (36 tall). The "TEST MODE F1" tag (offline, `HudWidgets.TestTag`) used to sit at y 10, BEHIND the badge, and the voice pills (online, `VoiceChat.OnGUI`) at y 42, overlapping its bottom edge: both now start at `HudWidgets.TopRightBelowBadge` (56 reference px) so they sit below the badge. `PvpBoard` moved from y 64 to 94 to stay under the first voice pill. (Several voice rows still overlap the PVP board: untouched.)
- *Six slots.* `WeaponBarRules.Slots` is 6 (the keys 1 to 6 follow from it: `Backpack.Update` loops over `Slots`); `HudWidgets.WeaponBar` sizes the plates to the room between the pack card and the ammunition panel (56 to 176 px each, 6 px gaps). Key hints in `KeybindScreen` and `HowToPlayContent` say "1 - 6". `NetDevRound12`'s pure bar check was rewritten for six slots (and the same check was run as a plain .NET program: all pass).
- *Looking through the sight (first person at full zoom).* `HeldGun.FindSight` finds the sight point once per gun: the red dot's `RedDot/Dot` child for the SMG / rifle / shotgun; for the pistol and anything without a red dot it works the rear sight out from the model's mesh bounds (top of the body, a sixth along it), for the sniper the scope's eyepiece (top, a third along; guessed from the bounds, NOT measured on the Barrett model). While the camera is at this body's eyes (`FirstPersonShare`, zoom 0.5 to 0.95) and the gun is up (not reloading, `raiseNow` near 1), the barrel points along the camera's forward (`ads`) and the grip is placed so that the sight is `RedDotRelief` 0.22 m / `IronRelief` 0.26 m / `ScopeRelief` 0.07 m in front of the camera on the line of sight (`FirstPersonGrip`; both `TwoHandPose` and `OneHandShouldered` use it; a gun with no sight found keeps the old shouldered offsets). `CameraFollow` pulls the near clip plane in to 0.04 in first person (the camera's own value comes back in third person and on disable). `HeldGun.LocalDotShare` (0..1) tells the crosshair (`Crosshair.OnGUI`) to hide while the view is through a red dot: the dot itself is the crosshair. Bullets are unchanged (muzzle to the camera's aim point). No zoom (field of view) was added for the sniper scope.
- *The body in first person.* The real cause of "the whole character shows while moving" was NOT found (nobody could run the game). What was changed to remove the likely causes: (a) the first person camera pivot now follows the body almost rigidly (`followSmooth` x 8 at full zoom) so a running body cannot get ahead of the camera and put its head and shoulders in the picture; (b) `CameraFollow.KeepBodyHidden` runs every frame and puts back the arms-shader materials / the ShadowsOnly mode of a body part if something swapped them, and the scan for new body parts runs 5 times a second instead of twice; (c) `FirstPersonArms.shader` throws away a sphere (`_BEHead`, 0.22 m, centred 8 cm above the head bone) round the head, except what is within 12 cm of a fingertip; the head position is pushed with the arms in `PushArms`. IF THE OWNER STILL SEES THE BODY WHILE MOVING: ask for a screenshot taken WHILE MOVING and check (i) whether it is the body's own shadow on the floor (the body casts its shadow on purpose), (ii) whether `rig.ArmsShown` is false for that character (`IsSkin` needs "Standard" materials: the new Synty builder characters may use another shader, in which case the whole body is ShadowsOnly), (iii) the run animation leaning the head forward of the camera.

**Then, the same night: no arms at all.** The owner looked at the view through the red dot (a picture) and asked: "when scrolled into first person can you remove the character model from being seen here". The pale forearms and hands of the arms shader were still filling the lower middle of the screen. `CameraFollow.ShowArms` (static bool) is now `false`: in first person every body part is ShadowsOnly (its shadow still falls) and only what is in the hands is drawn (the gun, a held melee weapon). Set `ShowArms = true` to bring the arms shader back (everything about it is still in place). `NetDevFpLook`'s "nothing of the body but its arms" check follows the flag (`ArmsShown == ShowArms`).

**And then, the pistol and the sniper rifle (the owner, with two pictures, 2026-10-20 night: "all the guns look good except the pistol and the sniper. For the pistol make an arm extended and a hand holding the gun. For the sniper see what you can do, I'm not positive, I just want iron sights for now").** The pictures: the pistol was seen end-on with no hand (a floating slide and grip), the sniper's view was blocked by the back of its scope (the eyepiece point guessed from the model bounds put the camera inside it). Now: (a) a gun held in ONE hand (`!twoHand`: the pistol) shows the owner's right arm reaching out: `HeldGun.LocalArmMode` = 1 while the view is at the eyes, `CameraFollow` then applies the arms shader to the right arm only (`_BEArmL*` are thrown far away; the arm's cut starts 75 % up the upper arm, `SetArm`'s new `upperShare`), and sets the body up again when the gun in the hand changes (`armsAppliedFor`); the pistol's sight point is then `armLength * 0.85` (24 to 45 cm) in front of the camera, i.e. at arm's length. (b) the sniper rifle has IRON SIGHTS for now (no scope view, no zoom): the line of sight runs along the top of the model from a sixth of the way along it, 6 % of the gun's height above the top of the scope, with the crosshair kept (no red dot). A proper scope view (zoom, a lens, the eyepiece point measured on the Barrett model) is not built. `NetDevFpLook`'s arms check expects the arm for the pistol only (`ArmsShown == (ShowArms || kind == Pew)`).

**Then the sniper scope and a stiff pistol arm (the owner, 2026-10-20 23:14: "for the sniper can you make it so we look down the scope and it zooms in a little with a normal crosshair in the scope. Also for the pistol the arm is glitching while it moves if you could make it more stiff").** This SUPERSEDES the sniper's iron sights above. (a) *Scope.* `HeldGun.LocalScope` (0..1, static, eased in/out over about a quarter of a second) is 1 when the local player is at the eyes with a SNIPER fully raised (the same `ads > 0.99` the other sights use: not while reloading). While it is up: `CameraFollow.ApplyScopeFov` narrows the camera's field of view to 1/`HeldGun.ScopeMagnification` (2.5, in tangent space; the field it had is remembered when the zoom starts and put back when it ends, also in `OnDisable`), and the mouse turns the view by the same ratio (so it is not twitchy); `HeldGun.HideForScope` switches off the rifle's MeshRenderers (only the ones that were on, and puts exactly those back; nothing else about the body or the arms changes); `Crosshair.OnGUI` draws `HudWidgets.Scope` instead of the reticle - everything outside a round lens (radius 0.47 of the screen height) dark, a thin rim, and a plain fine cross with a small gap and a dot (red over a foe) - at `GUI.depth = 40` so the rest of the HUD (ammo, hotbar, pack) stays on top of it. The lens is two `HudTheme.Ring` calls (a ring wider than the screen's half diagonal, so no texture is needed). The sight point of the rifle (`FindSight`) is still used to bring the gun to the line of sight during the ease-in. To change the zoom: `ScopeMagnification`; the lens size: `lens` in `HudWidgets.Scope`. (b) *Stiff pistol arm.* The shake came from the shoulder: the running animation and the wobbling body move it relative to the camera (which follows the body smoothly) while the hand is fixed to the camera, so the solved elbow and forearm swung. `HeldGun.OneHandShouldered` now first pulls the shoulder bone to where it is relative to the eyes (measured in the view's heading so looking up and down does not move it, smoothed at `ShoulderFollow` 1.5 per second, never moved more than `ShoulderMaxMove` 15 cm from where the animation put it), so the arm rides the camera like a viewmodel; it is reset when the gun is lowered or the view leaves first person. Only the one-handed gun (pistol) uses it; two-handed guns show no arms. `NetDevFpLook`: the sniper no longer has the muzzle-in-the-picture check (the rifle is not drawn in the scope); it has a scope check instead (`LocalScope > 0.95`, field of view below 0.6 of the one before, no rifle mesh on). NOT RUN IN UNITY: check the scope's lens looks right at other screen sizes, that the zoom does not fight any other code that sets `fieldOfView`, that the rifle comes back after reloading, and that the pistol arm no longer shakes while running / strafing (if it still does, raise nothing: lower `ShoulderFollow` toward 0.5, or also hold the hand to the camera in `SolveArm`).

**Then the HUD design from the preview, IN THE GAME (the owner, 2026-10-20 23:24, with a picture of the preview: "this time I want you to make the change. change the bottom bar to this style as well as change the ammo to see through. I also want you to remove the backpack from the bottom left corner just like in this screenshot").** This turns the design preview of ask (5) above into code. (a) `HudTheme.Glass` (new; `GlassTint` 0x1A388A, `GlassAlpha` 0.24): a see-through plate - a thin dark-blue tint, a pale 2 px edge and a faint dark line just outside it. `HudWidgets.WeaponBar` draws each slot with it (edge alpha 0.95 and pale blue for the one in hand, 0.5 for a slot with a weapon, 0.28 for an empty slot; the key caps, names and the blue bar under the one in hand are as before). `HudWidgets.AmmoPanel` uses it too (alpha 0.30), and so does the "SPARE n (m clips)" tag above it (`GunController.DrawSpare`: tinted by its colour, alpha 0.34, white text). (b) `Backpack.DrawHud` no longer calls `HudWidgets.PackCard` (the function is kept, marked as not drawn). Tab is still a TOGGLE (it opens the BACKPACK window, as before; the preview showed it held): the window already had the weight bar and "x / 44 lbs", and now also has a green chip "39.9 lbs left to carry" (red under 2 kg; left out if the loot chip leaves no room). The loot count / "in hand" lines of the old card are in that window already. To change how see-through the bar is: `HudTheme.GlassAlpha`; the ammunition panel: the 0.3f in `AmmoPanel`. NOT RUN IN UNITY: look at the bar and the ammunition on a bright wall (readability), and at the Tab window at a small size (the chip should simply disappear if it does not fit).

**Things to look at first in Unity.** First person with each gun (`qa_regress.ps1 -Methods FpLook:gfx`; its red-dot check now expects the dot within 4 % of the middle of the screen at 0.1 to 0.4 m and `HeldGun.LocalDotShare` > 0.9, the muzzle check is unchanged), the pistol's iron-sight line, the sniper's scope point, the left hand on the foregrip with the gun this close to the eyes, the right forearm filling the lower right of the screen, the near plane (0.04) showing walls up close, and the weapon bar at small window sizes (six plates between the pack card and the ammunition panel).

**Files changed.** `CameraFollow.cs`, `Guns/HeldGun.cs`, `Interaction/Crosshair.cs`, `Hud/HudWidgets.cs`, `Hud/WeaponBarRules.cs`, `Hud/KeybindScreen.cs`, `HowToPlayContent.cs`, `Backpack.cs` (comments), `Net/Voice/VoiceChat.cs`, `PvpBoard.cs`, `Resources/Shaders/FirstPersonArms.shader`, `Editor/NetDev/NetDevRound12.cs`, `Editor/NetDev/NetDevFpLook.cs`. The network protocol ("26", `NetConst.ProtocolVersion`) is unchanged: nothing here is networked, so players on the old and the new build can still share a run.

### 2.39 The QA test plan for the whole game (the owner's last ask of 2026-10-20 night, 23:30) - FINISHED 2026-10-21; STATUS and HAND-OFF

**STATUS (final, 2026-10-21): DONE - nothing is pending in the doc.** The doc is at rev 84 and holds 753 test cases in five sections, plus a sixth and last section (the smoke pass, the exit criteria, the release gate, the assumptions, the gaps, the findings for the owner and the coverage counts). Cases per category and area: FUN 271 (MOV 19, INT 23, PCK 17, MEL 18, GUN 22, GRD 25, CUS 18, OUT 26, LOT 13, ELV 19, VND 17, WIN 12, NET 24, PVP 18), UIX 131 (MEN 16, SET 16, KEY 9, CUS 7, HUD 18, OVL 19, SCL 15, TXT 10, CTL 13, ACC 8), EDG 153 (INP 17, ACT 16, INV 16, ECO 12, PHY 14, AIX 10, SEQ 16, OPN 12, DEV 20, NET 20), PRF 94 (FPS 17, HIT 11, MEM 11, LOD 11, SAV 12, SOK 10, MPF 11, STB 11), REG 104 (ONB 12, FLW 11, GAT 8, LAT 17, MPG 10, CHG 23, OLD 16, VER 7). 341 are High, 325 Medium and 87 Low. Every section ends with a checklist (1.15, 2.11, 3.11, 4.9, 5.9); the last section holds the smoke pass (6.1) and the release gate (6.3). Checked on the final markdown export: 753 unique IDs, five cells in every row, every Priority High / Medium / Low, every ID named in a checklist or a cross-reference exists, no stray escape characters; the quoted game texts were compared with the code by script, and the few the script could not match were checked by hand (they are format templates such as the backup notices, whose numbers are filled in at run time, or log-search words). **THE PLAN HAS NEVER BEEN RUN AGAINST A GAME:** every expected result is the design as read from the code and from these notes, and nothing in it was seen in Unity or in a build.

**The ask (the owner's words, shortened).** "Role: you are an expert Senior Video Game Quality Assurance (QA) Lead ... Generate a comprehensive, end-to-end Quality Assurance test plan and test-case suite. Break it down into clear categories: 1. Functional & Gameplay Testing (core loops, input response, win/loss states) 2. UI/UX & Menu Testing (settings, HUD scale, localization, controller vs. mouse/keyboard prompts) 3. Edge Cases & Negative Testing (button smashing, interrupting animations, inventory overflow, disconnecting controllers mid-game) 4. Performance & Stability Benchmarks (frame rate drops, memory leaks, load times, checkpoint saves) 5. Regression & Progression Flow (tutorial through late-game). Output format: a structured markdown table and checklist containing these exact columns: [Test ID, Feature/Area, Step-by-Step Action / Input, Expected Result], and [Priority: High/Medium/Low]." The owner added: "take your time with this one" and "If I happen to run out of session tokens please write the developer handoff and auto start the next session if the timer restart happens." Nothing in this task changes the game's code; do not run git.

**Where the plan lives.** A Claude Docs document (an artifact), NOT a file in the repo: "Overtime Escape: QA Test Plan and Test Cases", https://claude.ai/artifact/THtj96E3eC927vZ266pmtB (doc id `d4e96212-78fb-4d5e-9e35-547ba25c03d0`; its one tab is `dad6c647-f229`, the tab's body node `4c0608ad-b1d5`). Use the `mcp__Claude_Docs__*` tools (`read` with `ref {"object":"project","id":...}` first; `update` with `engine "prose"` to fill; the tools' own `guide` topic.index / topic.editing explain the ops). The doc has these parts, in order: the read-me (how to use the plan, what is under test, priority and IDs, the environments, the test aids, how to report a defect, entry and exit criteria); 1 Functional and Gameplay (1.1 to 1.14, checklist 1.15); 2 UI/UX and Menu (2.1 to 2.10, checklist 2.11); 3 Edge Cases and Negative (3.1 to 3.10, checklist 3.11); 4 Performance and Stability (a table of proposed targets, 4.1 to 4.8, checklist 4.9); 5 Regression and Progression (5.1 ONB, 5.2 FLW, 5.3 GAT, 5.4 LAT, 5.5 MPG, 5.6 CHG, 5.7 OLD, 5.8 VER, checklist 5.9); 6 Smoke pass, release gate and gaps (6.1 smoke pass, 6.2 exit criteria, 6.3 release gate, 6.4 assumptions, 6.5 gaps, 6.6 findings for the owner, 6.7 coverage). Environments used in the cases: E1 solo, E2 loopback (two copies on one PC), E3 real Steam (two to five accounts), E4 the Editor's Test Mode (offline only, F1; its elevator stops at floor 4), E5 a development build or the Editor with `TowerRules.DemoLastFloor` changed from 4 to 13 (never ship it); a case marked E5 that cannot be run is marked Blocked.

**The format the owner asked for (keep it).** Every table has EXACTLY five columns, in this order: `Test ID | Feature/Area | Step-by-Step Action / Input | Expected Result | Priority` and Priority is the plain word High, Medium or Low. IDs are `CAT-AREA-NN`, unique across the doc (`FUN` functional, `UIX` UI/UX, `EDG` edge cases, `PRF` performance, `REG` regression and progression; AREA is a three-letter code per feature area). No pipe characters or line breaks inside a cell (number the steps inline: "1) ... 2) ..."). The checklist part: `- [ ]` task lists (a smoke pass near the top of the doc, per-category exit criteria, the release gate at the end).

**How the test cases must be written (what a good one is here).** The plan is about THIS game, so the steps and expected results come from the code and from this file, never from a generic template: the real keys (`Hud/KeybindScreen.cs`, `HowToPlayContent.cs`), the real numbers (`Guns/RangedWeapon.cs` Specs, `Backpack.cs` capacity, `GuardLoadouts.cs`, `HeistManager.cs`, `WobblyPlayer.cs` stamina), the real rules (sections 2.1 custody, 2.10 guard sight, 2.11 outposts / hack / safes, 2.12 alarm panel, 2.13-2.14 pew and HUD, 2.20 voice, 2.21-2.23 quota / elevator / revenge rules, 2.28-2.31 guns, combat, armour, 2.34-2.37 darts, PVP room, first person) and the owner's standing rules (8.1). Where the game does not have something the owner's template names (a controller, localization, a save system, checkpoints), the plan must say so and test what is true instead (for example: "plugging a controller in must not break the keyboard and mouse game"), not invent a feature. NOTHING in the game has ever been played by the author of these test cases: expected results are the design as written, and the plan says so.

**Scheduled check-ins (the "auto start") - status.** Five one-shot scheduled tasks named "QA plan check-in 1/5" to "5/5" (trigger ids `trig_01WxXhTCA2yia3kEEMiCDydT`, `trig_017gMMNzTgn2nAkkzQKdVxYL`, `trig_01363VaxJJt2Krx5qiBDvLvV`, `trig_01SFmLYHCyhoiimuBThqp8Se`, `trig_016cZTLWeUWCQ2hzS7SVULoh`) were set at 23:34 on 2026-10-20 to wake the Claude session that wrote this at 00:34, 01:34, 02:34, 03:35 and 04:35 EDT, so that the work would resume by itself if the usage limit ran out. They were a safety net only: the session that set them also finished the plan. All five have fired. The fifth was deleted when the plan was finished (04:35 EDT). The owner first declined the deletion of 1/5 to 4/5, then said to delete what is not needed, and all four were deleted on the morning of 2026-10-21. The account has no scheduled task left for this plan, so there is nothing to clean up and nothing will wake a session about it (the ids above are kept only as a record; they no longer exist). A check-in was only a stored prompt: it had no authority beyond its text.

**If you are the next session, do this first.** (1) The plan is finished: do not rebuild it. (2) Read section 6.5 (gaps) and 6.6 (findings for the owner) of the doc: they are the open questions. (3) If the owner (or Patrick) played a build and reports what happened, correct the cases it touches in place: IDs never change, so a defect that quotes one stays valid; add a new case at the END of an area with the next number and never renumber. (4) When the game changes (a rule, a price, a text, the protocol), search the doc for the old value and fix every case that quotes it. Numbers that are repeated in many cases: the quota list (FUN-LOT-10, REG-MPG-01), the officers per floor (REG-GAT-01), the voice falloff (FUN-NET-14, REG-OLD-05), the join and version texts (FUN-NET-09, EDG-NET-12, REG-VER-03) and the autopilot counts (REG-VER-06, 6.1). (5) After any batch of edits export the doc as markdown and re-run the checks (unique IDs, five cells per row, a Priority word, every cited ID exists). (6) Keep this section up to date and never run git.

**Research digest (facts already checked in the code, for writing test cases; written 23:55, add to it as you check more):**
- **Movement / camera** (`WobblyPlayer.cs`, `CameraFollow.cs`, `Stamina.cs`): ONLY the LEFT Shift (sprint), LEFT Ctrl or C (crouch, held), LEFT Alt or V (look around, held) work (the right-hand keys do nothing); crouch ignores Ctrl while Up/Down arrow is held (Ctrl+Up/Down is the view). Walk `maxSpeed` 5 m/s, sprint x1.6 (8 m/s), crouch x0.55; sprint needs: key, not crouching, not in custody, moving, not winded; jump (Space, impulse 5.5, about 1.5 m) only when grounded and not stumbling, crouching, in custody, hack open or input-locked. Stamina 100, drain 16/s (empty after about 6 s), regen 12/s after a 1 s breather, winded (no sprint) from empty until 25 %; refilled on a new floor. Footstep gaps 0.28 s sprint / 0.5 walk / 0.75 crouch. Camera pitch limits -35 and 60; P, mouse wheel and Ctrl+Up/Down switch first / third person. An open hack panel zeroes movement and refuses crouch / jump / hide.
- **Pickups / pack** (`Backpack.cs`, `PlayerGrab.cs`, `HideSpot.cs`, `Doors/DoorManager.cs`): E reach 2.2 m (messages "Too far away - get closer", "Nothing there - aim the crosshair at an item", "Picked up <name> (<lbs>)", "Too heavy for your pack (<lbs>, <lbs> free)" + " - carry it with friends (Q)" for team loot); capacity 20 kg = 44.1 lbs (Tab bar green to 65 %, amber above, red above 90 %; "left to carry" chip red under 2 kg); Tab window "BACKPACK": IN YOUR PACK (EQUIPMENT / LOOT / OTHER, Equip or Put away, Drop, DROP EVERYTHING) and ON THE GROUND NEARBY (Take or Too heavy); the vendor pays 70 % of value; Q grabs (light items follow the hand, heavy loot needs enough holders: `HeavyLoot.massPerLifter` 6, one knock at most 45 of 100 integrity, no damage while held or 0.75 s after), LMB throws a held item (force 9); keys 1-6 pick the weapons of the bar (the first six weapons of the pack in pickup order, melee included; an empty slot does nothing; the numpad works; locked while Tab / shop / elevator panel / outpost / Esc menu / test panel is open); the first weapon picked up is equipped at once; clips: "+N rounds for your pew", "You have no <gun> for this clip", "You can't carry more than three clips" (twelve for the pistol); one armour at a time. Doors: prompts "[E] Open door" / "[E] Close door" / "[E] Unlock door" / "Something is in the doorway"; sliding doors stay open until closed; officers open doors on their route. H toggles hiding in a desk / locker / closet trigger (leaving it unhides; blocked in custody and in the hack). Z scan (`LootScanRules`): pulse 28 m/s, one every 2.4 s, marks last 6 s + 1.4 s fade, valuables worth at least $60 in the current room only, blocked in the docks, the elevator, a screen, custody and the run-start cinematic; colours (owner 2026-10-20): safes and valuables gold, unique items (weapons) red, cash green. Journal J checklist lines: "All valuables collected", "All unique items collected", "All safes opened", "All cash collected" (green tick + strike-through).
- **Guns** (`Guns/RangedWeapon.cs` Specs, `GunController.cs`, `GunRules.cs`): Pew (1.2 kg, 8 rounds, 2 spare clips, max 12 clips, reload 2.2 s, interval 0.3 s, range 45 m, spread 1.2 deg, guardHits 4, noise 45 m / silenced 12 m, clip $120); SMG (24, 2.0 s, 0.085 s, 38 m, 3.4 deg, guardHits 2, noise 40 / 11, mag $480, floor 5); Rifle (30, 2.6 s, 0.12 s, 70 m, 1.6 deg, guardHits 4, noise 55 / 14, $750, floor 8); Shotgun (6 shells, 8 pellets of guardHits 1, 3.0 s, 0.85 s, 24 m, 5.5 deg, noise 60 never silenced, $420, floor 6); Sniper (5, 3.4 s, 1.35 s, 120 m, 0.25 deg, guardHits 10, noise 85 never silenced, $600, floor 10). The player's Pew, SMG and rifle are silenced (the shotgun and sniper never). Auto-reload 0.25 s after the last round; LMB held repeats fire; R reloads; a switch cancels a reload; nothing fires with a menu open, the cursor free, pinned, hiding, knocked over or with an item held (the click throws it); empty with no spares: "Out of ammo - the dealer in the Loading Docks sells <gun's ammo plural>" (at most every 3 s); ammo panel bottom right (orange at 3 or fewer, red at 0), tag "SPARE  n  (c clips)" / "NO SPARE CLIPS"; the Docks have unlimited ammo; a found gun holds one clip and no spare. Bullets stop at the first wall / door / furniture, not at loose items or teammates. A guard has `hitsToKill` 10; a bullet does the gun's guardHits minus the shield's share (light 0.75, medium 0.5, heavy 0.3 pass; pistol needs 3 / 4 / 5 / 9 shots by tier), never knocks him over, cuts his radio call and frees a player he holds; a melee blow knocks him over (bat 3 s, crowbar 4 s, hatchet 6 s), cuts the call, frees a held player and does NOT count towards his knock-out (only the boss counts it, 1 of 30); punch (empty hands, LMB): reach 1.0 m, hit after 0.15 s, cooldown 0.55 s, stun 1.4 s, noise 9 m (miss 3 m), never counts. Throwables: mug stun 2.5 s, stapler 6 s, squeaky toy lures, extinguisher 10 s; impact noise 14 m, below 2 m/s silent. A guard down for good drops the key (floor 12) and one clip, the body sinks after 15 s (+1 s), the boss never goes.
- **Guards** (`GuardRules.cs`, `GuardAI.cs`, `Guns/GuardLoadouts.cs`): walk 2.2, investigate 3, chase 4.6 m/s; cone 65 deg each side, 14 m (hiding player 1.5 m), long sight 2.4x (34 m) within 9 deg; spot time 0.15 s + 0.12 s per metre (about 1.8 s at 14 m; backup officers x0.25); within 0.4 m always seen; then he stands and looks 2 s (`callSeconds`), calls backup if you are still in view (a gap over 0.3 s drops the call), chases until you are out of the cone for 5 s, investigates 3 s, patrols; catch radius 1.6 m; a knocked-over guard calls backup on getting up; patrol guards = base (3) + floor + 2 from floor 3; armed share none before floor 3, a quarter on 3, half from 8; floor 3 pistols + light shield, 5 SMG + light, 6 one shotgun + heavy, 8 rifles + medium and two shotguns, 10 exactly two snipers (no shield); only shotgun and sniper hits end a player's floor; donut break 1.5 % of patrol stops, 6.5 s, never armed / boss / post guards; the outpost guard is blind and deaf outside his room while the door is locked.
- **Custody** (`CustodyRules.cs`, `HeistHud.cs`): pin by touch (1.6 m) 12 s then busted; freed by a blow, bullet, punch or thrown item on the pinning officer; 3 s immunity; solo pin = instant team bust; nobody free = Failed: banner "BUSTED" + "Busted on <floor>" or "Backup was called - busted on <floor>", Exit button after 1.2 s (offline Restart + Exit; online Exit only, non-host sees "Waiting for host...", host gets "LEAVE THE RUN?" leave with party or solo); spectate a free teammate (A / D or mouse buttons); backup interval 30 s on floor 1 shrinking linearly to 12 s on floor 12 (28.4 / 26.7 / 25.1 on floors 2-4), first call 2 officers then 1 each; alarm panel stands down backup and jams radios 10 s; buy-back $300 + $50 x level busted on, only at the docks; vendor lines "One of your friends got got. You're lucky I have someone on the inside" / "Glad you made it. Make it fast and make it a good deal."
- **Quota / floors** (`TowerRules.cs`): `FirstQuota` 4300 x `QuotaMultiplier` 2.0, rounded to 100: floors 1-12 = 8600, 11400, 14800, 18800, 23400, 28700, 34800, 41600, 49300, 57900, 64500, 71400; floor 13 is the boss (no quota); `GroupSize` 3 (floors 3/6/9/12 are group ends: return to the docks); `KeyFloor` 12; `DemoLastFloor` 4 ("DEMO COMPLETE" banner at the docks); banners "QUOTA MET!" (+ "Surplus $X over the quota becomes your cash..."), "LOADING DOCKS", "ESCAPED THE TOWER".
- **Outposts / safes** (`Outpost/*`): hack = 4 steps of W/A/S/D, 0.6 s intro, 1 s window each, 0.05 s grace; gun safe hack = trace a 16 x 9 zigzag corridor with the mouse (a wall touch = alarm, letting go restarts); safes: cash on floors 1-2 (half the quota), weapons from floor 3; count 1 on floors 1-2, 2 on floor 3, 3 on floors 4-6, 4 from floor 7 (`SafeRules.MaxSafes` 4); crowbar break chance 35 %; a note gives the code 62 % of the time; PA: V at the signed-in outpost computer, up to 48 speakers, heard 22 m, guards in range rush the outpost for 12 s; training dummy (docks): 100 health, up 3 s after a knock-down, heals after 5 s at 15/s; PVP room: 100 health, fist 8, bullet 34, knock-out 4 s, reset 3 s after leaving.
- **Not verified by anybody**: nothing of the first-person, scope, pistol-arm and glass HUD work was run in Unity; real Steam between two accounts, real keys and the mouse, and the sounds have never been checked; floors 5-13 were never seen in a build; the How to Play page still has "[Write ... here.]" placeholders; there is no gamepad, no key rebinding, no localization, no run save (the Docks are the only checkpoint), and no on-screen version string.
- **More facts checked on 2026-10-21 (used in the cases from 5.7, 5.8 and 6).** Patrol officers 4, 5, 8, 9 on floors 1 to 4 (12 is the cap; the officer on duty in the outpost comes on top); `GuardRules.PatrolGuards`. Buy-back price 300 + 50 x the floor the player was busted on. Pin 12 s, immunity 3 s after a rescue, catch radius 1.6 m, the end screen's buttons appear after 1.2 s. Voice (`VoiceRules`, `VoiceChat`): full volume up to 4 m, then quadratic to nothing at 20 m (about 56 % at 8 m, 20 % at 13 m, 7 % at 16 m), the host relays a voice only to players within 24 m, the far ear is lowered to 30 %, the lobby gain is 0.8, and walls do not muffle (distance only). `HeavyLoot`: one knock takes at most 45 of 100 integrity, `MaxCarrySpeed` 9 m/s, a piece stuck 3.2 m for 0.8 s is freed, a piece 8 m from where it was is put back; banners "<NAME> SHATTERED - $<value> lost" and "<NAME> fell out of the world - put back where it last stood". `GuardAI.HasReach` uses `sightBlockers = ~0`, so a wall or a glass pane stops a pin while a body or a rigidbody crate does not. `PlaceGuards` drops patrol stops that cannot be walked from the lobby and silently skips an officer who is left with fewer than two stops. `GunController` compares with `ReferenceEquals`, so the model of a destroyed equipped gun leaves the hand. The rescue below y = -30 runs every 0.5 s; `WobblyPlayer.TeleportTo` and `NetPlayer.PlaceAt` are the only supported ways to move a player (a direct write is undone by the interpolation). `StartNetworked` waits up to 150 s for the host's seed ("The run did not start (the host did not answer)."); `BootLoader` sets `Application.runInBackground = true`. The intro lasts 57.6 s (the TV comes on at 5.4 s, a solo run waits 0.6 s first, skipping needs half the party rounded up). The outpost door is locked and unlocked by typing SECURITY at the terminal; the host ignores the request of a player who is pinned or not in the room. Joining: `NetConst.BuildVersion` is `Application.version + "/" + ProtocolVersion` ("1.0/26"), a mismatch is refused with "Game version mismatch. Update the game." (the browser shows an OTHER VERSION chip and greys JOIN), the lobby tag is "employees-take-revenge" (a build from before the rename cannot see the new lobbies), `MaxPlayers` is 5. Saved data: PlayerPrefs keys all begin "OvertimeEscape." (Appearance, MasterVolume, MusicVolume, MouseSensitivity, VoiceMode, VoiceVolume, Crosshair, TestMode); the look text is `RRGGBB-face-hat-height-width[-modelid]`, at most 56 characters, the model id lowercase letters, digits and underscores up to 24; `PlayerAppearance.Migrated` turns a look whose model is not one of the five playable dock workers into `DefaultToon(0)`, `syn_builder_highvis_01`. `QaAutopilot` runs only in the Editor or with a file `be_qa.flag` next to the exe: `-be-autopilot -be-out FOLDER`, `-be-autopilot-net host|client|introsolo|introoffline|introshots|introhost|introclient`, `-be-intro-skip`. Last recorded counts (Playtest_2026-10-20b, IL2CPP): solo 87, host 40, client 46 PASS; the built intro runs solo 15, solo skipped 10, offline 9, offline skipped 7, host 6, client 7. The menu Employees Take Revenge > Audit Rendering (find pink) writes `Logs/RenderAudit.txt`.

**How the doc was edited (technique notes for the Claude Docs tools).** Every `update` needs `container {"kind":"project","id":"d4e96212-78fb-4d5e-9e35-547ba25c03d0"}` AND `ref {"object":"node","id":"4c0608ad-b1d5"}`, `engine "prose"` and `payload {"ops":[...]}`. Insert before a block: `{"op":"insert","target":{"kind":"blocks","ids":["<session>.<n>"]},"side":"before","source":{"as":"markdown","from":{"kind":"inline","content":"..."}}}`. Replace a block (a pending block is resolved by it): `{"op":"replace","target":{"kind":"blocks","ids":[...]},"with":{"as":"markdown","from":{...}}}`. Change text inside a cell: `{"op":"replace","target":{"kind":"find","text":"<exact text>","parentId":"<optional>","nth":<optional>},"with":{"as":"text","from":{"kind":"inline","content":"..."}}}`; it keeps the formatting around the change. A `find` that matches more than once denies the WHOLE payload: scope it with `parentId` (the id of the table or paragraph, from a read) or `nth`. Escaping: the importer reads square brackets as link syntax; in the JSON write exactly two backslashes before a literal bracket (four show as visible backslashes) or, better, leave brackets out of new text (only the `- [ ]` task items use them). A table cell holds no pipe, line break, angle bracket, backtick, backslash or asterisk and only plain ASCII (write "under", not a less-than sign). Export: `mcp__Claude_Docs__export` with `file "dad6c647-f229"`, `format "markdown"` and `maxBytes 11534336`; the result is too large for the chat and is saved to a file as a JSON array whose second item's `text` is JSON with `data.bytes_b64`; decode it with Python. Pending blocks are left out of the export. The method that worked: build the new rows in a Python script that checks them (five cells, a Priority word, no forbidden characters, every cited ID exists in the last export, no duplicate ID) and prints `json.dumps` of the markdown, then paste that string into the call; insert before the next pending block so the doc grows in order. The scripts lived in the session's scratch folder and are not kept; this paragraph is the record.

**Corrections made while writing (so nobody puts the first drafts back).** The case text now states what the code does. Join refusals: by code, a started run, a full lobby and a code that does not exist all answer "No open lobby with code" plus the code (the Steam search leaves started and full lobbies out); a 4-character code answers "Codes are 5 characters."; another build answers "Game version mismatch. Update the game."; from an invite Steam's own refusal, "The lobby is full." and "That run has already started." apply (FUN-NET-05, FUN-NET-09, EDG-NET-12, EDG-NET-19). A leaver's pack is put down where he stood, except while a floor is being built or when the session ends (FUN-NET-17, EDG-INV-15, REG-MPG-08; the known-limits line in section 5 of these notes was stale and is fixed). The hidden elevator needs the real elevator above floor 4 (E5); in E4 the ride does nothing because the demo cap refuses floor 13, and the hidden elevator then stays dead until the floor is rebuilt; the text "There is no way down from here" is in the code but no click reaches it (FUN-ELV-16, FUN-ELV-17, EDG-SEQ-01). Restart after a loss reloads the scene into the Docks with floor 1 next, cash and bank at zero, the unlocks cleared, a new layout and the intro again (FUN-WIN-07, REG-FLW-08). Floor 3 has 8 patrol officers of whom 2 carry a pew and a light shield, floor 4 has 9 and 3, and the floor's only pistol is in the outpost's gun safe (REG-FLW-06). Pack loot is not cleared by a ride, so it banks at full value on the next floor (REG-FLW-07). The voice modes are named OPEN MIC and PUSH TO TALK (FUN-NET-15). The spawn point is the middle of the elevator car of the Docks (`FloorGenerator` line 129). The version text does not exist "only in the log": `Application.version` is used in `NetConst.BuildVersion` (sent when joining) and in the autopilot's first line (UIX-ACC-06). The purple glow behind unique items in the Z scan is by design and the UI is blue (REG-OLD-11). The project is still named BuildingEscape, so no case assumes a settings reset after the rename (REG-VER-04, REG-VER-05). A found-in-review slip fixed on the last pass: the run-start cinematic is 57.6 s (PRF-FPS-17 said about 55 s) and the "building level N failed" log line has a number in the middle (PRF-SOK-04).

**Findings that are not test cases (also in section 6.6 of the doc; nothing in the game code was changed).** (1) `Assets/Scripts/Net/README.md` (lines 3 and 43) says up to 4 players and a lobby with 4 slots; `NetConst.MaxPlayers` is 5. (2) Comments still use the old quota: `Outpost/SafeRules.cs` lines 16 and 341 say "$2,150 on floor 1" (a safe holds half of the quota, now $4,300) and `Outpost/OutpostLoot.cs` line 67 says "$150 on floor 1" (3.5 percent of the quota in $25 steps, now $300). (3) `OutpostLoot.WeaponPercent` (line 53) has no callers. (4) The F1 panel's list of safes (`TestMode.cs` line 214) calls every safe from floor 3 up "a pistol", although those safes may hold another gun, armour or nothing. (5) The DEMO COMPLETE banner (`HeistHud.cs` line 82) says the vendor sells the pew "now", but `Vendor.PewAfterFloor` is 3. (6) Nothing but `NetConst.ProtocolVersion` tells builds apart (the version text is 1.0 in every build): raise the protocol in every build that changes what is sent or how a floor is built, and put it in the zip name. (7) The known-limits line about a leaver's pack (section 5) was stale; it is fixed in this file.

**What is left for a human.** (a) Play the next build with the plan: the smoke pass (6.1) first, then 5.6 to 5.8 on every build, then the rest. Nobody has played any of the HUD, scope, pistol-arm or first-person work yet (REG-CHG-02 to REG-CHG-07). (b) The owner's decisions listed in 6.5 and 6.6: the surplus lost when a team rides up (EDG-ECO-04), no Leave with party after DEMO COMPLETE and a host who leaves without a question (REG-MPG-09), the empty win screen (REG-LAT-14), items carried between floors (REG-FLW-07), the DEMO COMPLETE wording (FUN-ELV-13). (c) Confirm or change the performance targets in the table at the start of section 4. (d) Fix the stale README and comments of finding 1 to 4. (e) Real Steam runs with two and five accounts, and the IL2CPP build over real Steam, which the notes record no run of (PRF-STB-10, REG-MPG-01, REG-MPG-02). (f) A development build with `DemoLastFloor` 13 (E5) to reach the hidden elevator, the key, the boss and the win screen with a party (REG-LAT-08, REG-LAT-11 to REG-LAT-16).

### 2.40 Two fixes of 2026-10-21 (a Claude session, the owner at school): the doubled monitor on the outpost's computer desk, and a killed guard's death animation - EDITED ONLY: NEVER COMPILED OR RUN IN UNITY, NOT COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED

**The asks (the owner, 2026-10-21).** (1) "In the guard outpost there are two monitors inside each other for the screen you click E to get to the nav menu. There should only be one monitor there for right now." (2) "When a guard is killed they are doing the stun animation from the asset, not the dead animation from the asset. The guard should only do the stunned animation when melee attacked. The guard should continue to fight if shot until dead. Then when the guard is killed they do the death animation." (3) "Don't push any changes, I want to review first." No git command was run: the files below are changed in the working folder and nothing else is touched.

**Fix 1, the doubled monitor.** Cause: `Interior/OfficePlanner.Outpost.cs` `ThOutpost` puts two decorative monitors (and a keyboard and mouse) on each of the three monitoring desks (at u = -0.5 and u = 0.3 along the desk), and then puts the slot of the outpost's computer on the LAST desk at u = 0.5 (`TopSlot(..., "computer")`); `FloorGenerator.Outpost.cs` `MakeComputer` builds that computer with a monitor of its own (the 0.44 m frame, the screen, the post-it, `ComputerProp`). The desk's second monitor (0.66 m wide, at u = 0.3) therefore stood in the same place as the computer's monitor, at the same depth: two monitors inside each other. Change: the second monitor of each desk is now put on AFTER the loop, for every placed desk except the last one (so the computer's desk has the left-hand decorative monitor at u = -0.5 and the computer's own monitor at u = 0.5, and no monitor in between). If there is no `layout.outpost` the old behaviour (a second monitor on every desk) is kept. The middle desk still gets the old CRT (`monitor_old`) as its second monitor (it is remembered per desk in `deskNo`). `MakeComputer`, `ComputerProp` and the computer's slot are NOT changed. Nothing here uses a random number, no network id is taken (the desk props are cosmetic pieces, not networked), and `ValuablePlan` skips the outpost, so no other floor content moves; the only differences are one `Piece` less in the outpost room (`room.info.pieceCount`, which nothing reads) and the second monitors of the first two desks being created after the other desk props instead of before them. **The owner may want to know:** the last desk still has the decorative monitor on its left, next to the computer (that is two monitors on the desk, side by side, not inside each other). If "only one monitor there" meant the whole desk, delete the line `r.Top(desk, "monitor_new", -0.5f, -dd / 2 + 0.2f);` for `i == 2` (or skip it when `i == 2 && op != null`) and the desk keeps just the computer.

**Fix 2, the death animation.** Cause: `GuardAI.CheckDown` marks a killed guard `Dead = true` AND `State = GuardState.Stunned` (with `stunUntil = float.MaxValue`, so the AI, the gun, the vision and the sounds all leave him alone). `GuardVisual.Update` only looked at the state: `if (ai.State == GuardState.Stunned) { anim.Stun(); return; }`, so a dead guard played the dazed loop (head down, arms hanging) standing up, instead of the asset's death clip (the `Down` slot of `CharacterAnimator`, filled with the pack's `_death_a` clip). Change in `GuardVisual.cs`: one line before the stun check, `if (ai.Dead) { anim.Fall(); return; }`. `CharacterAnimator.Fall()` plays the death clip from the start once and holds its last frame (it does nothing if he is already down, and it takes him out of the dazed pose if he was dazed when he was killed, for example a melee-stunned guard who is then shot). A puppet (a client) gets `Dead` from the host's snapshot (`NetApply`), so every machine plays it. `GuardCorpse` only sinks the body after `GuardRules.CorpseSeconds` and does not lay it down, so nothing else tips him over. The Party-rig fallback (no Tiny Toon pack) plays its own fall trigger through the same `Fall()`.

**"Stun only on melee; a shot guard keeps fighting until dead": already how the code works, nothing changed for it.** The only things that stun a guard are `MeleeHitHost` (a weapon blow), `PunchHost` (a punch), a thrown item (`Throwable`), the `WeaponHitHost` that only tests use and the F1 test menu. `BulletHitHost` (called from `GunController` and `Net/RunSync.Guns.cs`) never stuns: it adds damage, cuts the radio call and any hold on a player (the owner's rule: a bullet on the pinning officer frees the player), kills him when the damage reaches `hitsToKill`, and otherwise `TurnToShooter` sends a guard who was not chasing to investigate the shot (a guard already chasing keeps chasing). So the only guards seen in the dazed pose after this fix are the ones hit by melee, a punch or a thrown item, and the dead ones play the death clip. If the owner finds a shot guard that does NOT keep fighting in play, the thing to look at is `TurnToShooter`/`CutRadioAndRelease` (a shot guard who was Calling or Pinning goes to Investigate, not straight to Chase).

**Files changed (all CRLF, kept):** `Assets/Scripts/GuardVisual.cs` (the class comment and one line), `Assets/Scripts/Interior/OfficePlanner.Outpost.cs` (`ThOutpost`: `deskNo`, the second-monitor loop moved after the desk loop), and this file. **Originals** are in `DevTools\QaBackups\before_guard_death_and_outpost_monitor_2026-10-21\` (same relative paths, plus `DEVELOPER_NOTES.md`): copy them back to undo.

**Not verified (tell the owner):** none of this was compiled or run in Unity (Unity is not in the Linux VM; the edits were checked by reading the code around them and the names they use). Please open the project, let it compile, and look at: (a) an outpost on a floor with a computer: only the computer's own monitor on the right of the last desk, with its post-it, E still opens Nav/Hacked; (b) kill a guard with a gun: the death clip plays once and he stays down until the body sinks; (c) hit one with a bat or a punch: the dazed loop, and he gets up; (d) melee-stun a guard and then shoot him dead: he drops into the death clip from the dazed pose; (e) the same on a client. The Editor tests `NetDevGuardCall` (a melee-knocked officer stands dazed) and `NetDevToon` (the dazed slot) are about melee stun and should still hold; no test was added for "a dead officer shows the Down slot (`DebugWeights()[3]` high, `[6]` low)", which would be the check to add.

### 2.41 The scan's glow by worth (the owner, 2026-10-21, after he played the 2.40 fixes: "it worked perfect") - EDITED ONLY: NEVER COMPILED OR RUN IN UNITY, NOT COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED

**The ask (the owner, 2026-10-21).** "Do a full search through all the loot. I only want the high valuables to glow gold including the safes. Make medium tier loot blue and the lowest tier loot glows green" and, a moment later, "keep the weapons glowing red". Before this, the Z scan coloured a mark by its KIND (2026-10-20): safes and valuables gold, unique items (weapons) red, cash green, so every desk valuable was gold.

**The search.** The only loot glow in the game is the scanner's (`LootScanner.OnGUI`: a soft glow, the diamond and the label chip, all drawn in one colour through walls); no loot has a world-space glow, a material glow or an outline (the "Glow" part in `FloorGenerator.Valuables.cs` is a phone's lit screen, and the lamps / beacons are not loot). Everything the scan can mark: the 13 small valuables (`Interior/Valuables.cs`, on desks and at the outposts), the big treasures (`HeavyLoot`, `FloorGenerator.LootTable` and the office PC / printer), the cash bundles and the gold watch in the floor safes, the side cash in the gun safes, melee weapons, guns and armour (all "unique items"), the shut floor safes (`FloorSafe`) and the outpost's gun safe (`GunSafeProp`). Not marked: ammo clips, things under $60 (a mug), anything in a pack or a hand.

**The rule (`LootScanRules.GlowOf`, new, pure, in `LootScanRules.cs`).** (1) A unique item (weapon, armour) is RED whatever it is worth. (2) A safe (floor safe or gun safe) is GOLD. (3) Everything else goes by the scan's existing worth tier (`LootScanRules.Tier(value, quota)`, 0 small / 1 good / 2 great / 3 jackpot, calibrated on the floor's quota so it is the same on every floor): tier 0 = GREEN, tier 1 = BLUE, tier 2 and 3 = GOLD (`BlueFromTier = 1`, `GoldFromTier = 2`). The value is the thing's worth NOW (`Pickup.Value`, so a treasure that has been knocked about and lost value can drop from gold to blue). On floor 1 the tiers start at $302 and $602 (a typical valuable is 3.5% of the quota, $150). What that gives (floor 1 numbers; the other floors scale with the quota): GREEN - Cash Envelope $150, Casino Chips and Wallet $250 (mostly), Gold Pen $300 (about half), a gun safe's side cash; BLUE - Smartphone $400, Cash Stack $400, Handheld Console $450, Headphones $350, a Gold Pen at the top of its spread, about half of the Tablets and a quarter of the Cameras; GOLD - Gold Watch $700, Camera $650, Tablet $550 (top of the spread), Laptop $1000, Jewelry Box $1200, every big treasure (Golden Stapler, Crystal Trophy, Espresso Machine, Prototype Server, Executive Safe, office PC, printer), the floor safe's cash bundles (about $1,075 each on floor 1), and every safe. Of the small valuables on a desk about 29% are green, 49% blue and 23% gold (by the catalog's shares, before room themes). **To make gold rarer** (only the jackpots: Jewelry Box, a good Laptop, the biggest treasures, the safes) set `GoldFromTier` to 3 and `BlueFromTier` to 2 in `LootScanRules.cs`: nothing else needs to change. (I chose "great" and up as gold because the scanner already treated a safe as tier 2 and a big treasure as at least tier 2; if the owner wanted fewer golds he can say so.)

**Colours (`LootScanRules.GlowRgb`):** gold (1.00, 0.80, 0.18) as before, red (0.95, 0.22, 0.22) as before, green (0.28, 0.92, 0.42) as the old cash green, blue (0.30, 0.62, 1.00) new (a clearer blue than the ring's cyan 0.55 / 0.95 / 1.0).

**Files changed (all CRLF, kept):** `Assets/Scripts/LootScanRules.cs` (the `ScanGlow` enum, `BlueFromTier` / `GoldFromTier`, `GlowOf`, `GlowRgb`), `Assets/Scripts/LootScanner.cs` (`Find.glow`, set in `MarkOne` from the thing's current value with `TierQuota()` - the old unmultiplied quota, or floor 1's where there is none, which is the boss floor -; `GlowColour` replaces `KindColour` for the glow, the diamond, the label chip and the off-screen arrows; `TryGetGlow` for tests; the old unused `TierColour` palette removed), `Assets/Scripts/FloorChecklist.cs` (comments only: the scan no longer glows by kind; `ChecklistRules.Rgb` is kept, the checklist still counts by kind and `NetDevTest` still checks it), `Assets/Editor/NetDev/NetDevChecklistTest.cs` (two new pure checks: the tier boundaries at $300 / $310 / $600 / $610, safe gold, weapon red, and that the four glows are gold, blue, green and red), and this file. Nothing else was touched: no art, no material, no shader, no text, the diamond sizes (`Find.tier`) and the checklist are unchanged. **Originals** are in `DevTools\QaBackups\before_loot_glow_tiers_2026-10-21\` (same relative paths, plus `DEVELOPER_NOTES.md`).

**Not verified (tell the owner):** none of this was compiled or run in Unity. The edits were checked by reading, by comparing every changed file with its backup, and by balancing the braces; the two new `NetDevTest` checks have not been run. To look at in play: hold Z in a room with a desk of valuables: the cheap ones (an envelope, a wallet) green, the middle ones blue, the watches, laptops and jewelry gold; a shut safe gold; a weapon or armour red; the cash bundles of an open safe gold; and the diamond and the label chip take the same colour as the glow. The "N valuables" line at the top of the scan and the journal's checklist are unchanged.

### 2.42 QA of the guards' hitboxes (the owner, 2026-10-21: "sometimes when you aim at a guard and attack with either bullets or a weapon the attack does not connect even though it definitely should be") - EDITED ONLY: NEVER COMPILED OR RUN IN UNITY, NOT COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED

A Claude session worked on this while the owner was at school. Nothing was run, so every cause below was found by reading the code and reasoning about Unity's physics rules, not by watching a miss happen; the owner should play the checklist at the end before trusting it.

**What a guard's hitbox is.** `FloorGenerator.SpawnGuard` makes a primitive Capsule (CapsuleCollider radius 0.5, height 2, centre 0, no Rigidbody; a NavMeshAgent moves it) and `GuardVisual` hangs the animated model on it, scaled by the guard's look (`PartyCharacterFactory.GuardLooks`: Brute 1.25 high, Creeper 1.2, Enforcer 1.05, Boss 1.10 and the object itself scaled 1.35). The collider was never fitted to the model.

**Causes found (a bullet's, then the weapon's):**

- **A. The bullet leaves the muzzle, the red crosshair is the camera's line.** `GunController.AimPoint` traces from the camera and turns the crosshair red on a guard; `Shoot` then traces from the *muzzle*, which is lower than the eye and to one side of it. A cubicle panel, a desk edge or a door frame can block the muzzle's line to a guard the camera clearly sees, so the crosshair said "hit" and the bullet hit the panel. Likeliest cause of the owner's report.
- **B. The muzzle inside a close guard.** A ray never reports a collider it starts inside. The muzzle is half a metre from the player and the guard's capsule is a metre wide, so a guard standing close (one pinning somebody, say) had the muzzle inside him and the bullet flew through him.
- **C. Tall guards' heads above the capsule.** The capsule is 2 m high at scale 1 but a Brute/Creeper/Boss model (with hat) is taller, so shots at the head or hat went over the collider.
- **D. Dead guards' hitboxes.** Since the 2.40 death-clip change a corpse lies down (the animation) while its 2 m capsule still stood: an invisible pillar that soaked bullets meant for a guard behind it, and a wall for walking. (Side effect of my own 2.40 change.)
- **E. The 48-entry hit buffer.** `RaycastNonAlloc` returns the *first* N colliders it finds, not the nearest N, when the line crosses more than the array holds; a rifle across a long floor (walls, desks, chairs) could leave the guard at the end out.

**Changes:**

- `Assets/Scripts/Guns/Ballistics.cs`: buffer 48 -> 192 (E); a new `inside` array and, at the start of `Trace` for a player's bullet (`!hitsPlayers`), an `Physics.OverlapSphereNonAlloc` of 2 cm at the origin: if a live guard (or the dummy) not part of the shooter is there, the bullet hits him at distance 0 (B); a dead guard is skipped in the loop like a player in custody (D).
- `Assets/Scripts/Guns/GunController.cs`: new private fields `aimStart`, `aimDir` and `CameraLineSlack = 3f`; `AimPoint(out bool foe)` keeps its signature (`NetDevTest` calls it by reflection with one `out bool`) and stores the camera line in those fields; in `Shoot`, when the crosshair is on a foe and the muzzle's own line reaches no guard/dummy, the bullets leave the *camera's* line instead, provided that line starts within 3 m of the shooter and nothing solid lies between the muzzle and where it starts (A). The tracer, sound and noise still use the muzzle; `RequestGunShot` is sent the origin actually used so the host traces the same line (the host's limit is `GunMuzzleSlack` = 4 m from the shooter, so 3 m leaves room for lag).
- `Assets/Scripts/GuardVisual.cs`: `FitHitbox()` (in `Start`, after the model is attached) grows the capsule *upward only* (the bottom stays on the floor) to the top of the model's enabled renderers, at most 0.7 m (C); when the guard dies the capsule is disabled (D).
- `Assets/Editor/NetDev/NetDevPew.cs`: block "5c", two checks: a bullet starting inside a live guard hits him; a trace through a dead guard from behind does not stop at him. Not run.
- This file.

**Melee: no definite bug found.** `MeleeController.Hit` is an `OverlapSphere` (radius 1.4) 1.2 m ahead of the player on the swing line after a 0.22 s delay; the punch is radius 1.0, reach 1.0, 0.15 s. Both cover a guard standing in front of the player; the host takes a client's swing within `GuardHitReach` 3.6 m with a 0.35 s rate limit. The melee gains from C (the head) and D (no pillar), and nothing else was changed. Possible remaining cause the owner may feel: the swing lands 0.15-0.22 s *after* the click, so a guard who walks or is knocked out of the sphere in that time is missed; on a lagging client the 3.6 m check can also refuse a swing. Neither was changed because it is a design choice (a longer delay or a bigger sphere would make swings hit through corners). If misses with melee still happen, note the weapon, the distance and whether the guard was moving.

**Not changed on purpose:** the guard's vision, `HasReach` and `GuardVision.Clip` skip colliders with an `attachedRigidbody` (things that move); the host-side `Forgiven()` 1 m forgiveness for a guard the shooter saw (only pellet 0); the guards' bullets (`GuardGun`, `hitsPlayers = true`, none of the new code runs for them); no art, material, shader, text or scene.

**Originals** are in `DevTools\QaBackups\before_guard_hitbox_fix_2026-10-21\` (same relative paths, plus `DEVELOPER_NOTES.md`). Its `GuardVisual.cs` is as it was *after* the 2.40 death-clip fix and before the hitbox edits.

**Not verified (tell the owner):** none of this was compiled or run in Unity. `Ballistics.cs` alone was compiled with stand-in Unity types (it builds; that only proves the C# is legal, not that it behaves); the other files were checked by reading, by comparing every changed file with its backup, and by balancing braces. To look at in play: shoot a guard standing right in front of you (pinning a teammate, or just next to you) at point-blank range; shoot a guard over a cubicle panel and around a door frame with the crosshair red; shoot the head or hat of a Brute and a Creeper and of the boss; kill a guard and shoot another one standing behind the corpse; walk through where a corpse lies (it should no longer block); and shoot from across the longest corridor of a floor. In a hosted game do the same as a client.

### 2.43 The owner's list of 2026-10-21 (seven asks): a shot officer calls for backup, the pistol's hands, bodies you can walk through, the Go click that fired a shot, treasures you can lift from both sides, the ping, the elevator countdown - BUILT, TESTED IN UNITY AND APPLIED (a Windows session with Unity: the work was done in a scratch copy of the real project and applied with backups)

**The asks (the owner, 2026-10-21, verbatim).** (1) "Please implement officer's should start calling for backup after being shot and if a player eliminates them in time the call will be cancelled". (2) "Also the first person mode for the pistol has the character's hands somewhat visible and blocking the weapon model a bit, please make the hands not visible for the pistol similar to the rifle". (3) "A eliminated officer should remain on the ground but can now be walked through". (4) "When you click go on the elevator menu you fire a shot from your gun that should not be happening." (5) (with a picture of the server room: a yellow box between two racks) "In the screenshot attached there is a object that two people can pick up together but it is not accessible from both sides." (6) "Add a ping system. Players can ping my pressing down on the scroll wheel and whatever is in their cross hairs will be the target. The ping should only work on lootable items, comsumable items, weapons and doors." (7) "When someone leaves the elevator while the countdown is active, the countdown should reset(cancel) and then when they get back in the elevator a player should have to click it the elevator button again". And: "A lot has changed since you last worked on the project, only work on what i just sent you please" / "Catch yourself up on where the project is at now".

**State: BUILT, TESTED IN UNITY (a Windows session with Unity, in a scratch copy of the real project: nothing was run on the real project) and APPLIED to the real project with backups in `DevTools\QaBackups\before_asks_2026-10-21`.** No build was made: the newest build is still `Playtest_2026-10-20b`, which has none of this and a different protocol (this work raised `NetConst.ProtocolVersion` to "27": old and new builds cannot share a run). No git command was run; nothing is committed.

**1. A shot officer calls for backup; killing him in time cancels the call** (`GuardAI.BulletHitHost`, new `BeginShotCall`, `CheckDown`). A bullet that does not put him down makes him stop where he is and radio for `callSeconds` (2 s, the same ring over his head, the same radio sounds as a sighting), whether or not he sees anybody; when the call is through `HeistManager.CallBackup(this)` sends the officers (2 the first time on a floor, then the backup clock) and `AfterGetUpCall` sends him after whoever shot him (chase if that player is free, else investigate). The bullet that kills him clears the call in `CheckDown` (`calling`, `callTimer`, `callAnyway`, `callOnGetUp`): no ring, no backup, no officers. A second bullet during the call neither restarts nor stops it (and if he was in a sighting call, it no longer needs the player in view). Not started by a bullet: an officer who is already chasing (his chase began with a call, so his backup is on the way), one who is knocked over (he calls on getting up as before), the boss, one who is not on the NavMesh. A held player is still freed by the bullet (the owner's 2026-10-19 rule). A melee blow or a punch still cuts a call (and he calls again on getting up). An armed officer lowers his gun while he radios (GuardGun only raises it in Chase) and then chases: shooting an officer in a fight makes him stop for 2 s. Tunable: `GuardAI.callSeconds`. In numbers: the pistol (guardHits 4) kills an unshielded officer in 3 shots (0.6 s after the first at 0.3 s intervals), a light shield 4 shots, a medium shield 5, a HEAVY shield needs 9 shots (2.4 s: that call cannot be cut in time with the pistol, which is the pistol against heavy armour). **SUPERSEDES** these older statements: 2.35 ("a bullet does not knock over, so it starts no call"), 2.39 research digest and 2.40 ("a bullet cuts his radio call"): a bullet no longer cuts the call; the QA plan's cases that quote it need the same correction. Tests: scenario `Asks1021` (a shot starts the call at once; a second shot does not reset it; four shots in 0.9 s cancel it: no backup and no officers 3 s later; a single shot's call goes through: backup called, 4 -> 6 officers, he chases the shooter; a melee blow still cuts it) and the updated `GuardCall` scenario (13 PASS: "a bullet starts no call" became "a bullet starts a call").

**2. The pistol's hands are not shown in first person** (`HeldGun.ShowPistolArm = false`; `LocalArmMode` is 1 only when it is true). The owner's 2026-10-20 night ask ("for the pistol make an arm extended and a hand holding the gun") is switched OFF behind that flag, not deleted: the arm is still posed (out of sight; it still casts its shadow) and the pistol is held at arm's length like before, now alone in the picture as the rifle is. Set the flag to true to bring the arm back. `CameraFollow`'s comment follows. Tests: `FpLook` has a new check ("Pew: no hands in the picture", arm mode 0, arms shown false) and its old arms check follows the flag; picture `%TEMP%\be_shot_fplook_Pew.png` (the pistol seen end-on, nothing else of the body).

**3. A dead officer lies there and can be walked through** (`GuardAI.SettleBody`, called from `LateUpdate` on every machine once `Dead`): every collider of his body is switched off. 2.42 had already switched the standing capsule off in `GuardVisual.Update`, but only when the officer has a model; now `GuardAI` itself does it, so it holds for every officer, on the host and on the puppets (the snapshot sets `Dead`). The body stays where it fell, in the asset's death clip (the `Down` slot, 2.40), until `GuardCorpse` sinks it after `GuardRules.CorpseSeconds` (15 s) as the owner asked on 2026-10-18: I read "should remain on the ground" as "keep lying there, do not vanish early" and left that timer alone; if the owner wants bodies to stay for the whole floor, make `GuardCorpse` skip the sink. Tests (`Asks1021`): all of the body's colliders are off, he is still there, the Down slot weighs 1.0 (the dazed one 0), a capsule cast through him from all four sides meets nothing of him, a ray through him is not stopped by him, two seconds later he is still lying there; picture `%TEMP%\be_shot_asks_corpse.png`.

**4. Clicking GO no longer fires the gun** (new `Guns/TriggerLatch.cs`, `GunController`). Cause: the elevator panel closes on the click (the ride begins: `ElevatorPanel.Update` closes it as soon as `Sealing`), the mouse button is still held down, and in the next frame `GunController.CanUse()` is true again and `Input.GetMouseButton(0)` is "held fire". Now a button that is DOWN while the gun cannot be used (a menu, the shop, the pack, the elevator panel, the free cursor, hands full, the dart game) latches, and the latch opens only when the button is up; a held button repeats as before. The same fix covers every other menu that closes on a click. Test hooks: `GunController.TestTrigger` (a test's own fire button) and `TestCursorLocked` (a batch run cannot lock the cursor), `TriggerLatched`. Tests: 7 pure checks (`TriggerLatch`) and the scenario drives it for real: panel open + button held -> no shot, panel closes with the button still held -> no shot and the latch is on, button up -> latch off, new press -> shot.

**5. A treasure for two or three on the floor can be reached from both sides** (`Interior/LootPlan.cs`, `FloorGenerator.Interior.cs PlaceLootFromLayout`). The owner's picture is the prototype server in the server room: the planner put its 1.5 m square against the east racks, so the racks stood 0.2 m from it on one or both sides and only one end was open. `LootPlan.RoomForCarry` (now `RoomForCarry(layout, slot, lifters, sizeX, sizeZ)`) asked for room on ONE side and did not know the room's walls (the maze's cell walls are not in `Layout.walls`). New rule for a thing standing on the FLOOR (the server, 2 carriers; the executive safe, 3): its box clear of furniture and walls; a spot for one person (a free disc of `StandRadius` 0.35 m, 0.40 m for three) `StandOffset` 0.45 m beyond each face, inside the room (the room's edge counts as a wall: `EdgeClearance`); two carriers need an OPPOSITE pair, three need two sides (any two: three people cannot all stand opposite each other); and a second person must be able to WALK to the far side: `WalkGrid` floods the room's floor (0.25 m cells, a person's 0.3 m half width kept clear of furniture, walls, the edge and the box) from a spot just inside every doorway and the room's guard stop (`RoomInfo.wx/wz`) with the box in place, and both spots must be reached - this is what rules out the owner's aisle (the box fills it, the room behind it is a dead end). A thing on a counter keeps the old rule (one side, 1.15 m, the carriers stand side by side in front). A treasure whose planned spot fails is MOVED by `LootPlan.TryFindCarrySpot` to the nearest spot of its own room (0.25 m grid, up to 3.5 m, nearest first, no randomness: every machine finds the same one; its box clear of furniture and walls, 1 m clear of the room's doorways, the guards' stops and `layout.clear`) where the rule holds and, for a MOVED treasure only, where the floor's NavMesh is (open ground an agent of radius 0.5 can reach within 2 m of it: `onNavMesh` in `RoomForCarryCore`; the LootQa rule "loot on show can be walked to" counts exactly that, and a spot the planner chose is left as it was so the safes in their corner pockets are not lost for it); if there is none it is left out as before (`[Loot] floor N: X treasures placed, Y moved ..., Z spots skipped` in the log). Measured on 15 real floors (`Asks1021Test`): 187 treasures for two or three stand on the floor; the rule of before (one open side) would have kept 95; now 58 are fine as planned, 89 are moved and 40 are left out (servers 24 / 81 / 18, safes 34 / 8 / 22): more are kept than before, each with two reachable spots. In narrow server rooms (3.1 m wide, racks on both long walls: a 1.1 m aisle) nowhere has room, so there the server is left out (a floor holds fewer "Prototype Server"s); to keep it there, give those rooms fewer racks in `OfficeThemes.ThServer` (it would change the random sequence of that room's floor) - not done. Floors are built differently, so the protocol is "27". Tests: 14 pure checks (the owner's aisle refused, a dead-end aisle refused, a wide aisle fine, a server in a pocket moved, the room edge as a wall, a corner fine for three and not for two, the narrow room loses it, the old counter rule kept, the real floors by kind) and, in Play Mode on floors 1 and 2, a physical check with a person-sized capsule: every treasure standing on the floor has its carrier spots free (`SpotBlocker` names whatever stands in the way); pictures `%TEMP%\be_shot_asks_server_f*_*.png`.

**6. The ping** (new `Ping/PingRules.cs`, `Ping/PingSystem.cs`, `Net/RunSync.Ping.cs`; `SoundBank`/`OutpostSound`: `SoundId.Ping`; `KeybindScreen`, `HowToPlayContent`). Click the mouse wheel (middle mouse button, `PingRules.MouseButton` 2): the thing the crosshair is on gets a marker that the whole team sees through walls for 8 s (`PingRules.Seconds`; in at once, out over the last 1.2 s; a little pop and a spreading ring as it goes up). What can be pinged (the owner: "lootable items, consumable items, weapons and doors"): any item lying about (`Pickup`): loot (worth `PingRules.LootMinValue` $50 or more: "Gold Watch  $700", a heavy one "Prototype Server  $2,500  (x2 carry)"), weapons and armour (gear: melee and ranged weapons, `ArmourItem`: I counted armour as a weapon), spare clips and anything worth less than $50 (consumables); and a doorway (`DoorManager`, open or shut, "Door" / "Locked door"; the elevator's own doors are not in the door manager and cannot be pinged). Never a wall, a desk, a guard, a player, a safe or a keypad. The aim is `PingSystem.TryAim`: the camera's ray from the player's own depth (like the crosshair), the first wall or desk in the way hides what is behind it, items are aimed at by their box grown with the distance, up to `MaxRange` 80 m; the nearest item or door wins. The marker (`OnGUI`, depth 30, under the HUD) is a diamond in the pinger's colour (`HudTheme.PlayerColour`) with a sign ($ loot, ! gear, + used up, D door), the name chip above and "PLAYER n   12 m" below, an arrow on the edge of the screen when it is out of view, and it follows an item that is pushed and goes when the item is taken, stored, carried, destroyed or the floor ends; one marker per player (a new ping replaces it; half a second between a player's pings). Not while a menu / the shop / the elevator panel / a screen is up, the cursor is free, or the player is pinned or busted. Network: the pinger's machine shows its marker at once and (online) `RunSync.RequestPing(kind, id, pos)` -> the host checks (a real sender, a finite point within `MaxRange` + 10 m of the sender's body, not in custody, its own `RateLimiter` of a burst of 3 and 1.5 a second per player on top of the general request limit) -> `PingedRpc(slot, kind, id, pos)` to everybody, which `PingSystem.OnPinged` shows (the pinger's own machine skips it); the id is the item's `NetId` or the door's index, so the label is looked up on each machine; offline (PLAY SOLO) only the player's own marker shows. Sound: `SoundId.Ping` (appended at the END of the enum and of the variants list; generated: two short notes, A5 then E6, `OutpostSound.Ping`), 2D, the pitch rises a little with the pinger's slot. The words on the Keybinds screen ("MMB  ping loot, gear, doors (wheel)") and the How to Play line are PLACEHOLDERS for the owner to rewrite. Protocol "27" (a new message). Tests: 13 pure checks (`PingRules`, the clip's spectrum by Goertzel, the key lists, the protocol), the Play Mode `Asks1021` (a ping on a valuable, a spare clip, a gun, a door, none through a wall, none at a wall / the sky / beyond 80 m, the marker, the cooldown, replacement, a teammate's marker, taken / destroyed items, the 8-second end), the real-window `PingUi` (pictures `%TEMP%\be_ui_ping_*.png`) and the two-machine `PingNet`.

**7. The elevator countdown is cancelled when somebody steps out** (`ElevatorExtraction.Seal`, `CancelCountdown`, `CancelledText`). The "Doors closing in 2 / 1" countdown used to check `EveryoneAboard` only once, at its end (so somebody who stepped out and came back in time did not matter, and somebody still out when it ended aborted it). Now `Seal` looks every frame: the moment `EveryoneAboard` is false the countdown is cancelled for everybody (`ElevatorExtraction.CancelledText`, "Countdown cancelled - someone left the elevator. Click GO again": a PLACEHOLDER text), the doors stay open, `Sealing` is false (the panel can be used again) and the floor stays chosen on the panel; nothing restarts when they get back in: a player has to click GO again. Stepping out and straight back in inside the two seconds also cancels it. Tests (`Asks1021`, the real Docks elevator, the player teleported out and in): GO starts it, half a second in it still runs, one step out cancels it at once, the destination is still chosen, three and a half seconds after they are back nothing has started and the car has not moved, GO again starts it, a quick step out and in cancels it too, and with everybody inside for the whole countdown the ride goes.

**Tests, in numbers (scratch copy of the real project, Unity 6000.0.45f1).** Pure tests 647 PASS, 0 FAIL (the real project before: 625). Scenarios (0 FAIL each): `Asks1021` 57 checks (elevator 12, gun click 6, officer calls 8, corpse 7, treasures 2 floors, ping 22); `GuardCall` 13; `FpLook` 15; `PingUi` 1 (+ 3 pictures); `PingNet` and the regression of the other scenarios: see the line "REGRESSION" at the end of this section.

**Not verified (tell the owner).** The mouse wheel click on a real mouse (the tests call the same code: `PingSystem.PingFromView`); the sound of the ping (generated; its spectrum is checked, nobody has heard it); the look of the markers at sizes other than 1920 x 888 and over bright walls (the picture shows markers close together overlapping their names); a real Steam run of the ping; the whole list on floors 5 to 13 (the scenarios ran on floors 0 to 2); the corpse's death clip on a puppet client (the snapshot sets `Dead`, the same code runs, but no client looked at it). The server room is not rebuilt: see item 5. The two-machine scenarios GunNet, DemoNet (the client's side) and OutpostNet fail with and without this work (see REGRESSION below): they were not looked into.

**Files changed** (all CRLF kept): `Scripts/GuardAI.cs`, `Guns/HeldGun.cs`, `CameraFollow.cs` (a comment), `Guns/GunController.cs`, new `Guns/TriggerLatch.cs`, `ElevatorExtraction.cs`, `Interior/LootPlan.cs`, `FloorGenerator.Interior.cs`, new `Ping/PingRules.cs` and `Ping/PingSystem.cs`, new `Net/RunSync.Ping.cs`, `Net/NetConst.cs` ("27"), `Audio/SoundBank.cs`, `Audio/OutpostSound.cs`, `Hud/KeybindScreen.cs`, `HowToPlayContent.cs`; Editor: `NetDev/NetDevAsks1021.cs` (the Play Mode scenario), `NetDevAsks1021Test.cs` (the pure checks), `NetDevPingNet.cs`, `NetDevPingUi.cs`, `NetDevGuardCall.cs`, `NetDevCombat.cs`, `NetDevFpLook.cs`, `NetDevHarness.cs` (entry points `Asks1021`, `PingUi`, `PingNet`), `NetDevTest.cs` (one hook line). New files have their `.meta` files. **Originals** of every replaced file are in `DevTools\QaBackups\before_asks_2026-10-21\` (same relative paths).

**Where the tools of this session are:** `DevTools\QaScripts\asks_2026-10-21\` (the patch scripts p1 to p25, `apply1021.ps1` - copies only the files of this section, refuses when the real file has changed since, backs up -, `runmp.ps1` / `mp_baseline.ps1` for two machines, `qa_regress.ps1`, `final_regress.ps1`, `build.ps1`, `ui_dev.ps1`, `touch.ps1`, the master texts of the scenarios and the regression summaries); the pictures are in `DevTools\Pictures_2026-10-21\`.
