They are tests of the pew's earlier rules (the pew lies on floor 3 now, the vendor's clip price is 120) or older faults in the networked store / ammunition path; nobody has looked at them since. Left as they are: they are not part of the owner's list of 2026-10-21.

**One failure of this work's own test, found and fixed on the way:** the first final run of `PingNet` failed ("timeout waiting for the client's ping"): the test pinged the item with the lowest network id, which on some seeds lies more than 90 m from the elevator, where the host correctly refuses a ping (`PingRules.HostAccepts`: 80 m of range plus 10 m of slack; a floor is 144 m x 90 m). The test now uses the two valuables nearest the elevator (`NetDevPingNet.TwoValuables`); the game was not at fault. Three runs in a row passed after that.

### 2.44 A playtest build with the F1 cheat panel (the owner, 2026-10-21 evening: "make me a build ... I want to be able to click F1 in game to use the editor mode in the build") - EDITED ONLY: NEVER COMPILED OR RUN IN UNITY, NOT COMMITTED

**The problem.** `TestMode` (the F1 cheat panel: cash, weapons, loot, floor jumps, god mode) only existed in the Unity Editor: `Boot` returned unless `Application.isEditor` and the menu switch (`Employees Take Revenge > Test Mode`) was on. A built game never had it.

**The change.** `Assets/Scripts/TestMode.cs`: `Boot` now asks a new `Wanted()`: in the Editor it is the same menu switch as before; in a built game it is true when a file `testmode.txt` lies next to the exe, or the game is started with `-be-testmode`. So a build for the owner gets the file and has F1; a build for friends does not get it and has no cheats (do not put the file into a friends' build; the safety scan of `final_build.ps1` would stop on it as an unexpected top-level entry). The panel still shuts itself while a networked game is on (`NetSession.Online`: the cheats create things the sync does not know about), so F1 works in a solo run, not in a Steam lobby run. Nothing else of the panel was changed (its "weapons / guns not set up" notes depend on the resources of the build, as before).

**The build.** Unity does not run in the Claude workspace, so a script does it: `DevTools\QaScripts\build_playtest_f1.bat` (double-click it; the Unity Editor must be CLOSED on the project, because it builds the project itself in batch mode, with the project's own Library): runs `Unity.exe -batchmode -nographics -executeMethod BuildingEscape.EditorTools.DemoBuild.Build` (6000.0.45f1 at `C:\Program Files\Unity\Hub\Editor\`), output `Builds\Playtest_F1_2026-10-21\EmployeesTakeRevenge.exe` (it refuses to overwrite a folder), writes `testmode.txt` next to the exe, removes the `*DoNotShip*` Burst folder, makes `Builds\Playtest_F1_2026-10-21.zip`. What happened goes to `Builds\build_playtest_f1_status.txt` and Unity's log to `Builds\build_playtest_f1.log` (Builds is git-ignored). `ProjectSettings` is not changed by it (the product name was already "Employees Take Revenge"). **The build is also the first compile in Unity of everything written since the owner last had the Editor open (2.41's loot glow, 2.42's hitbox fix): a compile error shows in the log as `error CS`.**

**Originals** are in `DevTools\QaBackups\before_f1_in_build_2026-10-21\` (TestMode.cs and this file). Not compiled, not run.

**UPDATE 2026-10-22 (the owner: "I only wanted the F1 for that build anyway"):** the `TestMode.cs` change above was TAKEN OUT of the project again (the original is back, so the repo has no F1-in-builds code and no build made from it will have F1). The changed file is kept for the next time a build needs F1: `DevTools\QaBackups\f1_in_build_patch_2026-10-21\TestMode.cs` (copy it over `Assets\Scripts\TestMode.cs`, build, put `testmode.txt` next to the exe). The playtest build already made (`Downloads\Playtest_F1_2026-10-21`) keeps its F1. `build_playtest_f1.bat` only works with that patch applied.

### 2.46 A crowbar that snapped on a safe stayed in the hand (the owner, 2026-10-22: "the crowbar still stays in your hands animation wise even though its not actually there. I was swinging with what I thought was a crow bar but it was actually my fists") - EDITED ONLY: NEVER COMPILED OR RUN IN UNITY

**Cause.** `FloorSafe.UseUp` removes the snapped crowbar from the pack (`Equipped` becomes null: the swings were punches, as the owner felt) and destroys it. `MeleeController.Update` only rebuilt the hand model when `eq != shown`; `shown` still pointed at the destroyed crowbar, and Unity treats a destroyed object as equal to null, so `null != destroyed` is false: the old model was never removed. **Fix** (one line, `Assets/Scripts/MeleeController.cs`): `if (eq != shown || (heldModel && !shown)) Show(eq);` - a model that is shown for a weapon that no longer exists is taken away (the first-person arm follows: `FirstPersonMelee` ignores a destroyed model). Original: `DevTools\QaBackups\before_broken_crowbar_in_hand_2026-10-22\`. Not compiled, not run. Not checked: other machines of a networked run (they follow `BroadcastConsumed`); the same pattern (`!=` against a destroyed object) may sit in `GunController` for a gun that is destroyed in the hand - not looked at.

**UPDATE 2026-10-22 (the owner, two small things):** (1) the test menu's safe list no longer says "a pistol" / "cash" after each safe (it is now "F4 safe 2/3: room 491, code 6417, (code on record)"). (2) The bottom lines of the safe's keypad ("your notes say the code is ...", "Esc - step back") lay under the item shortcut bar: the bar is not drawn while the safe panel is up (`Backpack.DrawHud` asks `SafePanel.Instance.Showing`, which is public now). Originals: `DevTools\QaBackups\before_broken_crowbar_in_hand_2026-10-22\Assets\Scripts\` (TestMode.cs, SafePanel.cs; Backpack.cs was not copied: the change is the one `if` around `HudWidgets.WeaponBar` in `DrawHud`). Not compiled, not run.

**UPDATE 2 2026-10-22 (the owner):** the test menu's "Empty pack (drop everything)" dropped the weapons too; it is now "Drop all loot" and drops only the pieces that are loot (`Pickup.Loot != null`), one by one with `Backpack.Drop`; weapons, armour and other equipment stay in the pack. `Backpack.DropAll()` itself is unchanged. Original: `DevTools\QaBackups\before_broken_crowbar_in_hand_2026-10-22\Assets\Scripts\TestMode_before_drop_loot.cs`. Not compiled, not run.

**UPDATE 3 2026-10-22 (the owner: "when clicking the drop everything button it also drops weapons ... change the name to drop all loot and have it only drop the loot in the inventory"):** this is the red button in the Tab window. It is now "DROP ALL LOOT" and `Backpack.DropAll()` drops only pieces with `Pickup.Loot` (weapons, armour, keys, ammunition and the rest stay in the pack); the networked path (`RunSync.HostDropAll`) filters the same way. The test menu's "Empty pack (drop everything)" is now "Drop all loot" and calls the same method (the per-piece loop of the update above was taken out). `DropAll` has no other caller. ### 2.47 The red dot: smaller and in the middle of the sight's window (the owner, 2026-10-22, with a picture through the SMG's sight: "make the red dot a little smaller and more centered in the red dot scope") - EDITED ONLY: NEVER COMPILED OR RUN IN UNITY

`RangedWeapon.AddDot` (`Assets/Scripts/Guns/RangedWeapon.cs`): the dot's size went from 0.008 to 0.006 of the gun's scale, and its height from 0.6 of the sight model's height (box centre + 10%) to 0.715. Reason: the sight model's vertices were read from the FBX (in cm): the foot is 0 to 2.78, the hexagonal window 2.78 to 6.46, so the window's middle is at 4.62 = 0.715 of 6.46; the old dot at 3.88 cm lay 0.74 cm below it, which is the offset seen in the picture (the dot is always at the middle of the screen, so the ring looked pushed up). The first-person view places the gun by the dot (`HeldGun.FindSight` uses `RedDot/Dot`), so the ring is now round the dot. The depth of the dot (z) is unchanged: the box centre, which lies at the front edge of the window, not in its middle (the window's tube is 0 to 4.6 cm of the model's z; I could not tell from here whether Unity flips z on import, so I left it). It applies to every gun with a sight (SMG, rifle, shotgun). Original: `DevTools\QaBackups\before_red_dot_smaller_2026-10-22\Assets\Scripts\Guns\RangedWeapon.cs`. Not compiled, not run.

### 2.48 Health-based guard combat: melee weapons take HP off a guard (the owner, 2026-10-22: "right now melee weapons are not killing guards which is not the intent ... health based system for guards. Melee weapons should deal a specific amount of damage") - EDITED; THE PURE RULES WERE RUN OUTSIDE UNITY (11 checks pass) AND THE GAME ASSEMBLY WAS COMPILED OUTSIDE UNITY (0 errors); NEVER COMPILED BY UNITY ITSELF, NEVER RUN IN UNITY

**Root cause.** Since 2026-10-19 ("Melee weapons knock over a guard one shot ...", Q3 near line 1249, and the "starting points for the combat model" note near line 1313: both still describe the OLD rule) `GuardAI.MeleeHitHost` only called `Stun()` for a normal guard: `if (IsBoss) { Damage += 1f; ... }` was the only place a blow touched the guard's damage count. `CheckDown()` (the one place a guard dies) tests `Damage >= hitsToKill`, and only bullets (`BulletHitHost`), the legacy `WeaponHitHost` and the boss ever raised `Damage` - so no number of crowbar, bat or hatchet blows could put a guard down. That was deliberate then; the owner's 2026-10-22 messages replace it.

**What it is now.**
- A base guard has **100 HP** = `hitsToKill` (10, now `GuardHealthRules.BaseHitsToKill`) x `GuardHealthRules.HpPerHit` (10 HP). Read-only `GuardAI.MaxHealth` and `GuardAI.Health` show it (the host's / offline game's numbers; a client's guard is a puppet and only learns alive / dead from the snapshot). The boss keeps `hitsToKill = 30` (300 HP) and his old rule (a blow is one of his thirty hits) until his floor is redone.
- **Damage per hit** (ONE place: `Assets/Scripts/GuardHealthRules.cs`): crowbar **15** (7 hits), baseball bat **25** (4 hits), hatchet **35** (3 hits). Crowbar < bat < hatchet. They reach the weapons as `MeleeWeapon.Spec.guardDamage` (new field, `Specs` table in `MeleeWeapon.cs`); `Spec.damage` (14 / 11 / 22) is still what the training dummy and a PVP rival lose and was NOT touched (there the crowbar hits harder than the bat, the opposite of the guard order: the owner did not ask).
- `MeleeController.Hit` passes `spec.guardDamage` to `GuardAI.MeleeHit(stun, by, damage)` -> `MeleeHitHost`, which adds `HpToHits(damage)` to `Damage` (`GuardHealthRules.DamageAfterBlow`) and calls `CheckDown()`. The blow that takes the last HP puts him down for good (the fall, the key and clip drops, the boss win - once); any other blow still knocks him over for the weapon's own time (crowbar 4 s, bat 3 s, hatchet 6 s), cuts his radio call and frees a player he holds, exactly as before.
- Bullets and blows take from the SAME pool (a pew bullet is 4 hits = 40 HP; the guns kill as before: 3 pew bullets, 2 SMG pairs ...). One pew bullet and then four crowbar blows put a guard down. Health never regenerates (`Damage` is only ever raised, in three places).
- **No group-size input anywhere.** The damage is a constant of the weapon, the HP is `hitsToKill` x 10, and nothing reads how many guards are about, alerted or attacking (only the boss differs). A swing that finds three guards takes the same off each of them.
- **Each blow lands once.** `MeleeController.Hit` runs once per swing (one `Physics.OverlapSphere`; a `seen` set per guard, so a guard's several colliders count once; `swinging` and `nextSwing` block a second swing during the first). A swing with nothing in the zone finds no guard and does no damage. `MeleeHitHost` and `CheckDown` return at once for a guard who is already down (no more damage, no second death, no second drop), and a dead guard's colliders are off anyway.
- **A client's blow (network):** `RunSync.HostGuardHit` takes the damage from the weapon the HOST sees in that player's hand (`weapon.Data.guardDamage`; a client never sends a number). Its 0.35 s rate limit was per player, so on a client a swing that found two guards hurt only the first (a group-size effect!); it is now per player AND guard (`key = (who << 16) | id`, the table is cleared above 256 entries): every guard in the swing is hit once, and a repeat request for the same guard inside 0.35 s counts once.

**Tests.**
- **RUN, outside Unity:** `Assets/Editor/NetDev/NetDevMeleeHealthTest.cs` (`NetDevTest.MeleeHealthTest()`, called from `NetDevTest.cs` after `Asks1021Test()`). I compiled that very file with Roslyn against the real `GuardHealthRules.cs`, `GunRules.cs` and `SoundSynth.cs`, the real spec tables (their text is cut out of `MeleeWeapon.cs` / `RangedWeapon.cs` by a script) and a stand-in for `Mathf` / `Debug`: **11 of 11 checks pass** (100 HP; 15 / 25 / 35; crowbar < bat < hatchet; 7 / 4 / 3 hits; blow by blow health falls by exactly the weapon's damage, no reset, down at the 7th / 4th / 3rd hit and not before; the same in crowds of 1, 3, 8 and 24 and a blow on one guard leaves the others alone; health never below 0; a zero-damage blow changes nothing; pew 40 HP and three bullets still kill; bullet + crowbar blows share one pool; dummy / PVP damage untouched). Four deliberately broken copies of the rules (hatchet 34, a blow that replaces instead of adds, negative health, 12 hits) were each caught. All edited files were also run through Roslyn's SYNTAX check (no errors).
- **COMPILED, outside Unity (the owner's Unity install cannot be shared with the session, so the DLLs of the playtest build `Builds\Demo_2026-10-21_1854\EmployeesTakeRevenge_Data\Managed` and the `Library\Bee\artifacts\...\*.ref.dll` were used as references):** all 226 source files of `Assembly-CSharp` (every runtime edit of 2026-10-22 and the new `GuardHealthRules.cs`; the file list is Unity's own, from `Library\Bee\artifacts\1900b0aE.dag\Assembly-CSharp.rsp`, plus the one new file) with the .NET 8 SDK's Roslyn at `-langversion:9.0`: **0 errors**. Limits: it is the player profile (`UNITY_EDITOR` is not defined, so the `#if UNITY_EDITOR` blocks of 15 files are skipped - none of them is a file edited on 2026-10-22), and it is not Unity's own compiler or analyzers. The 74 `Editor\NetDev` files were compiled the same way with small stand-ins for the UnityEditor calls: **no error on any line changed on 2026-10-22** (five mistakes injected on purpose into those lines were each caught); the other errors there are the missing UnityEditor stand-ins and the `#if UNITY_EDITOR` test hooks of other files (`WobblyPlayer.testMove`, `GunSafeHackPanel.TestPointer` ...), so it is not a full compile of the Editor assembly. A compile that stops early looks clean: the first try (all 97 Editor files) reported only missing-type errors and did not even look inside the method bodies, which is why the injected-mistake check was done.
- **Edited but NOT RUN (they need Unity Play Mode):** `NetDevCombat.cs` (the melee block: 100 HP at the start, one blow takes exactly 15 / 25 / 35, the same guards go down after exactly 7 / 4 / 3 blows, five more blows on a dead guard change nothing), `NetDevR7.cs` (the old "twelve blows never knock out" is now six crowbar blows leave 10 HP and the seventh kills), `NetDevMeleeNet.cs` (health after the first and second blow). `NetDevAsks1021`, `NetDevGuardCall` and the "blow while he pins a player" check call `MeleeHitHost(stun, by)` with no damage = "a blow that only knocks over", which still works as they expect.
- **Not tested by anyone yet:** a swing in the game itself against one guard and against several (initial HP, per-hit damage, death on the 7th / 4th / 3rd hit, a miss, a client's blow on the host, the crowbar still opening a safe).

**Things to know.** (1) The crowbar's SAFE opening (`FloorSafe`, `SafeRules`) was not touched and does not read `guardDamage`. (2) Each blow resets the guard's stun to its full time, so a 7-blow crowbar combo (one blow every 0.9 s) keeps him on the floor the whole time; stop for longer than the stun and he gets up (and radios), with the damage kept. (3) Shields do not reduce melee, as before. (4) The Vendor shop blurbs and a few comments in `RangedWeapon.cs` still say "a melee hit counts as one" / "stuns a guard 4 s" - words only. (5) On a client the sound of a blow uses the puppet's `Dead` flag, which only changes with the snapshot, so the "killed" variant of the hit sound may play a moment late there. Originals: `DevTools\QaBackups\before_guard_health_melee_2026-10-22\` (GuardAI, MeleeWeapon, MeleeController, RunSync.Requests, NetDevCombat, NetDevR7, NetDevMeleeNet, NetDevTest, this file).

### 2.49 Esc did not close the gun safe's hack screen (the owner, 2026-10-22: "escape is not closing the menu out when you go to hack the chest to get a gun from the outpost") - EDITED; COMPILED OUTSIDE UNITY (0 errors), NEVER RUN IN UNITY

**Cause.** `GunSafeHackPanel` (`Assets/Scripts/Outpost/GunSafeHack.cs`) backs out through `BackOutReason()` -> `session.Release()` -> `End(false, reason, false)`. `Release()` only lets go of a stroke in progress; a `GunSafeHackSession` is `Finished` only when it was won or a wall was hit. So after Esc (or walking off, or a jam) the session was still live: `OnGUI` draws any session that is not `Finished`, so the screen stayed up (with the mouse locked again behind it, `OutpostUi.Hide` having run), and `Running` stayed true, so `Begin` -> `Start` returned at once and the safe could not be hacked again. The computer's `HackPanel` does not have the bug: it calls `session.Cancel()`, which finishes its session.
**Fix** (one line, in `End`): when `report` is false (a back-out) the panel drops its session: `session = null; safe = null; trail.Clear(); wasDown = false;`. A won / hit session still stays up for `ResultSeconds` to show the result, as before.
**Test** (edited, NOT run): the gun safe scenario of `NetDevR7.cs` has two new checks after "the alarm panel calms it down": walking away beyond `GunSafeProp.HackReach` takes the screen away at once (`!Running`, `Session == null`, `!OutpostUi.Open`), and the hack can be started again at once. (A batch run has no keyboard, and the panel ignores Esc while `TestPointer` is set, so the test backs out by walking off; Esc, a jam and "player hidden / in custody" all go through the same `End`.)
**Compiled outside Unity** the way 2.48 describes, now also with `UNITY_EDITOR` defined (three UnityEditor calls in other files stood in for): the whole game assembly 0 errors; no error in `NetDevR7`, `NetDevCombat`, `NetDevMeleeNet` or `NetDevMeleeHealthTest`.
**Not changed:** at the bottom of this screen the item shortcut bar still covers the "Esc - give up" line (the safe's keypad hides the bar, this screen does not: `Backpack.DrawHud` could ask `GunSafeHackPanel.Running` as it asks `SafePanel.Instance.Showing`). Originals: `DevTools\QaBackups\before_gunsafe_esc_2026-10-22\`.

### 2.50 The F1 test menu's safe list shows what each safe really holds (the owner, 2026-10-22: "you can add it back if it works this time") - EDITED; COMPILED OUTSIDE UNITY (0 errors), NEVER RUN IN UNITY

**Why it was taken out before.** The old words after each safe in the list said "a pistol" (or "cash") for every safe whatever it held. That was wrong - on floors 3 and up a safe holds a gun OR a piece of armour OR nothing - so the owner had it removed.
**Now.** `SafeRules.ContentsText(runSeed, floor, index)` (new, pure, in `Outpost/SafeRules.cs`) builds the words from the SAME rules the floor is built from (`WeaponContents`, `HasSideCash`, `HasSideClip`, `HasSideWatch`): "cash" on floors 1 and 2; else the gun by name ("Pew pistol", "SMG", "Rifle", "Shotgun"), "Light / Medium / Heavy Armour" or "no gun or armour", then " + cash", " + spare clip", " + gold watch" for what lies beside it. The F1 list (`TestMode.OutpostTools`) adds " - holds <words>" to each "F4 safe 2/3: room ..., code ..." row. The row for a safe that is on show ("This floor's safe N") used to say "N pistol(s)" for anything in the safe's gun list (armour included) and now says what the rules say plus what is really in it (`SafeFilling`: gun/armour, cash bundles, clips, watches), and warns "the gun/armour the rules call for was NOT made" when the rules call for one and the safe has none (`FloorSafe.FillWithGuns` silently skips a gun whose model is missing - that line would show it).
**Checked outside Unity.** Real `Assembly-CSharp.dll` built from the project's sources, run under .NET 8: 111,000 safe rows (floors 1-12, 3,000 seeds each, every safe) - the words agree with `WeaponContents` / the side-item rules in every row (0 failures); a deliberately wrong armour label failed ~34,000 rows, so the check does catch mistakes. Whole game assembly compiled in the player and the UNITY_EDITOR profile (0 errors); the new check in `NetDevGunsTest.cs` compiles (an injected misspelled member was caught at that line; the other ~160 errors of the editor-tests compile are Unity editor classes the harness has no stand-in for, none in a file touched here).
**Test** (edited, NOT run in Unity): `GunsTest` in `NetDevGunsTest.cs` has one new check, "the test menu names each safe for what it really holds" (60 runs x floors 1-12; the words match the rules and are not the same for every safe).
**Seen while checking (rules as they are, NOT changed):** a safe that holds no gun or armour can still hold a spare clip (e.g. seed 7, F4 safe 2: "no gun or armour + spare clip + gold watch"), since `HasSideClip` does not ask whether the safe holds a gun. Over 3,000 seeds per floor: floor 3 half Light Armour / half empty; floors 4+ about 28 % a gun, 31 % armour, 41 % empty. Originals: `DevTools\QaBackups\before_testmenu_safe_contents_2026-10-22\` (TestMode, SafeRules, NetDevGunsTest, this file).

### 2.51 The loot scanner: a little more radius, and scanned loot easier to see (the owner, 2026-10-22: "increase the radius of the scanner by just a little bit ... I would also like the loot be more visible if it gets scanned ... not as big as the screenshot but similar") - EDITED; COMPILED OUTSIDE UNITY (0 errors), NEVER RUN IN UNITY

**Radius.** The scan only ever marked what lay in the room the player stood in (his rule of 2026-10-17), and the pulse already covered the whole room (it reaches the farthest corner, 28 m/s, 30 m at most), so "a bigger radius" could only mean a little way past the walls. `LootScanRules.ExtraReach = 3f` (new) and `LootScanRules.InScan(sameRoom, distance)`: a thing in ANOTHER room (or in none) is marked as well when it lies within 3 m of the player; the room rule is otherwise as before. `LootScanner.StartPulse` uses it for pickups, the shut floor safes and the gun safe; the pulse is kept going at least ExtraReach + 0.6 m so a closet is reached; the top-of-screen count ("N valuables here") counts what is marked. **One number to change:** `ExtraReach = 0f` gives the old room-only rule back; the glow wave on the floor is still held inside the room's walls.
**Visibility** (`LootScanner.OnGUI`, drawn like the owner's screenshot but smaller): the label is two boxes, the NAME on top (18 pt, white) and "Value: $x" (15 pt, in the glow colour, with "(x2 carry)" for a piece that takes two) under it, both tinted by the glow colour; one steady ring (46 px at 1080p) round the diamond and two rings that swell out of it to 118 px and fade; the glow blob 74 -> 92 px; the diamond 14/17/20 -> 17/20/24; the distance 12 -> 13 pt; the off-screen arrows 12 -> 14. Things with no value (safes, weapons) show the name box only. Sizes scale with the screen like the rest of the HUD (`HudTheme.S`). A mock-up picture was drawn outside the game for the owner (not a screenshot). **Not changed:** how long a mark stays (6 s), the colours, the key (Z), the pulse speed.
**Checked outside Unity.** Whole game assembly compiled in the player and UNITY_EDITOR profiles (0 errors); the new pure rule run against the real assembly (7 checks incl. ExtraReach, edges, SameRoom unchanged); an injected misspelling in `LootScanner.cs` and in the scenario was caught. Editor tests compile bar two known stand-in gaps (`EditorWindow.GetWindow`, lines 57 and 178 of NetDevEtr.cs, old).
**Tests edited, NOT run in Unity:** `NetDevEtrTest.ScanRulesTest` has a new pure check for `ExtraReach` / `InScan`; the Play-mode scan scenario in `NetDevEtr.cs` now expects "the room + what lies within ExtraReach m of the player" instead of "not one in any other room, however near" (helpers `nearBy`, `isValuable`, `safesNear`, `marksWanted`; the status-line count, the walk into room B and the hallway check changed the same way). The pure test "only the room the player is in is scanned" still holds: `SameRoom` itself is unchanged. Originals: `DevTools\QaBackups\before_scanner_radius_marks_2026-10-22\` (LootScanner, LootScanRules, NetDevEtr, NetDevEtrTest, this file).
